# ARCHITECTURE.md

## 1. Current Repository Structure

As of Phase 0 assessment (2026-08-23), the repository contains only:

```
ServerSleuth/
├── skill.md      # Product & architecture specification (source of truth)
└── CLAUDE.md     # Guidance for Claude Code instances working in this repo
```

There is no `.sln`, no `.csproj`, no test project, no CI/CD config, no `README.md`, no scripts, and no `.git` repository initialized yet. `dotnet --list-sdks` reports SDK `10.0.400` available on the development machine, which can target `net8.0` (LTS) as required by `skill.md` §3.1.

**Conclusion:** There is no existing architecture to reconcile against. Per the Phase 0 decision rule in the operating instructions ("如果 repository 是空的，再按照 skill.md 建議的架構建立"), this document adopts `skill.md`'s proposed architecture directly, with only the adaptations noted in §2 below (all additive, none contradicting the spec).

## 2. Proposed ServerSleuth Architecture

Adopting `skill.md` §4 verbatim as the project layout, with two concrete decisions the spec left open:

- **Target framework**: `net8.0` for every project (LTS, matches §3.1). Language version: latest C# available on net8.0 (C# 12), `Nullable` enabled, `ImplicitUsings` enabled.
- **Solution file**: a single `ServerSleuth.sln` at repo root referencing all projects, so `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` work from the root without per-project invocation.

```
ServerSleuth.sln
src/
├── ServerSleuth.Core/                # Domain models, interfaces, enums, evidence, graph — platform-agnostic
│   ├── Models/
│   ├── Interfaces/
│   ├── Enums/
│   ├── Evidence/
│   ├── Graph/
│   └── Services/
│
├── ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/      # Cross-platform technical plumbing (process exec, file IO, networking)
│   ├── Common/
│   ├── FileSystem/
│   ├── Networking/
│   └── Process/
│
├── ServerSleuth.Windows/             # Windows-only scanners (built conditionally / guarded at runtime)
│   ├── Services/
│   ├── IIS/
│   ├── Registry/
│   ├── COM/
│   ├── Software/
│   ├── ScheduledTasks/
│   ├── Certificates/
│   └── Runtimes/
│
├── ServerSleuth.Linux/               # Linux-only scanners
│   ├── Systemd/
│   ├── Packages/
│   ├── Cron/
│   ├── Docker/
│   ├── Runtimes/
│   └── Services/
│
├── ServerSleuth.Analysis/            # Correlation, Dependency, Risk, Migration — consumes Core models only
│   ├── Dependency/
│   ├── Risk/
│   ├── Migration/
│   └── Correlation/
│
├── ServerSleuth.Reporting/           # Json, Csv, Html output — consumes Core + Analysis output only
│   ├── Json/
│   ├── Csv/
│   └── Html/
│
└── ServerSleuth.Cli/                 # Composition root: DI wiring, scanner registry, command handling

test/
├── ServerSleuth.Core.Tests/
├── ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests/
├── ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests/
├── ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests/
├── ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/
├── ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests/
└── ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests/
```

Naming note: the spec's illustrative namespace prefix is `ServerDiscovery.*`; this repository's product name is **ServerSleuth**, so all namespaces/projects use the `ServerSleuth.*` prefix instead. No other deviation from the spec's structure.

## 3. Project Responsibilities

| Project | Responsibility | Must NOT contain |
|---|---|---|
| `ServerSleuth.Core` | Domain entities, `IDiscoveryScanner`, `Evidence`, `Confidence`, `DiscoveryResult`/`DiscoveryError`, graph model, scanner status enums | Any OS-specific API call, any `System.Runtime.InteropServices` Windows/Linux-only usage |
| `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` | `IProcessRunner`, `IFileSystem`, `IPortInspector`-style abstractions shared by both platforms; timeout/cancellation helpers | Business/domain logic, risk rules |
| `ServerSleuth.Windows` | Concrete scanners: `WindowsServiceScanner`, `IISScanner`, `COMScanner`, `WindowsRegistryScanner`, installed-software, scheduled tasks, certificates, runtimes | Correlation logic, risk scoring |
| `ServerSleuth.Linux` | Concrete scanners: `SystemdScanner`, `PackageScanner`, `CronScanner`, `DockerScanner`, Linux process/runtime scanners | Correlation logic, risk scoring |
| `ServerSleuth.Analysis` | Correlation engine, dependency graph builder, risk rules, migration readiness scoring, checklist generation | Any scanning/discovery I/O, any reporting/formatting |
| `ServerSleuth.Reporting` | Serialize final model to JSON/CSV/HTML | Any analysis or correlation logic — it renders what Analysis produced |
| `ServerSleuth.Cli` | Argument parsing, DI composition, scanner registry wiring, profile selection, orchestration of Discovery → Correlation → Analysis → Reporting pipeline | Scanner implementation details, analysis rules |

## 4. Core Domain Model

All entities derive from a common `DiscoveryEntity` base (per `skill.md` §5) carrying: `Id`, `Name`, `Type`, `Version`, `Status`, `Architecture`, `Path`, `Publisher`, `Description`, `Source`, `Confidence`, `Evidence`, `Tags`, `Metadata`. Concrete entities (`Server`, `OperatingSystem`, `Service`, `Process`, `Application`, `WebSite`, `ApplicationPool`, `Port`, `Software`, `Package`, `Runtime`, `Sdk`, `Dll`, `ComComponent`, `ScheduledTask`, `Container`, `Database`, `Certificate`, `Configuration`, `EnvironmentVariable`, `File`, `ExternalDependency`) extend this base and add only fields relevant to that entity — no field is forced onto entities where it doesn't apply (§5).

`Status` is a fixed enum: `Installed | Configured | Running | Listening | Referenced | Used | Unknown` — never collapsed or inferred without evidence (§2, §5).

## 5. Discovery Architecture

Pipeline (mirrors `skill.md` §33 and the operating instructions §5):

```
Discovery → Evidence → Normalization → Correlation → Dependency Graph → Risk Analysis → Migration Assessment → Reporting
```

Each stage only consumes the previous stage's output model — no stage reaches back into raw scanner internals. `DiscoveryContext` (passed into every `ScanAsync`) carries the active `ScanProfile`, `CancellationToken`, and any scan-root configuration; it does not carry mutable global state.

## 6. Scanner Architecture

```csharp
public interface IDiscoveryScanner
{
    string Id { get; }
    PlatformSupport PlatformSupport { get; }
    Task<DiscoveryResult> ScanAsync(DiscoveryContext context, CancellationToken cancellationToken);
}
```

Rules:
- A scanner's only job is finding raw facts and attaching Evidence — never correlating across scanners, never scoring risk.
- A scanner reports `ScannerStatus` (`Supported | PartiallySupported | AccessDenied | NotApplicable | NotInstalled | Failed`) rather than throwing on expected failure modes (missing command, denied permission).
- Registered through a `ScannerRegistry` (Core interface, populated at Cli composition time) so `list-scanners` can enumerate platform/profile support without invoking scanners.

## 7. Evidence Architecture

`Evidence` is a value object: `{ Type, Location/Detail, CapturedAt }`, attached as a collection on every `DiscoveryEntity`. Evidence types enumerated in `skill.md` §6 (Registry, FileSystem, Process, Command, ServiceConfiguration, IISConfiguration, SystemdConfiguration, PackageManager, NetworkSocket, EnvironmentVariable, ConfigurationFile, DockerInspect, ScheduledTask, CertificateStore, PE metadata, ELF metadata). Evidence is never optional for a non-Unknown-status entity — an entity with no evidence cannot claim a status stronger than `Unknown`.

## 8. Correlation Architecture

Lives entirely in `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Correlation`, operating only on normalized `DiscoveryEntity` collections plus their evidence — never on raw scanner-specific types. Correlation rules are explicit, named, unit-testable functions (e.g. `ProcessToServiceCorrelationRule`), not inline heuristics inside scanners (§33, operating instructions §5, §11). Correlation is also where deduplication happens (§32): entities discovered redundantly by multiple scanners merge into one logical entity, unioning their evidence lists.

## 9. Dependency Graph Architecture

`DependencyGraph` (Core.Graph) is a simple directed graph: `Nodes` (typed per §21: Server, Application, Service, Process, Website, AppPool, Runtime, SDK, Software, DLL, COM, Port, Database, Container, Certificate, Configuration, ExternalDependency) and `Edges` (typed per §21: HOSTS, RUNS, BINDS, USES, DEPENDS_ON, LOADS, LISTENS_ON, HOSTED_BY, CONFIGURED_BY, REFERENCES, CALLS, CONNECTS_TO). Every edge carries `Evidence` and `Confidence` — an edge without evidence is a bug, not an acceptable placeholder (§40, §43 in this thread's instructions).

## 10. Analysis Architecture

`ServerSleuth.Analysis` is split into four independent sub-concerns that each consume the dependency graph + entity set and produce their own output, never each other's internals:
- **Dependency**: builds/validates the `DependencyGraph`.
- **Correlation**: builds cross-entity relationships (feeds Dependency).
- **Risk**: applies configurable rules (Critical/High/Medium/Low per §23) against the graph — rules are data/config-driven, not hard-coded branches per scanner.
- **Migration**: produces the readiness score (§41, explainable — every point deducted/added must be traceable to a factor) and the checklist (§40, every item cites the evidence that generated it).

## 11. Reporting Architecture

`ServerSleuth.Reporting` has one serializer per format (Json/Csv/Html), all consuming the same final assembled model (entities + graph + risks + checklist + errors). JSON is canonical and versioned (`schemaVersion`, §30); CSV and HTML are projections of the same data, not independently computed.

## 12. CLI Architecture

`ServerSleuth.Cli` is the composition root: wires DI, builds the `ScannerRegistry`, resolves the requested `ScanProfile`, runs Discovery → Correlation → Analysis → Reporting in order, and writes the output directory (§46). Commands per §28: `scan [--profile] [--output] [--format]`, `version`, `doctor`, `list-scanners`.

## 13. Windows/Linux Platform Isolation

`ServerSleuth.Core` and `ServerSleuth.Analysis` must compile and have their tests run on any OS — no `#if WINDOWS`/`#if LINUX` inside them, no P/Invoke, no OS-specific NuGet packages. All OS-specific code lives only in `ServerSleuth.Windows` / `ServerSleuth.Linux`, selected at runtime by `PlatformSupport` + `RuntimeInformation.IsOSPlatform` checks in the Cli composition root — not by conditional compilation that would produce different binaries per OS. This keeps `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` for Core/Infrastructure/Analysis/Reporting runnable on the Windows dev machine here without a Linux target.

## 14. Security Model

Enforced at the `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` and `ServerSleuth.Reporting` boundary, not left to each scanner's discretion:
- A shared `SecretRedactor` (Infrastructure.Common) recognizes the patterns in `skill.md` §24/§904-943 (`Password=`, `Pwd=`, `ConnectionString=`, `API_KEY=`, `TOKEN=`, `SECRET=`, `PRIVATE_KEY=`, etc.) and every scanner that reads config/env text runs values through it before attaching them as Evidence — raw secret values must never enter a `DiscoveryEntity` or `Evidence` object in the first place, since Reporting only re-serializes what it's given.
- No scanner executes discovered binaries; no scanner connects to a discovered database/service using discovered credentials; no scanner performs any mutating OS call (documented exhaustively in `skill.md` §34, §51 non-negotiables — see also `CLAUDE.md`).

## 15. Error Handling

`DiscoveryResult` carries both successful entities and a `DiscoveryError[]` (with the originating scanner Id, message, and whether it was a permission failure). The Cli orchestrator never lets one scanner's exception abort the run — each `ScanAsync` call is wrapped, and an unexpected exception is converted to a `Failed` `ScannerStatus` + recorded error, with the scan continuing (§26, §969-983).

## 16. Testing Architecture

- Unit tests per project (`test/ServerSleuth.<X>.Tests`), xUnit (see IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md Phase 1 for the concrete choice and rationale).
- Windows/Linux scanners are tested against **fixtures** (recorded registry exports, `systemctl` output samples, IIS config snippets, package manager output) — never against a live production server (§31, §1156-1163).
- Core/Analysis tests run cross-platform since those projects have no OS dependency.
- Required negative-path coverage per entity/scanner: missing component, permission denied, invalid configuration, command unavailable, unexpected output, architecture mismatch, duplicate discovery, conflicting evidence, scanner failure isolation (§31 items 1-10).

## 17. Extensibility Strategy

New scanners (e.g. future `RedisScanner`, `NginxScanner`) implement `IDiscoveryScanner` and register in the `ScannerRegistry` — no change to `Core` required. New report formats implement a `IReportWriter`-style interface in `Reporting` without touching Analysis. New risk rules are added as data/config in `Analysis.Risk` without touching scanners (§38-39).

## Phase 2 Addendum — Common Infrastructure (as built)

Phase 1's models/interfaces required no changes for Phase 2 — `IDiscoveryScanner`, `DiscoveryContext`, and `DiscoveryResult`/`DiscoveryError` all sit unchanged above the new `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` layer. `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` references `ServerSleuth.Core` only; `ServerSleuth.Core` still has zero project references, preserving the dependency direction in §9/§13.

Implemented, under `src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/`:

- **`Common/OperationStatus.cs`** — a primitive-operation outcome enum (`Success | AccessDenied | NotFound | Timeout | Cancelled | InvalidInput | IoError | ExecutionFailed | StartFailed | Unsupported`), distinct from Core's scanner-level `ScannerStatus`. Every Infrastructure abstraction reports through this instead of throwing for expected failure modes.
- **`Common/ScannerLogEvents.cs`** — fixed `EventId` constants for the structured log events named in skill.md §37 (`ScannerStarted/Completed/Failed/Skipped`, `PermissionDenied`, `DiscoveryCount`), so scanners in later phases log consistently rather than inventing event names.
- **`Process/`** — `IProcessRunner` + `ProcessRunner`, `ProcessRequest`, `ProcessResult`. Launches via `Process.StartInfo.ArgumentList` (never a concatenated shell command string), captures stdout/stderr/exit code/duration, enforces a per-request `Timeout`, honors `CancellationToken`, and kills the full process tree (`Process.Kill(entireProcessTree: true)`) on both timeout and cancellation to avoid orphaned children.
- **`FileSystem/`** — `IFileSystemReader` + `FileSystemReader`, `FileEntryInfo`, `FileSystemResult<T>`. Wraps `File`/`Directory` calls, classifying `UnauthorizedAccessException`→`AccessDenied`, `FileNotFoundException`/`DirectoryNotFoundException`→`NotFound`, `IOException`→`IoError` — any other exception type is left to propagate as a genuine bug rather than being silently absorbed.
- **`Networking/`** — `IPortInspector` + `NetworkEndpoint` DTO only. No Windows/Linux socket enumeration yet by design (Phase 3 / Phase 6); a fake implementation in the test project exercises the contract shape today.
- **`Security/`** — `ISecretRedactor` + `SecretRedactor`, `SecretRedactionRule`, `SecretSeverity`. Rule-based and extensible: each rule is a named `Regex` + severity, tested independently. Default rules cover `Password/Pwd/UserPassword`, `ConnectionString`, `API_KEY`, `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `PRIVATE_KEY` (all key=value/key:value shaped, anchored so a bare word inside a longer identifier like `TokenType` or `PasswordPolicy` does not match), plus freestanding-secret rules for PEM private key blocks, JWTs, and `Bearer` tokens.
- **`DependencyInjection/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`** — `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` registers `IProcessRunner`, `IFileSystemReader`, `ISecretRedactor` as singletons. `IPortInspector` is intentionally not registered — no concrete type exists to bind it to until Phase 3/6.

No telemetry, external HTTP calls, or network dependency were introduced anywhere in this layer, per skill.md §17/§35 and operating instructions §17.

## Phase 3 Addendum — Windows Foundation (as built)

`ServerSleuth.Windows` targets **`net8.0-windows`** (not plain `net8.0`) — this was the one deliberate deviation from the Phase 0 "every project targets net8.0" default, made because it lets the project call `Microsoft.Win32.Registry`, `System.ServiceProcess.ServiceController`, and `System.Management` (WMI) directly without `[SupportedOSPlatform]` suppression noise, while `Core`/`Infrastructure`/`Analysis`/`Reporting` remain plain `net8.0` and still build on any OS per §13. `ServerSleuth.Windows` references `Core` + `Infrastructure` only; `Core` still has zero references.

Five scanners implemented, each an `IDiscoveryScanner`, each with an `internal static` pure-mapping method (`BuildEntity`/`BuildEntities`) kept separate from the OS-calling `ScanAsync`, so every mapping is unit-testable without touching the real machine (an `InternalsVisibleTo` was added to `ServerSleuth.Windows.csproj` for `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests` to reach those):

- **`windows-os-scanner`** (`OperatingSystem/WindowsOsScanner.cs`) — `Server` + `OperatingSystem` entities from `Environment`/`RuntimeInformation` (evidence: Windows Environment) augmented by `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion` (evidence: Windows Registry) when readable; falls back to `RuntimeInformation.OSDescription` at lower confidence when the registry read fails, rather than failing outright.
- **`windows-process-scanner`** (`Process/WindowsProcessScanner.cs`) — enumerates via `System.Diagnostics.Process.GetProcesses()` (`ProcessEnumerator`), augments with `Win32_Process` WMI rows (`ProcessWmiProvider`, including a `GetOwner()` call per process for the account) for executable path/command line/parent PID/owner. A process whose privileged fields can't be read is never dropped — it's kept with those fields left `null` (never an empty string) and a `*Status = AccessDenied|Unavailable` metadata entry, and counted toward an overall `PartiallySupported` result rather than failing the scan.
- **`windows-port-scanner`** (`Networking/WindowsPortScanner.cs`, backed by `WindowsPortInspector` implementing Phase 2's `IPortInspector`) — sourced from the `MSFT_NetTCPConnection`/`MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint` CIM classes (`root\StandardCimv2`), **not** `netstat` text parsing, because they expose the owning PID directly (`NetworkTableProvider`). Process name is resolved via `Process.GetProcessById` (`ProcessNameResolver`). The scanner's only correlation is what `Port.OwningPid` already carries — no graph edges are built here; that's Phase 5.
- **`windows-service-scanner`** (`Services/WindowsServiceScanner.cs`) — enumerates Win32 services via `ServiceController.GetServices()` (`ServiceEnumerator`, which only returns Win32 services, not drivers, matching skill.md §7's scope), then reads `HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\<name>` (`ServiceRegistryDetailReader`) for `ImagePath`, `ObjectName`, `Start`/`DelayedAutostart`, `DependOnService`, and the `Parameters\ServiceDll` value — i.e. never just `Get-Service`. A service running under a non-built-in account is tagged `MigrationRelevant` (built-in accounts: `LocalSystem`, `NT AUTHORITY\LocalService`, `NT AUTHORITY\NetworkService`, `NT AUTHORITY\SYSTEM`); the service's account **name** is captured, never a password (Windows never exposes one via the registry — LSA secrets are a separate, unread store).
- **`windows-installed-software-scanner`** (`Software/WindowsInstalledSoftwareScanner.cs`) — reads all three Uninstall registry roots from skill.md §10 (`RegistryView.Registry64`, `RegistryView.Registry32` for the WOW6432Node view, and `HKCU`). Skips entries with no `DisplayName` or `SystemComponent=1` (patches, not software). **Deliberately does not merge across sources** — see the decisions log below.

**Phase 1 touch-up**: `DiscoveryEntity.Version` changed from `{ get; init; }` to `{ get; set; }` (`src/ServerSleuth.Core/Models/DiscoveryEntity.cs`) — OS version is only known after a registry lookup that happens after the entity is constructed, the same situation `Status`/`Confidence` already had a mutable setter for. Core's 31 tests were re-run and still pass; no other Phase 1 shape changed.

**Registry access** goes through a new `ServerSleuth.Windows.Registry.IWindowsRegistryReader` (mirroring Infrastructure's `IFileSystemReader` pattern) rather than raw `Microsoft.Win32.Registry` calls scattered through scanners — classifies `UnauthorizedAccessException`/`SecurityException` → `AccessDenied`, missing key → `NotFound`, consistent with the Infrastructure error-model pattern from Phase 2.

A minimal `ServerSleuth.Windows.DependencyInjection.AddServerSleuthWindows()` registers all Windows abstractions and all five scanners against `IDiscoveryScanner` (resolvable as `IEnumerable<IDiscoveryScanner>`) — deliberately not a full scanner-registry/plugin system; real orchestration is Phase 9's job.

Full per-scanner detail (data sources, permissions, evidence, limitations, security, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 4A Addendum — IIS Discovery (as built)

Added `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/IIS/` — no new project, extends `ServerSleuth.Windows`.

- **`IIisConfigurationProvider` / `IisConfigurationProvider`** — the "IIS Provider → Mapper → Domain Entity" split instruction §20 asked for. The provider loads `Microsoft.Web.Administration.dll` **at runtime via `Assembly.LoadFrom` + `dynamic`**, not a compile-time project reference — see the decisions log below for why. It returns plain DTOs (`IisSiteRow`, `IisBindingRow`, `IisApplicationRow`, `IisAppPoolRow`, wrapped in `IisSnapshot`/`IisProbeResult`) so `Microsoft.Web.Administration` types never leak past this one file — `IisScanner.BuildEntities` (the Mapper) and its tests depend only on these DTOs, matching Phase 3's established `internal static` pure-mapping pattern.
- **`IisScanner`** (`windows-iis-scanner`) — produces `WebSite`, `Application` (reused — see below), and `ApplicationPool` entities. `IisAvailability.NotInstalled`/`AccessDenied`/`Failed` map directly to the matching `ScannerStatus` values; a successful probe with per-site/per-pool partial failures maps to `PartiallySupported` with those failures surfaced as `DiscoveryError`s alongside the entities that *did* read successfully — nothing is dropped silently.
- **Entity reuse decision**: an IIS "Application" (virtual path + physical path + pool) uses the *existing* `Core.Models.Application` entity — the same one Phase 7 (§20) will later populate via cross-scanner grouping — rather than inventing an `IisApplication` type. `skill.md` §5's entity list only has one `Application` node, and §21's graph node list is the same; this scanner just populates it from a different, IIS-specific source. `Application.ComponentEntityIds` carries `[siteId, appPoolId]`, giving the low-level `Site HOSTS Application` / `Application USES ApplicationPool` relationship data §13 asked for — no `DependencyEdge`/`DependencyGraph` object is built here; that's still Phase 5.
- **Bindings**: `WebSite.Bindings` (already a `string[]` in the Phase 1 model) holds one formatted line per binding (`"https://0.0.0.0:443 (host: erp.company.com)"`); the model's singular `Protocol`/`HostName`/`Port`/`CertificateThumbprint` fields are populated from the *first* binding as a "primary binding" convenience — this is a pre-existing Phase 1 model shape (one binding's worth of singular fields alongside a list of all of them), not a new gap introduced here. Full binding detail (including certificate store name) for every binding is preserved as per-index `Metadata` (`Binding0.CertificateThumbprint`, `Binding0.CertificateStore`, ...) so nothing beyond the "primary" binding is lost.
- **Physical path validation**: `IisScanner` takes Phase 2's `IFileSystemReader` and calls `GetFileInfo` on every site/application physical path, turning a missing/denied path into `PhysicalPathStatus = NotFound|AccessDenied` metadata (never a silently-empty string) — reusing existing Infrastructure rather than adding a second path-checking mechanism.
- **Secrets**: no connection strings, passwords, or `applicationHost.config` contents are ever read wholesale — the provider only reads the specific typed properties (Name, State, PhysicalPath, Bindings, pool identity, etc.) needed for these three entity types. Certificate handling is thumbprint + store name only, never the private key.

Full detail (data sources, permissions, entities, evidence, security, known limitations, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 4B Addendum — COM/ActiveX Discovery (as built)

Added `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/COM/` — no new project, extends `ServerSleuth.Windows`.

```
Windows Registry (CLSID keys)
        ↓
IWindowsRegistryReader (existing, Phase 3 — reused as-is, no changes)
        ↓
ComClsidReader (Provider→Mapper split: reads only present child keys — InprocServer32,
        LocalServer32, ProgID, TypeLib, Version — never an unbounded traversal)
        ↓
WindowsComScanner.BuildEntity (pure mapper)
        ↓
Core.Models.ComComponent (existing Phase 1 entity — reused as-is, no changes)
        ↓
EvidenceRecord { Type = Registry }
```

- **Registry views, not manual WOW6432Node traversal**: the same `RegistryView.Registry64`/`Registry32`/`Default` convention `WindowsInstalledSoftwareScanner` established in Phase 3 is reused verbatim for `ComRegistrationSource` — the OS registry redirector handles the 32-bit mapping, so there is exactly one traversal per view, never a double-count from combining `Registry64` with a manual `\WOW6432Node\` path.
- **Registration ≠ Usage**: every discovered `ComComponent` gets `Status = EntityStatus.Installed` and `Metadata["RegistrationStatus"] = "Registered"` — never `Used`. `ComComponent`'s own Phase 1 doc comment already stated this distinction; Phase 4B is the first scanner to actually populate it. An `Application → USES → ComComponent` edge is explicitly **not** built here — that requires evidence of actual instantiation (e.g. a process with the DLL loaded), which is Phase 5 Correlation's job, not registry discovery's.
- **No aggressive merge across registry views/scopes**: the same CLSID registered under both `Registry64` and `Registry32` (or `HKLM` and `HKCU`) produces **two distinct `ComComponent` entities** — `Id` is `com:{scope}:{view}:{CLSID}`, so `Registry64`+`Registry32` registrations of the same CLSID never collide or silently overwrite each other. `Clsid` (canonicalized to uppercase) is preserved identically on both entities so Phase 5's correlation engine can find and merge them later using exactly the identity fields (CLSID + scope + view) skill.md §20 calls for.
- **Path/argument parsing never guesses**: `ServerReference.Parse` handles quoted paths (with or without trailing arguments) unambiguously, and a bare, unquoted path with no space is unambiguous — but an *unquoted* value containing a space is inherently ambiguous (could be a legitimate unquoted path-with-spaces, or a path followed by arguments) and is reported as `RawReferenceDetected = true` with the original raw value preserved, rather than guessing where the path ends. This directly implements skill.md §16's explicit instruction not to `Trim('"')`-and-assume.
- **File verification, not DLL deep analysis**: for a resolved (non-ambiguous) server path, the scanner checks existence/size/last-write-time via Phase 2's `IFileSystemReader`, and — only for a file that exists — reads `FileVersion`/`ProductVersion`/`CompanyName`/`ProductName` via .NET's `FileVersionInfo.GetVersionInfo` (a standard, non-executing Win32 VERSION-resource read, wrapped in a new `IFileVersionMetadataReader`). **PE Machine-type (architecture) parsing was deliberately not implemented** — skill.md §2 explicitly excludes "DLL deep analysis" from Phase 4B's scope, and custom byte-level PE header parsing falls on that side of the line, whereas `FileVersionInfo` is a well-established safe/static metadata API already conceptually inside §17's "File Verification" list. Every `ComComponent`'s `Architecture` therefore stays `Unknown` in this phase — a documented, deliberate boundary, not an oversight.
- **Secrets**: `LocalServer32`'s parsed `Arguments` (the one field with any realistic chance of containing something secret-shaped, e.g. a `--ApiKey=...` flag) and any raw ambiguous value stored as metadata are passed through the existing Phase 2 `ISecretRedactor` before being set — never a second redaction implementation. A detected secret additionally sets `Metadata["SecretDetected"] = "true"`.
- **Performance, measured, not assumed**: this dev machine has ~14,189 real CLSID registrations (8031 `Registry64` + 6143 `Registry32` + 15 `HKCU`, confirmed independently via `Get-ChildItem` before the scanner was written). The real-machine integration test measured a full scan at **~5.6 seconds** — the "only read child keys that are actually present" design (checking `GetSubKeyNames` once per CLSID before ever querying `InprocServer32`/`LocalServer32`/`ProgID`/`TypeLib`/`Version`) keeps this well within a generous 120-second bound asserted by the test itself, not just noted in a comment.

Full detail (data sources, permissions, entities, evidence, security, known limitations, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 4C Addendum — Scheduled Tasks + Certificates (as built)

Added `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/ScheduledTasks/` and `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Certificates/` — no new projects.

**Scheduled Tasks**: `windows-scheduled-task-scanner` reads the native Task Scheduler 2.0 COM API via `Type.GetTypeFromProgID("Schedule.Service")` + `dynamic` — the same late-binding pattern as the Phase 4A IIS provider (`Microsoft.Web.Administration`), for the identical reason: no compile-time COM dependency, so the project builds anywhere, and Task Scheduler (unlike IIS) is *always* present on Windows so there's no `NotInstalled` branch — only `AccessDenied`/`Failed`/`Supported`/`PartiallySupported`. Recursively enumerates every task folder from `\` with `GetTasks(1)` (flag 1 = include hidden tasks — migration assessment needs to see hidden tasks, not just visible ones). Only Connect/GetFolder/GetTasks/GetFolders/property getters are ever called — never `RegisterTask`/`Run`/`Stop`/`Delete` or any property setter.

- **Multiple actions/triggers on one Core entity**: `Core.Models.ScheduledTask` (Phase 1) has singular `Action`/`Trigger` string fields. Rather than changing Core, the scanner follows the exact convention `WindowsPortScanner`'s binding metadata and `WindowsServiceScanner`'s dependency list already established: the singular fields hold the *primary* action/trigger (first `Execute` action; first trigger), and every action/trigger is fully represented via indexed `Metadata` (`Action0.Path`/`Action0.Arguments`/`Action0.WorkingDirectory`/`Action0.ScriptPath`, `Trigger0.Type`/`Trigger0.Enabled`/`Trigger0.StartBoundary`/`Trigger0.EndBoundary`, ...).
- **Script path extraction** (`ScriptPathExtractor`): a small, separately-testable pure helper that recognizes known script hosts (`powershell.exe`/`pwsh.exe`/`cscript.exe`/`wscript.exe`/`cmd.exe`) and extracts a referenced `.ps1`/`.vbs`/`.js`/`.bat`/`.cmd` path from the action's arguments via regex — best-effort, never a full command-line parser, and never executes or reads the script's contents (only an existence check via `IFileSystemReader`, same `PathStatus` convention as every other scanner).
- **Disabled ≠ removed**: a disabled task is still fully mapped to an entity (`Enabled = false`, `Metadata["State"] = "Disabled"`) — never dropped or treated as "not migration-relevant." skill.md §10 is explicit about this and it's enforced by a dedicated test.
- **Description and action arguments pass through `ISecretRedactor`** before becoming metadata, exactly like the COM scanner's `LocalServer32` arguments — the same reasoning (a task's arguments/description are free text an admin wrote, and could contain something secret-shaped).

**Certificates**: `windows-certificate-scanner` uses .NET's standard `X509Store`/`X509Certificate2` API (`OpenFlags.ReadOnly`) against four targeted stores (`LocalMachine\My`, `LocalMachine\WebHosting`, `LocalMachine\Root`, `CurrentUser\My`) — never every store on the machine. Reuses `Core.Models.Certificate` (Phase 1) unchanged; richer fields (SerialNumber, Store, StoreLocation, HasPrivateKey, SignatureAlgorithm, PublicKeyAlgorithm, KeySizeBits, FriendlyName, CertificateStatus, RiskLevel) live in `Metadata`, following the same established convention.

- **Expiry/risk classification lives outside the scanner**: `CertificateExpiryClassifier` is a standalone, pure, stateless helper (`ClassifyStatus`/`ClassifyRiskLevel`) taking an explicit `asOf` parameter — never `DateTime.Now` internally — so tests are fully deterministic (per skill.md §22) and the risk-band thresholds (§16: >90d Normal, 31–90 Warning, 8–30 High, ≤7/expired Critical) aren't hard-coded inline in `WindowsCertificateScanner`. This is *not* the Migration Risk Engine (Phase 7) — it only labels one certificate's own validity window, with no cross-entity reasoning.
- **Never exposes private key material**: `HasPrivateKey` is a boolean flag read from `X509Certificate2.HasPrivateKey`; the scanner never calls any private-key-accessing member (`GetRSAPrivateKey()`, `Export(X509ContentType.Pfx)`, etc.).
- **Thumbprint normalization for cross-scanner matching** (§19): every `Certificate.Thumbprint` is uppercased and whitespace-stripped. This matters because Phase 4A's IIS scanner already stores a binding's certificate thumbprint (via `Convert.ToHexString`, which is naturally uppercase/no-separator) on `WebSite.CertificateThumbprint`/`Metadata["BindingN.CertificateThumbprint"]` — with both sides normalized identically, a thumbprint join between an IIS binding and a `Certificate` entity works reliably without extra effort. **No active correlation code was written** for this — per the instructions' explicit "lightweight reference is allowed, but don't build the graph yet," the join key being consistently formatted on both sides *is* the lightweight reference; actually walking `WebSite → Certificate` is left for Phase 5.

Full detail (data sources, permissions, entities, evidence, security, known limitations, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 4D Addendum — Runtime/SDK Discovery (as built)

Added `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Runtimes/` and `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Runtimes/Detectors/` — no new project.

```
IRuntimeDetector (one per runtime family — DotNetFramework, DotNetRuntime, DotNetSdk, Java, Python, Node, Php, Go)
        ↓ each produces RuntimeDetectionRow[] independently
RuntimeDiscoveryScanner (orchestrator — isolates one detector's failure from the rest)
        ↓
RuntimeEntityBuilder (one shared generic mapper, not one per family)
        ↓
Core.Models.Runtime / Core.Models.Sdk (existing Phase 1 entities, unchanged)
```

This is the explicit architecture the Phase 4D instructions required (never one scanner with hard-coded `if runtime == X` branches) — a new runtime family is added by writing one more `IRuntimeDetector` and registering it in DI; nothing else changes.

- **Installed vs. Detected vs. ExecutableAvailable** (§3): every entity's `Status` is `EntityStatus.Installed` (never `Used` — matches the same principle already established for COM/Services), while `Metadata["ExecutableAvailable"]` and `Metadata["DetectionSources"]` carry the finer-grained distinction the instructions asked for. A registry-only Java entry with no resolvable `java.exe` is still reported (`ExecutableAvailable = false`), never discarded.
- **SDK vs. Runtime are different entity kinds, never conflated** (§18): `RuntimeEntityKind.Sdk` maps to `Core.Models.Sdk`, `RuntimeEntityKind.Runtime` maps to `Core.Models.Runtime` — `DotNetSdkDetector` only ever produces `Sdk` rows.
- **Every version is its own entity** (§17): `dotnet --list-runtimes`/`--list-sdks` output is split line-by-line, each line its own row (verified: this dev machine reports 6 distinct .NET runtime component/version pairs, never collapsed to "the newest"); Python's `py -0p` listing plus a known-directory scan surface every installed interpreter, not just the one PATH resolves first.
- **Reusable executable resolution, not `where.exe`** (§14): a single `IExecutableLocator` (PATH-splitting + `IFileSystemReader.Exists` checks against known install directories) is shared by every detector that needs to find an executable — no detector shells out to `where.exe`, and none recursively scans `C:\`.
- **Conflicting evidence is preserved, never silently resolved** (§26): `JavaDetector` is the one family with a real registry+command dual-evidence path; when both agree on the same `JavaHome`, they merge into a single row with both `DetectionSources`; when they disagree on version, the row keeps a `ConflictNote` describing both observations rather than picking one silently (verified by a dedicated test).
- **.NET Framework release-to-version mapping uses Microsoft's own documented `>=`-threshold table** (§6), not a guessed heuristic — a `Release` value below the lowest known threshold falls back to the registry's own raw `Version` string plus a `ConflictNote`, rather than being forced into a version bucket.
- **Architecture hint is a cheap path-based heuristic, not PE parsing** (§27): `ArchitectureHint.FromPath` checks for `"Program Files (x86)"` vs `"Program Files"` in the resolved path — the same reasoning as Phase 4B's decision to skip PE Machine-type parsing (it would be "DLL deep analysis," out of scope here too).
- **Command safety** (§22, §28): every detector only ever runs a fixed, hard-coded metadata/version query (`--version`, `-version`, `--list-runtimes`, `--list-sdks`, `-0p`, `version`) via `IProcessRunner` with a single shared `RuntimeDetectionDefaults.CommandTimeout` (10s) — no detector ever executes a script, program, or package-manager install/restore command, and no argument is ever built from untrusted/discovered data.
- **Environment variables are redacted before storage** (§19): `GoDetector` reads `GOROOT`/`GOPATH` through `ISecretRedactor` before they ever reach `Metadata`.
- **Detector isolation**: `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner` catches any exception a detector throws and converts it to a `DiscoveryError` for that detector only — one broken detector never prevents the others' results from being returned, and the overall scanner status is `PartiallySupported` (never `Failed`) when this happens, matching the "no crash, no silent all-or-nothing" principle used throughout.

Full detail (data sources, permissions, entities, evidence, security, known limitations, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 4E-1 Addendum — Configuration Discovery (as built)

Added `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Configuration/` — no new project.

```
IisScanner / WindowsServiceScanner / WindowsScheduledTaskScanner (re-run directly, not duplicated)
        ↓ their entities' physical/executable paths
ScanRootCollector (dedupe by normalized path)
        ↓
ScanRoot[] (never an arbitrary or filesystem-wide path)
        ↓
WindowsConfigurationScanner: EnumerateFiles(*.config/*.json/*.xml/*.ini/*.yaml/*.yml/*.properties, recursive within each root)
        ↓ per file: size check → read (auto encoding detection) → structural validate (JSON/XML) → ConfigurationContentAnalyzer
ConfigurationFileRow
        ↓
Core.Models.Configuration (existing Phase 1 entity, unchanged)
```

- **Scan roots are always derived, never arbitrary** (§4-5): `WindowsConfigurationScanner` takes the *actual* `IisScanner`/`WindowsServiceScanner`/`WindowsScheduledTaskScanner` instances as constructor dependencies and re-runs their `ScanAsync` to get real `WebSite`/`Application`/`Service`/`ScheduledTask` entities, then `ScanRootCollector` extracts `PhysicalPath`/executable-directory from each. This is genuine reuse of already-built scanners (not a duplicate implementation) — the DI registration was changed so `IisScanner`, `WindowsServiceScanner`, and `WindowsScheduledTaskScanner` are registered as their own concrete types (shared singleton) in addition to being exposed via `IDiscoveryScanner`, so both this scanner and the top-level scanner list use the same instances.
- **Structural parsing only for JSON/XML** (§7, §11): `StructuralValidator` uses `System.Text.Json.JsonDocument` and `System.Xml.XmlReader` — both already part of the BCL, no new package. XML parsing is hardened against XXE (`DtdProcessing.Prohibit`, `XmlResolver = null`), verified by a dedicated test using an actual XXE payload targeting a local file. YAML is deliberately **not** structurally parsed — per skill.md §11's explicit instruction to avoid introducing a large dependency (no YamlDotNet) — it and any unrecognized extension go through the same conservative text-based analyzer as everything else.
- **One universal content analyzer for every format** (`ConfigurationContentAnalyzer`): secret detection, external endpoints, UNC paths, environment-variable references, and runtime references are detected by scanning the raw decoded text with regex, regardless of format — a URL or UNC path is the same string shape whether it sits inside a JSON string, an XML attribute, or an INI value, so one implementation covers all 7 formats rather than seven separate extractors.
- **Secret detection reuses `ISecretRedactor` directly** — no second redaction implementation. Building this scanner surfaced two real, pre-existing gaps in `SecretRedactor` (Phase 2), both fixed as part of this phase (not scope creep — they directly blocked Configuration Discovery's stated goal): (1) a quoted JSON-style key (`"password": "x"`) wasn't matched because the closing quote sat between the key and the separator; (2) compound camelCase/snake_case keys common in JSON configs (`clientSecret`, `accessToken`, `refreshToken`) weren't matched because the existing word-boundary rules require the key to stand alone. Both fixes are general improvements to shared Infrastructure, benefiting every future scanner, not just this one.
- **Connection strings matched by shape, not by key adjacency**: the original design looked for a value immediately following a `"connectionString"` key, but real `appsettings.json` nests values under `"ConnectionStrings": { "Default": "..." }` — arbitrarily deep. Fixed (caught by a real-shaped test, not assumed) to match from the first recognizable ADO.NET-style key (`Server=`, `Host=`, etc.) to end-of-line or closing quote, working uniformly across JSON/XML/INI regardless of nesting. `DatabaseReference` never carries `Password`/`Uid`/`User Id` — only `Type`/`Host`/`Port`/`Database` are ever extracted.
- **Large-file and encoding handling reuses Phase 2's `IFileSystemReader`** — `GetFileInfo` checked before ever reading (1 MB cap, `SkippedTooLarge` otherwise), and `.NET`'s `File.ReadAllTextAsync` (used internally by `IFileSystemReader.ReadTextAsync`) already auto-detects UTF-8/UTF-8-BOM/UTF-16LE/UTF-16BE, so no separate encoding-detection code was needed — verified by tests writing real temp files in each encoding.
- **Never dumps raw file content**: `Core.Models.Configuration` and its `Metadata` only ever carry structural facts (format, parse status, size, detected sections/endpoints/database references/UNC paths/env vars/runtime references) — the file's actual text is read into memory transiently for analysis and never stored on the entity.
- **A disabled/degraded per-file/per-root read never aborts the scan**: `AccessDenied`/`NotFound`/`Unreadable`/`SkippedTooLarge`/`PartiallyParsed` are all still-produced entities (never dropped), and a root that doesn't exist (`NotFound` on `EnumerateFiles`) is treated as a non-error (an owner entity's path simply not existing on disk is a normal finding, not a scanner problem) while a genuine `AccessDenied` on a root is recorded as a `DiscoveryError` and degrades the overall status to `PartiallySupported`.

Full detail (data sources, permissions, entities, evidence, security, known limitations, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 4E-2 Addendum — DLL / Native Dependency Discovery (as built)

Added `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Binaries/` — no new project.

```
IisScanner / WindowsServiceScanner / WindowsScheduledTaskScanner / WindowsComScanner (re-run directly)
        ↓ their entities' paths                            ↓ COM InprocServer32/LocalServer32
ScanRootCollector (Phase 4E-1, reused)          ComScanRootCollector (new — same ScanRoot type)
        ↓                                                    ↓
merged ScanRoot[] — minus anything under %windir% (SystemDirectoryExclusion)
        ↓
BoundedDirectoryWalker (depth ≤8, files ≤10,000/root, never follows reparse points)
        ↓ + every COM-referenced file checked directly regardless of walk results
BinaryDiscoveryRow (path, size, PE analysis, version metadata, contributing roots)
        ↓
Core.Models.Dll (existing Phase 1 entity, unchanged) — one entity per unique path, evidence merged across all contributing sources
```

- **PE parsing via `System.Reflection.Metadata`'s `PEReader`**, not a hand-rolled byte-offset parser: an official, first-party, minimal, read-only PE/CLR-metadata reader — exactly what skill.md §33's "prefer BCL/manual minimal parser, avoid a large dependency without justification" calls for. Managed-vs-native is determined via `PEReader.HasMetadata` (whether a CLR header is present) — never by loading or executing the file. Verified against real files: this solution's own compiled `ServerSleuth.Core.dll` (managed, PE32, Machine=I386 — the standard convention for an AnyCPU assembly, not a bug), `kernel32.dll` (native, with a real, non-empty import table), and `notepad.exe` (correctly typed as `Exe`).
- **Import table parsed manually via `PEReader.GetSectionData`**, walking `IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR` entries to collect imported module *names* only — never recursing into the imported DLLs themselves (Phase 5's job). Delay-imports are explicitly marked `Unsupported` rather than attempted — skill.md §13 explicitly permits this fallback, and the primary (non-delay) import table already delivers the requirement's main value.
- **A real, measured performance problem was found and fixed during this phase**: the first real-machine run took **6 minutes 39 seconds** and surfaced 29,461 binaries — because several of the ~14,189 COM registrations from Phase 4B resolve into `%windir%\System32`/`SysWOW64`, and walking those directories in full (each holding thousands of unrelated OS-shipped files) made "bounded per-root" discovery unbounded in aggregate. Fixed by `SystemDirectoryExclusion`: any root under `%windir%` is never walked for *other* files, while the *specific* file a COM registration references is still checked directly (skill.md §16 is unaffected). Re-measured: **8.3 seconds**, 18,125 binaries — a >45x speedup, and the remaining count is genuine application/vendor content, not OS noise.
- **Deduplication merges by normalized absolute path** (skill.md §29): the same file discovered via, say, both an IIS application root's directory walk and a direct COM reference becomes **one** `Dll` entity with evidence from both sources and `ReferencedByEntityIds` containing both owners — verified by a dedicated test using a real file copied into a temp IIS root and separately referenced by a synthetic COM registration.
- **Dangling references are preserved, not discarded** (skill.md §16): a COM registration whose `InprocServer32`/`LocalServer32` points at a file that no longer exists still produces a `Dll` entity (`Metadata["FileStatus"] = "NotFound"`, `Status = Unknown`) — this is exactly the kind of migration-risk evidence a stale/dangling registration represents.
- **Bounded by construction, not by luck**: `BoundedDirectoryWalker` enforces a max depth (8) and max files per root (10,000), never follows a reparse point/junction/symlink (checked via the new `IFileSystemReader.IsReparsePoint`, a small Infrastructure addition), and records `DepthLimitReached`/`FileLimitReached` rather than silently truncating.
- **Secret handling**: imported module names (free text from the PE, in principle attacker-influenceable in a hostile binary, though never executed here) pass through `ISecretRedactor` before becoming metadata, consistent with every other scanner's convention — even though DLL names are not expected to look secret-shaped in practice, the pass-through costs nothing and closes the theoretical gap.

Full detail (data sources, permissions, entities, evidence, security, known limitations, tests) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 5A Addendum — Dependency Correlation Foundation (as built)

New project: `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/` (targets plain `net8.0`, references `ServerSleuth.Core` only — no dependency on `ServerSleuth.Windows`, so it stays usable once Linux discovery exists too).

```
DiscoveryResult[] (from any already-run scanners, Phase 1-4)
        ↓ flatten to entities
CorrelationContext (Identity Resolution)
   — ById, plus Dll-by-normalized-path and Certificate-by-normalized-thumbprint indexes
        ↓
10 x ICorrelationRule.Evaluate(context) → CorrelationCandidate[]
   (each candidate: Source, Target-or-null, Type, Confidence, Evidence, UnresolvedReason)
        ↓
CorrelationEngine validation:
   target unresolved? → rejected (diagnostics)
   source/target missing from input? → rejected
   self-edge? → rejected
   zero evidence? → rejected ("no evidence = no edge", skill.md §12)
        ↓ else
DependencyGraph.AddEdge (Core, Phase 1) — same Source/Target/Type already gets evidence-merged, higher Confidence wins
        ↓
CorrelationResult { Graph, Diagnostics }
```

- **Identity resolution reuses each scanner's existing Id scheme rather than re-deriving identity.** IIS Site→Application→ApplicationPool relationships are read directly off `Application.ComponentEntityIds` (already populated by `IisScanner` in Phase 4A) by looking up each component id's actual runtime type in `CorrelationContext.ById` — never by string-matching an id prefix like `"iis-site:"`, so the rule stays correct even if an id format changes. Configuration ownership is read from `Configuration.Metadata["OwnerEntityId"]`, which `ScanRootCollector` (Phase 4E-1) already assigned unambiguously (one owner per bounded scan root) — Phase 5A does not re-derive ownership from a path-prefix heuristic.
- **`WindowsPathNormalizer`**: the single reusable Windows path normalizer (skill.md §5). Handles quoted paths, trailing separators, `/` vs `\`, case-folding for the comparison key (Windows paths are case-insensitive), and safely-resolvable `%ENVVAR%` references via an injectable `IEnvironmentVariableResolver` (real implementation reads `Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable` — a lookup, not command execution). Deliberately does **not** collapse `Program Files` and `Program Files (x86)`, and does **not** merge distinct UNC hosts (`\\ServerA\Share` vs `\\ServerB\Share`) — verified by dedicated tests for both.
- **`CommandLineReference`**: parses a raw command-line-shaped string (Service.ExecutablePath's registry `ImagePath`, e.g. `"C:\Foo\bar.exe" -k netsvcs`) into an executable path and arguments, using the same never-guess-on-ambiguity algorithm as `ServerSleuth.Windows.COM.ServerReference` (Phase 4B) — reimplemented rather than referenced, because `ServerSleuth.Analysis` must stay platform-agnostic and cannot take a `ServerSleuth.Windows` dependency. An unquoted value containing a space is preserved as unresolved rather than guessed at, exactly like `ServerReference`.
- **10 correlation rules implemented, matching skill.md §14's list**: Site→HOSTS→Application, Application→USES→ApplicationPool, Application→CONFIGURES→Configuration, Application→CONTAINS→Binary (Medium confidence — containment is not evidence of use), Service→RUNS→Binary, ScheduledTask→RUNS→Binary, COM→REFERENCES→Binary, IISBinding→BINDS_TO→Certificate, Binary→IMPORTS→Binary (same-directory-only resolution — an import named `Vendor.dll` only ever resolves to a copy in the *importing* binary's own folder, never a same-named DLL elsewhere, verified by the §23 negative fixture), and Configuration→REFERENCES→Runtime (Low confidence — a text marker, not an explicit reference).
- **Database/Endpoint/UNC references from Configuration are intentionally not correlated into edges.** No scanner yet produces `Database`/`ExternalDependency`/UNC-share entities (the Core models exist but are unused), so an edge to one would either invent a node or point at a nonexistent id — both forbidden by skill.md §12/§17. They remain visible as `Configuration.DetectedDependencyReferences` metadata (Phase 4E-1) until a future phase adds real entities for them.
- **`DependencyEdgeType` extended with three new values**: `Contains`, `Imports`, `Configures` — the existing enum (`Hosts`/`Runs`/`Binds`/`Uses`/`DependsOn`/`References`/…) covered 7 of the 9 spec vocabulary words already; only these three needed adding.
- **`DependencyGraph`/`DependencyEdge` (Core, Phase 1) needed no changes** — `AddEdge`'s existing same-Source/Target/Type merge-evidence-and-keep-higher-confidence behavior already implements skill.md §13's duplicate-evidence-merging requirement exactly as specified, so Phase 5A's engine simply calls it rather than reimplementing merge logic.
- **Determinism**: `CorrelationEngine` iterates rules in a fixed order and each rule iterates its input lists (never a dictionary's enumeration order) — verified by a test that runs the same input through the engine twice and asserts an identical edge set.
- **Diagnostics (`CorrelationDiagnostics`)**: every candidate a rule produces is accounted for — it becomes a new edge, is merged into an existing edge's evidence, or is rejected with a specific machine-readable reason (unresolved target, missing source/target entity, self-edge, no evidence) — auditable per skill.md §16, not just a pass/fail scan result.

Full detail (entities/relationships/rules table) is in `SCANNERS.md`.

## Phase 5B Addendum — Application Boundary & Cross-Scanner Correlation (as built)

New: `Core/Boundaries/ApplicationBoundary.cs` (Core) + `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Boundaries/` (Analysis) — no new project.

```
DiscoveryEntity[] + DependencyGraph (Phase 5A output, consumed as evidence — never recomputed)
        ↓
CorrelationContext (Phase 5A, reused directly — no duplicate identity-resolution code)
        ↓
BuildAnchors: one WorkloadAnchor per IIS Application / Service-with-resolvable-exe / ScheduledTask-with-resolvable-exe
        ↓
BuildCandidate per anchor: members = anchor itself
    + graph.EdgesFrom(anchor) of type Contains/Configures/Runs (Phase 5A edges, reused as evidence)
    + any Configuration whose OwnerEntityId already names this anchor directly (Rule D — read, not rediscovered)
        ↓
MergeSharedExecutableWorkloads: group Service/Task candidates by their Runs-target Dll id
    exactly 2 sharers → merge (High confidence)
    3+ sharers        → NOT merged, recorded as a SharedBinary diagnostic (too common = infrastructure, not identity)
        ↓
RecordCommonParentCandidates: same-parent-directory pairs recorded as AmbiguousCandidate diagnostics — never merged
        ↓
BoundaryAnalysisResult { ApplicationBoundary[], BoundaryDiagnostics }
```

- **`ApplicationBoundary` is a new Core model, deliberately distinct from `Core.Models.Application`.** `Application` already means "one IIS site + virtual path" (established in Phase 4A and used that way by `IisScanner` ever since) — repurposing it for Phase 5B's broader "logical workload" concept would silently change what dozens of existing Phase 4A tests assert about. `ApplicationBoundary` lives in a new `Core.Boundaries` namespace as an analysis *output* (like `DependencyGraph`/`DependencyEdge`), not a discovered entity — no `Status`/`Path`/`Architecture`, only `Id`/`Name`/`MemberEntityIds`/`Evidence`/`Confidence`/`Reason`.
- **`ApplicationBoundaryEngine` reuses Phase 5A's `CorrelationContext` and `DependencyGraph` directly** rather than re-deriving identity or re-scanning entities for ownership. Binary/Configuration membership for IIS Applications comes straight from Phase 5A's already-computed `Contains`/`Configures` edges; only Configuration ownership for Service/Task anchors (which Phase 5A's Application-only rule never covered) needed a small direct read of `Configuration.Metadata["OwnerEntityId"]` — explicitly *preserving* that metadata, not rediscovering it, per skill.md §6 Rule D.
- **The cross-workload merge rule is deliberately narrow: exactly two anchors sharing one RUNS target.** skill.md §7's own example is a Service+Task pair; skill.md §8 warns that a shared binary must not automatically imply the same workload (shared/common infrastructure is normal). The dividing line implemented here is sharer *count*, not sharer *type*: exactly 2 anchors (of any Service/Task combination) pointing at the identical resolved binary merge at High confidence; 3 or more are treated as common infrastructure (e.g. many unrelated Windows Services legitimately share `svchost.exe` as their `ImagePath`) and are recorded as a `SharedBinary` diagnostic instead of merged. This one rule is what keeps the ERP fixture's Service+Task correctly merging while never accidentally lumping together every `svchost.exe`-hosted service on the box.
- **Common parent directory and name similarity are recorded, never acted on.** `RecordCommonParentCandidates` produces `AmbiguousCandidate` diagnostics for any two boundaries whose root paths share an immediate parent — surfaced for a human/future rule to consider, but never merged automatically (skill.md §9). Name similarity (`ERP` vs `ERPWorker`) has no code path at all — it was never implemented as merge evidence, per skill.md §10's explicit instruction that it may only ever be *supporting*, never determinative, evidence the current ruleset doesn't yet use.
- **Determinism**: boundary ids are content-derived, not random — `boundary:{anchorId}` for a single-anchor boundary, `boundary:{sortedAnchorId1}+{sortedAnchorId2}` for a merged one (anchor ids sorted ordinally before joining) — verified by a test that runs the same input through the engine twice and asserts an identical boundary-id set.
- **Diagnostics cover all 8 categories skill.md §20 asks for**: workload candidates evaluated, confirmed boundaries, merged boundaries, rejected merges, ambiguous candidates, shared binaries, unresolved ownership, and evidence conflicts (the last is an empty-but-present collection today — no current rule produces conflicting ownership claims about the same entity, so leaving the type available for a future rule is honest; inventing a conflict to populate it would not be).
- **No new discovery, no filesystem/registry/process/network access anywhere in this layer** — confirmed by inspection; `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` only ever reads from the `DiscoveryEntity[]`/`DependencyGraph` it's handed.

Full detail is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 5B entry (no `SCANNERS.md` changes — no scanner contract was touched).

## Phase 5C Addendum — Dependency Expansion (as built)

New: `Core/Boundaries/DerivedWorkloadDependency.cs` (Core) + `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Expansion/` (Analysis). One narrow, additive change to `ServerSleuth.Windows/Configuration/` (justified below).

```
Configuration.Metadata (Endpoint{i}.*/Database{i}.*/NetworkPath{i}.*, Phase 4E-1)
        ↓
ExternalDependencyExtractor: one ExternalDependency candidate per detected reference
   Database{i} → Database or Redis kind (by DatabaseReference.Type)
   Endpoint{i}  → ExternalApi or LDAP kind (by scheme)
   NetworkPath{i} → FileShare kind
        ↓ deterministic id (ExternalDependencyIdentity — never guesses a missing port)
DependencyExpansionEngine.BuildExternalDependencies: dedupes by id, merges evidence, adds
   Configuration --REFERENCES--> ExternalDependency edges to a CLONE of the Phase 5A graph
        ↓
AddRuntimeVersionEdges: Configuration's NEW "RuntimeVersion: netX.Y" reference (see below)
   matched only against Runtime entities whose Version major matches — never guesses among
   multiple installed majors when only a bare family marker exists (that stays Phase 5A's job)
        ↓
BuildCertificateAssociations / BuildComAssociations / BuildExternalDependencyWorkloadAssociations:
   ApplicationBoundary-level DerivedWorkloadDependency records, each preserving a DerivedFrom
   provenance string plus the original entity-level Evidence
        ↓
DependencyExpansionResult { ExternalDependency[], ExpandedGraph, DerivedWorkloadDependency[], Diagnostics }
```

- **A genuine, narrow data-contract blocker was found and fixed, not worked around.** Phase 5C's own fixture (skill.md §30) requires linking a Configuration to a *specific* installed .NET version (`net8.0`, never `net6.0`/`net10.0` when all three are installed) — but Phase 4E-1's `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` only ever detected family-level *presence* markers (`DOTNET_ROOT`, `dotnet.exe`, `runtimeconfig.json`), never an actual version string, and `Configuration` entities never carry raw file text for Analysis to re-derive one from (skill.md's own no-raw-content rule). This is exactly the "genuine blocker" the Phase 5C instructions allow a narrow scanner change for. Fixed with one small, additive regex (`TargetFrameworkPattern`) anchored to a `TargetFramework(s)` key, populating a new `ConfigurationAnalysisResult.RuntimeVersionReferences` field — existing `RuntimeReferences` (family-only) behavior is completely unchanged, and the new field defaults to empty, so no existing test's expectations moved.
- **Three more small, additive metadata-completeness fixes to `WindowsConfigurationScanner`**: `Endpoint{i}.Path`, `Database{i}.Port`, and `NetworkPath{i}.Path` are now also persisted to `Configuration.Metadata`. All three values were already being computed by `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` since Phase 4E-1 — they just were never written to metadata, which would have forced Phase 5C's `ExternalDependency` normalization to either guess a port/path or drop real, already-detected data. Purely additive (only new keys, only when the value is present); nothing existing changed.
- **`ExternalDependency` (Core, Phase 1) is reused as-is** — no new Core entity type was needed; it already had exactly the `Kind`/`Endpoint` shape skill.md §7 wants, with the richer normalized fields (`Host`/`Port`/`Database`/`Scheme`/`Server`/`Share`/`Path`) going into `Metadata`, the same convention every other scanner already uses for entity-specific detail.
- **`DerivedWorkloadDependency` (new Core model) is deliberately NOT a `DependencyEdge`.** `ApplicationBoundary` is not a `DiscoveryEntity` and does not belong in `DependencyGraph`'s node set (a Phase 5B design decision this phase does not revisit) — collapsing "workload X appears to depend on Y" (proven only by tracing through boundary membership) into the same model as "entity X depends on entity Y" (proven directly) would blur a real distinction skill.md §21-22 explicitly cares about. Its `DerivedFrom` field is the auditable provenance chain skill.md §22 requires.
- **The COM correlation rule is deliberately narrow and two-tiered** (skill.md §5-6, §23): a boundary only ever gains a COM association when (a) the COM-referenced binary is *directly* a member of that boundary (High confidence), or (b) some boundary-member binary `IMPORTS` the COM-referenced binary (Medium confidence, one hop weaker). No other path exists — there is no "same server, so associate" fallback. Verified against a 200-unrelated-COM-registration stress test: the one legitimately-connected COM registration is the only association the ERP boundary ever receives.
- **Cross-workload derived dependencies use `Confidence = min(boundary.Confidence, edge.Confidence)`** — the same "weakest link" aggregation principle Phase 5B already established for merged boundaries, applied consistently here too.
- **Determinism**: `ExternalDependency` ids are content-derived (case-folded host/type/share, port omitted rather than guessed when absent) — verified by running the same input through `DependencyExpansionEngine` twice and asserting an identical id set.

Full detail is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 5C entry and `SCANNERS.md`'s Configuration Scanner section (updated for the additive metadata/TFM changes).

## Phase 5D Addendum — Dependency Graph Validation (as built)

New: `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Validation/` — no new project, no Core changes, no scanner changes (this phase touches nothing upstream of Phase 5A-5C's own output).

```
DiscoveryEntity[] + DependencyExpansionResult (Phase 5C) + ApplicationBoundary[] (Phase 5B)
        ↓
GraphValidator.Validate(...) — read-only: only ever calls Nodes/Edges/EdgesFrom/EdgesTo,
   never AddNode/AddEdge
        ↓
  Node Integrity → Edge Integrity → Duplicate Edge Detection → Evidence Validity →
  Provenance Integrity (+ Confidence Escalation) → Confidence Consistency → Self-Edge
  Detection → Orphan Analysis → Cycle Detection (iterative Tarjan SCC) → Unresolved
  Dependency Analysis (MissingBinary vs UnresolvedBinary) → ExternalDependency/Certificate/
  COM/Runtime domain-specific checks
        ↓
GraphValidationResult { Findings[], Orphans[], Cycles[], Summary }
```

- **This is a pure diagnostic layer — it cannot mutate what it inspects.** `GraphValidator` never calls `DependencyGraph.AddNode`/`AddEdge`; every category is a read-only pass over `graph.Nodes`/`graph.Edges`/`EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo`. Verified by a dedicated test that snapshots node/edge counts before and after validation and asserts they never change.
- **Two of skill.md's requested checks (duplicate node, duplicate edge) are structurally unreachable through the graph's own public API, and the validator is honest about that rather than fabricating a test for an impossible state.** `DependencyGraph.AddNode` throws on a duplicate Id, and `DependencyGraph.AddEdge` always merges a matching Source/Target/Type triple into the existing edge rather than adding a second one — so a `DependencyGraph` instance can never actually contain either. The validator still implements both checks properly (as regression safety nets, exactly as skill.md §5 anticipates: "Phase 5A/5C should normally already merge them. This validator exists to catch regressions."): duplicate-node detection compares the *raw* `entities[]` list (which can legitimately contain a duplicate Id that got silently dropped when the graph was built — a real, catchable defect); duplicate-edge detection groups `graph.Edges` by Source/Target/Type and is tested to confirm it correctly reports zero on a normal graph.
- **Cycle detection uses an iterative (non-recursive) Tarjan's strongly-connected-components algorithm.** A recursive implementation risks a real call-stack overflow on a long dependency chain (e.g. a 1,000-node import chain, which the large-graph test exercises); the iterative version with an explicit work stack has no such risk and remains O(V+E).
- **Confidence-escalation checking uses the same "weakest link" invariant Phase 5C's engine already enforces when computing `DerivedWorkloadDependency.Confidence` (`min(boundary.Confidence, edge.Confidence)`)** — the validator re-derives and checks `dependency.Confidence.Value <= boundary.Confidence.Value` independently, so a future Phase 5C rule change that broke this invariant would be caught here rather than silently shipping.
- **Runtime-mismatch validation re-derives the TargetFramework-to-installed-version match from the edge's own evidence text** (`"TargetFramework=net8.0 matched installed runtime version 8.0.10"`) rather than trusting that Phase 5C's `AddRuntimeVersionEdges` computed it correctly — an intentionally paranoid, self-contained check that doesn't call back into Phase 5C code at all.
- **Orphan and evidence-shape checks encode the same "installed ≠ used, registered ≠ used" principle the entire project has held since Phase 1** — a COM registration, an installed-but-unreferenced Runtime, or an unused Certificate with zero edges is `Expected`, never an error; only core infrastructure entity types (Service/ScheduledTask/WebSite/Application) with zero edges are flagged `Unresolved`, since those are the types expected to almost always participate in at least one relationship.

Full detail is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 5D entry.

## Phase 6A Addendum — Linux Foundation Discovery (as built)

New project `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/` (targets plain `net8.0`; references `ServerSleuth.Core` + `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` only — **no** reference to `ServerSleuth.Windows`, matching §3's project-responsibility table). Four scanners, each `PlatformSupport.Linux`:

```
LinuxOsScanner            — /etc/os-release (primary) + /proc/sys/kernel/* (fallback) + `uname -m` (architecture only)
LinuxProcessScanner       — /proc/<pid>/{status,cmdline,exe} via IProcProvider
LinuxPortScanner          — /proc/net/{tcp,tcp6,udp,udp6} + socket-inode→pid via ISocketOwnershipResolver, both behind the existing IPortInspector (Phase 2)
LinuxSystemdServiceScanner — `systemctl list-units --output=json` + `systemctl show <unit>` via ISystemdProvider
```

- **No Windows-specific concepts duplicated as "LinuxX" domain models** — `Server`/`OperatingSystem`/`Process`/`Port`/`Service` (Core, Phase 1) are reused exactly as-is, same as `ServerSleuth.Windows` already does. Linux-only detail (systemd's `LoadState`/`ActiveState`/`SubState`/unit file path, a process's raw UID, a port's socket inode) goes into `Metadata`, never a new field on the shared entity — consistent with skill.md (Phase 6A) §13.
- **`IPortInspector` (Phase 2) gets its second real implementation.** `LinuxPortInspector` reads `/proc/net/*` directly — never `netstat`/`ss`/`lsof` — mirroring `WindowsPortInspector`'s structure so `LinuxPortScanner` and `WindowsPortScanner` are near-identical thin wrappers around the same cross-platform abstraction, exactly the pairing Phase 2 was designed to enable.
- **`/proc/net/{tcp,tcp6,udp,udp6}` hex-address decoding** (`ProcNetParser`): IPv4 is a 4-byte word, byte-reversed; IPv6 is 4 such words kept in group order — the standard, well-documented proc(5) encoding. Verified against a known `::1` encoding and a known `127.0.0.1:8080` encoding.
- **Socket ownership (inode → PID) is resolved via `/proc/<pid>/fd/*` symlink targets shaped `socket:[inode]`** (`SocketOwnershipResolver`) — never guessed from a process's command line containing a similar-looking port number (skill.md §6 explicitly forbids this). A `fd` directory unreadable for another user's process (the common case when not running as root) simply contributes nothing to the map; the port's `OwningPid` stays null rather than being inferred.
- **The one Linux-specific mechanism not otherwise covered by an existing Infrastructure abstraction — resolving the `/proc/<pid>/exe` and `/proc/<pid>/fd/*` symlink targets — uses `System.IO.FileInfo.LinkTarget` directly**, the same pattern `ServerSleuth.Windows` already uses for OS-specific mechanisms not worth a cross-platform abstraction (e.g. WMI). A failed resolution (access denied, process exited, zombie, kernel thread) returns null, never throws.
- **systemd discovery uses only machine-readable output** — `systemctl list-units --output=json` (a real JSON array) and `systemctl show <unit>` (`Key=Value` per line) — never `systemctl status`'s human-formatted prose. `ExecStartParser` extracts the executable path from systemd's own `{ path=... ; argv[]=... }` property shape via a targeted regex, never a best-effort split.
- **A genuine, narrow data-contract gap was found and closed the same way Phase 5C's TargetFramework gap was**: `Database{i}`/`Endpoint{i}`/`NetworkPath{i}` metadata precedent aside, this phase needed no scanner-contract change — Phase 6A is entirely new code, so there was nothing upstream to patch.
- **Username resolution (`/etc/passwd` lookup for a process's UID) is explicitly out of scope for Phase 6A** — the phase's own instructions list only `/etc/os-release`, `/proc/*`, and systemd metadata as allowed read targets; `/etc/passwd` is a new file outside that list. The raw UID is preserved in `Process.Metadata["Uid"]` instead of being resolved or guessed.
- **Process start time is not populated.** Computing it would require assuming the kernel's `CLK_TCK` (typically but not portably 100) to convert `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s tick-based `starttime` field into a real timestamp — an assumption, not a fact read from the system. Left `null` rather than guessed; documented as a known limitation.
- **Real-machine validation was not performed** — the development host is Windows (skill.md (Phase 6A) §18 explicitly forbids faking a "real Linux" result on a non-Linux host). `LinuxRealMachineSmokeTest` detects the host OS at runtime and explicitly reports non-execution via test output rather than silently passing or fabricating Linux data; full fixture/fake coverage (83 tests) substitutes for it in this environment.

Full detail is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 6A entry and `SCANNERS.md`'s new Linux scanner section.

## Phase 6B Addendum — Linux Package, Runtime, and Scheduled Job Discovery (as built)

Three new scanners in `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/` (`Packages/`, `Runtimes/`, `Cron/`), plus one cross-cutting refactor that moved the platform-neutral half of Phase 4D's runtime-detection architecture into `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`.

```
LinuxPackageScanner
   ← IPackageManagerProvider (DpkgPackageProvider | RpmPackageProvider | ApkPackageProvider)
        each queries its own manager directly via IProcessRunner — never guesses the
        distribution from /etc/os-release first; an unavailable executable is simply
        NotInstalled for that one manager
   → Core.Models.Package (Phase 1, unchanged) — deduplicated by (Name, Version,
     Architecture) within ONE manager's results only, never across managers

LinuxRuntimeDiscoveryScanner
   ← IRuntimeDetector (DotNetRuntime/DotNetSdk/Java/Python/Node/Npm/Php/Go — one per family)
        reuses the SAME ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Runtimes.{IRuntimeDetector,
        RuntimeDetectionRow, RuntimeDetectionResult, RuntimeEntityKind, RuntimeEntityBuilder,
        RuntimeDetectionDefaults, DotNetCliOutputParser} Windows' Phase 4D detectors already
        use — extracted there this phase specifically so neither platform duplicates it
   → Core.Models.Runtime / Sdk (Phase 1, unchanged)

LinuxScheduledTaskScanner
   ← bounded cron sources only: /etc/crontab, /etc/cron.d/*, /etc/cron.{hourly,daily,weekly,
     monthly}/*, /var/spool/cron/crontabs/* (Debian) or /var/spool/cron/* (RHEL fallback)
   → Core.Models.ScheduledTask (Phase 1, unchanged) — cron semantics map onto it directly
```

- **A genuine architectural extraction, not a duplication.** Phase 4D's entire runtime-detection model (`IRuntimeDetector`, `RuntimeDetectionRow`, `RuntimeDetectionResult`, `RuntimeEntityKind`, `RuntimeEntityBuilder`, `RuntimeDetectionDefaults`, and the `dotnet --list-runtimes`/`--list-sdks` output-line parser) lived entirely inside `ServerSleuth.Windows`, which `ServerSleuth.Linux` must never reference. Rather than duplicating ~150 lines of entity-mapping/confidence logic for Linux, these six pieces were moved verbatim (namespace changed to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Runtimes`) into the already-shared Infrastructure project — exactly what the Phase 6B instructions asked for ("extract only the genuinely shared abstraction without breaking existing behavior"). `RuntimeEntityBuilder.Build` gained one **optional** parameter (`resolveSource`, a `Func<RuntimeDetectionRow,string>`) so each platform can supply its own Source label ("Windows Registry" vs a generic "Registry"/"Command") without the shared builder knowing about either platform's evidence-source vocabulary — Windows's own call site was updated to pass its existing `EvidenceSources.WindowsRegistry`/`WindowsProcessApi` labels explicitly, so its actual entity data is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Verified**: the full pre-existing 299-test Windows suite passed unmodified after the move.
- **What stayed Windows-only, deliberately**: `IExecutableLocator`/`ExecutableLocator` (PATH split by `;`, `.exe` extension matching) and `ArchitectureHint` (checks for `"Program Files (x86)"`) are genuinely Windows-shaped — Linux has its own `IExecutableLocator`/`LinuxExecutableLocator` (PATH split by `:`, no extension) as a distinct type in its own namespace, never referencing Windows's.
- **A real, host-specific bug was found and fixed while writing the Linux runtime-detector tests**: `System.IO.Path.GetDirectoryName`/`Path.Combine` silently rewrite `/` to `\` when the assembly happens to run on Windows (confirmed by a throwaway diagnostic test) — since Linux discovery data is always `/`-shaped regardless of which OS is doing the analysis, every Linux runtime detector now uses a small internal `LinuxPath` helper (forward-slash-only `GetDirectoryName`/`Combine`) instead of `System.IO.Path`. This was caught by a real test failure, not assumed.
- **Package identity is `package:{manager}:{name}:{version}:{architecture}`, scoped to one manager.** Two packages named `openssl` from `dpkg` and `rpm` are never merged into one entity — different managers, different entities, matching skill.md §8's explicit prohibition; `Core.Models.Package` (already existing since Phase 1, exactly for this purpose) is reused unchanged.
- **`apk`'s package/version split relies on Alpine's own `-r<release>` naming convention** (`ApkPackageLineParser`), since `apk info -v` has no per-field query-format option like `dpkg-query`/`rpm --queryformat` — a naive "last hyphen" split would break on names like `ca-certificates`.
- **Cron command redaction**: every raw command is passed through the existing `ISecretRedactor` (Phase 2) before being stored as metadata — never persisted raw. Executable-path extraction (`CronCommandPathExtractor`) only ever recognizes an explicit leading absolute path (`/opt/erp/bin/worker ...`) — a bare command name (`python3 script.py`) or shell chaining (`cd / && ...`) is left unresolved rather than guessed, since resolving either would require either a PATH lookup the cron job's own environment controls, or evaluating shell syntax — both explicitly forbidden.
- **`/etc/cron.{hourly,daily,weekly,monthly}` entries are NOT schedule-line files** — each file in those directories IS the job (run-parts convention), so no line-parsing happens there at all; the directory name alone supplies the `Trigger` ("Hourly"/"Daily"/"Weekly"/"Monthly").

Full detail is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 6B entry and `SCANNERS.md`'s Linux scanner section.

## Phase 6C Addendum — Linux Container Discovery (as built)

One new scanner in `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Containers/`, following the same provider-per-implementation pattern established by Phase 4D's `IRuntimeDetector` and reused in Phase 6B for `IPackageManagerProvider`.

```
LinuxContainerScanner
   ← IContainerRuntimeProvider (DockerContainerRuntimeProvider | PodmanContainerRuntimeProvider)
        both are thin public wrappers over one internal ContainerCliRuntimeProvider,
        parametrized only by executable name ("docker" | "podman") — Docker's and
        Podman's read-only CLI/JSON output is compatible enough for ps/inspect/images/
        volume ls/network ls+inspect that duplicating the parsing per runtime would have
        been pure duplication, not a genuine platform difference
   → Core.Models.Container (Phase 1, unchanged) reused via a Type discriminator for all
     four kinds: "Container" | "Image" | "Volume" | "Network" — no DockerContainer/
     PodmanContainer types introduced
```
