- **`ScanProgressReporter`'s existing, already-asserted-on console text was preserved byte-for-byte.** `WriteDiscoveryComplete`/`WriteAnalysisComplete` gained a `TimeSpan elapsed` parameter, but the new `Duration: X.XXs` line is only ever printed when `verbose` is true — every line an existing test already matches exactly (`"Discovery complete"`, `"Completed."`, `"Blocked:             1"`, etc.) is unchanged, so zero existing test needed modification to keep passing. The new `WriteScannerBreakdown` method (also verbose-only) iterates `AggregateDiscoveryResult.ScannerResults` in its own already-deterministic registry order — no new sorting/reclassification logic was introduced.
- **Exit-code test coverage was completed, not the exit codes themselves.** `CliExitCode` (6 values) was inspected and left untouched (skill.md §8: "preserve the existing six exit codes... do not invent a second exit-code system"). The one code with no direct test before this phase, `GeneralFailure`, now has two (`ScanCommandGeneralFailureTests.cs`), exercised via a new `ThrowingFakeDiscoveryEngine` that throws a non-cancellation exception — confirming the concise-message/no-stack-trace/no-partial-report contract `CliApplication`'s existing top-level `catch` already implemented, never adding a new code path to reach it.
- **Cancellation testing was deepened to cover the realistic mid-run case, not just the pre-cancelled-token case.** The two existing tests only ever passed an already-cancelled `CancellationTokenSource`. A new `CancellingFakeDiscoveryEngine` cancels the SAME token source the test itself holds from inside its own `RunAsync` — simulating cancellation arriving while discovery is genuinely in progress — then throws via `ThrowIfCancellationRequested`, exactly like a real scanner loop's own token check would. This is still fully deterministic (no timing race, no real keyboard input, no `Task.Delay`) — skill.md §9's "test cancellation deterministically" requirement.
- **Two genuine real-machine acceptance runs (Windows + Linux) were the final verification, not a fake/simulated substitute.** On Windows, `--verbose` surfaced real per-scanner data (14,189-entity COM scanner, 18,095-entity binary-discovery scanner, etc.) and the exit code was captured directly from the process (not through a piped `tail`, which — as Phase 10A-I/10A-J's own transcripts show — silently reports the wrong code) and confirmed to be 4 (PartialDiscovery), matching the 4 partial scanners shown. On Linux, the existing WSL2 Ubuntu instance Phase 6G had already validated against was reused — its native repo copy pre-dated the CLI project entirely (no `ServerSleuth.Cli` there), so the solution was built and run directly from the Windows-mounted path (`/mnt/f/...`) rather than re-copying, avoiding any new install beyond the .NET SDK Phase 6G had already set up with explicit prior user approval. `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` both succeeded natively on real Linux (Cli.Tests 57/57, both TFMs) and the real `scan` run correctly reported `linux-container-scanner` as `PartiallySupported` (Docker daemon unreachable) and `linux-kubernetes-scanner` as `NotInstalled` — neither was installed or forced into a passing state, per skill.md §20's explicit instruction.
- **A tooling artifact, not a ServerSleuth bug, initially made the Linux exit code look wrong.** A multi-line `bash -lc` heredoc passed through `wsl.exe` failed to propagate `$?` correctly (reproduced independently with a bare `false; echo $?` in the same invocation shape) — re-running as a single-line command confirmed the true exit code (4) matched the printed partial-scanner count exactly. Documented here so a future session does not misattribute this class of artifact to the CLI itself.

Full detail — including the complete real-machine console transcripts for both operating systems and the full regression numbers — is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 10B entry.

## Phase 10C Addendum — Target Abstraction & Remote Discovery Architecture (as built)

Introduces the architectural foundation for a future explicit scan-target concept — WITHOUT implementing any actual remote transport. New locations: `src/ServerSleuth.Core/Targets/` (`ScanTarget`, `TargetKind`, `TargetPlatform`), `src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Targets/` (`ITargetTransport`, `LocalTargetTransport`). Changed: `DiscoveryContext` (new optional `Target` property), `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` (one new registration), `ScanCommand`/`ScanOptions`/`ScanOptionsParser`/`HelpText`/`ScanProgressReporter` (the CLI's `--target` surface).

```
ServerSleuth.Core.Targets (pure data — no dependency beyond Core itself)
   TargetKind        Local | Remote
   TargetPlatform     Unknown | Windows | Linux
   ScanTarget         Id (deterministic) / Kind / Platform / DisplayName — no credential field

ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Targets (Infrastructure → Core is the existing allowed direction)
   ITargetTransport        { ScanTarget Target; IProcessRunner ProcessRunner; IFileSystemReader FileSystemReader; }
   LocalTargetTransport    the only implementation — wraps the SAME already-registered local
                            IProcessRunner/IFileSystemReader singletons, zero behavior change
```

- **`ScanTarget` lives in Core, `ITargetTransport` lives in Infrastructure — a deliberate layering decision, not an accident.** `ITargetTransport` composes `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`, both Infrastructure types; Core must never depend on Infrastructure (the project's own established one-directional layering: Core → Infrastructure → Windows/Linux → Analysis → Reporting → Cli). Putting `ITargetTransport` in Core would have required either duplicating `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`'s shape into Core (a real second abstraction, contradicting skill.md §1's "reuse existing abstractions whenever possible") or inverting the dependency graph. `ScanTarget` itself has no such conflict — it references nothing outside Core — so it stays where every other pure Core data model (`DiscoveryEntity`, `Confidence`, `DependencyEdge`) already lives.
- **`ScanTarget.Local()`'s Id is the fixed literal `"local"`, never a hostname/GUID/timestamp.** There is exactly one logical "the machine ServerSleuth is running on" per scan; a hostname could be ambiguous (renamed, DHCP-reassigned) or require an actual system call this phase has no reason to make, and a GUID/timestamp would violate skill.md §2/§15's explicit determinism requirement ("two identical local targets must produce the same target identity"). `ScanTarget.Remote(hostName)` DOES need a real identifying value (there being more than one possible remote host), so its Id is derived from a normalized (trimmed, lowercased) form of the host name the caller supplies — still fully deterministic, still never random, even though no remote transport exists to give that Id operational meaning yet.
- **`DiscoveryContext.Target` reuses the existing execution-context type rather than introducing a second, parallel "target execution context."** skill.md §4 explicitly permits this ("if the current architecture needs it, introduce a target execution context" — the existing `DiscoveryContext` already IS one, carrying Profile/CancellationToken/ScanRoots into every scanner run). The new property is deliberately NOT `required`, so every pre-existing `new DiscoveryContext { Profile = ..., CancellationToken = ... }` call site across dozens of test files and every scanner kept compiling with zero changes — confirmed by the full regression (every Phase 1-10B test passes unmodified).
- **`ITargetTransport` is a boundary the future remote transport would satisfy, not something anything in the codebase actually consumes yet.** No `IDiscoveryScanner`, no `DiscoveryEngine`, no Analysis-layer type takes an `ITargetTransport` — scanners still receive `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` directly via DI, completely unchanged from every prior phase. `ITargetTransport`'s only real job today is making `LocalTargetTransport`'s registration prove, structurally, that "local scanning is now explicitly a target" (skill.md §3) rather than an unstated default — while giving a future SSH/WinRM implementation (not built here) an already-defined shape: `Target` (which target it serves) + its OWN `ProcessRunner`/`FileSystemReader` implementations. Because `IDiscoveryScanner` implementations only ever depend on `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` (never `ITargetTransport` itself), swapping which transport is active would only ever be a composition-root concern — exactly matching skill.md §5's "must support future transports without making discovery depend directly on SSH or WinRM."
- **No command-execution API was added, and none could be without also changing the one interface every scanner already depends on.** `IProcessRunner` still has exactly one method (`RunAsync(ProcessRequest, CancellationToken)`), and `ProcessRequest` still keeps `Executable`/`Arguments` separate — the exact same shell-injection-safe shape established in Phase 2, untouched. `ITargetTransport` declares zero methods of its own (only property getters) — there was never a design considered where it could grow an `Execute(string)`-shaped API, since its entire operation surface IS the pre-existing `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` contracts. Mechanically verified (not just documented) by 4 new `NoArbitraryShellExecutionTests`.
- **`IPortInspector` was deliberately left OUT of `ITargetTransport`.** Unlike `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` (both registered once, cross-platform, in `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()`), `IPortInspector` has no cross-platform default implementation — `WindowsPortInspector`/`LinuxPortInspector` are each registered directly by their own platform's `AddServerSleuthWindows()`/`AddServerSleuthLinux()`. Wrapping it into a cross-platform `ITargetTransport` today would require restructuring that DI registration order/timing, a change beyond this phase's own "architecture foundation, no behavior change" scope — recorded as a Known Limitation rather than silently worked around.
- **Analysis's target-agnosticism is now a structurally enforced property, not merely an observed one.** `TargetAgnosticismTests` reflects over the entire compiled `ServerSleuth.Analysis` assembly — every public type's every public method parameter, method return type, and property — asserting none is `ScanTarget`/`ITargetTransport`/`DiscoveryContext`, plus a direct check that `ServerSleuth.Analysis` has no assembly reference to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` at all (so it could not even compile against `ITargetTransport` if a future change tried to introduce one accidentally).
- **The CLI's `--target` option exists to make rejection of an unsupported target explicit, not to pretend remote scanning works.** `local` (case-insensitive) is accepted and provably behaves identically to omitting `--target` (a dedicated exit-code-equality test); any other value throws `CliArgumentException` during argument parsing — before `IDiscoveryEngine`/`ITargetTransport` is even resolved from DI — guaranteeing zero discovery, zero network activity, and zero report file for a rejected target (skill.md §11: "must reject unsupported remote targets clearly rather than silently treating them as local").
- **Real Windows and Linux acceptance runs confirmed both zero regression and the new target visibility.** `--verbose` now prints `Target: local (Windows)`/`Target: local (Linux)` as the very first line of progress on both real machines, with entity counts/exit codes/timings statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10B's own real-machine numbers — proving the new DI registration and `DiscoveryContext.Target` threading add no measurable overhead and change no existing behavior.

Full detail — including the complete real-machine console transcripts and the full regression numbers — is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 10C entry.

## Phase 10D-1 Addendum — Remote Transport Foundation (as built)

Establishes the smallest secure abstraction for a future Linux→SSH / Windows→WinRM transport — WITHOUT implementing either. New: `src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Targets/RemoteOperationKind.cs`, `RemoteOperation.cs`, `RemoteOperationResult.cs`, `RemoteTransportKind.cs`, `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.cs`. Changed: `OperationStatus` (3 new values), `ScanTarget.Remote(...)` (host validation, optional `platform` parameter).

```
ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Targets (unchanged: ITargetTransport/LocalTargetTransport, Phase 10C)
   RemoteOperationKind      ProcessQuery | FileRead | DirectoryQuery — closed vocabulary,
                             sized to exactly what ITargetTransport exposes today
   RemoteOperation          Target / Kind / Executable+Arguments (ProcessQuery only) /
                             Path (FileRead|DirectoryQuery only) / Timeout — never a raw
                             command string; DescribeForLogging(ISecretRedactor) for safe diagnostics
   RemoteOperationResult    Status (OperationStatus, reused) / ExitCode / StdOut / StdErr /
                             ErrorMessage / Duration — mirrors ProcessResult's shape
   RemoteTransportKind      Ssh | WinRm — pure mapping data, Linux→Ssh, Windows→WinRm
   RemoteTargetTransportFactory
                             ScanTarget → ITargetTransport seam; Create() ALWAYS throws
                             NotSupportedException — no transport is implemented
```

- **Nothing new was executed by anything.** `RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationResult` are pure data — no `IDiscoveryScanner`, no `DiscoveryEngine`, no scanner, no CLI code path constructs or executes one. They exist only so a future SSH/WinRM `ITargetTransport` implementation has an already-defined, injection-safe shape to accept/return instead of inventing one under implementation pressure later — the same role `ITargetTransport` itself played in Phase 10C for `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`.
- **`RemoteOperationKind`'s vocabulary is deliberately smaller than skill.md's own example list.** skill.md (Phase 10D-1) §4 offers `ProcessQuery`/`FileRead`/`DirectoryQuery`/`RegistryQuery`/`NetworkQuery`/`ServiceQuery` as examples but explicitly warns not to blindly adopt them. Inspection of `ITargetTransport` (Phase 10C) shows it exposes exactly two cross-platform operation surfaces today — `IProcessRunner` and `IFileSystemReader` — so the vocabulary was sized to match those exactly: `ProcessQuery` (→ `IProcessRunner`), `FileRead` (→ `IFileSystemReader`'s non-enumerating members), `DirectoryQuery` (→ `IFileSystemReader`'s `EnumerateFiles`/`EnumerateDirectories`, kept distinct from `FileRead` since listing a subtree is a materially broader operation to authorize than reading one named file). `RegistryQuery`/`NetworkQuery`/`ServiceQuery` were NOT added — Windows Registry/WMI/`ServiceController`/COM access has no cross-platform Infrastructure abstraction to classify against yet (those scanners call the Windows-only BCL/COM APIs directly); adding those classifications now would describe operations no transport boundary can actually carry, so this is recorded as a **Known Limitation** below rather than invented speculatively.
- **`RemoteOperation` structurally cannot become an `Execute(string command)` API.** Executable/Arguments (ProcessQuery) and Path (FileRead/DirectoryQuery) are separate, typed, nullable-by-classification properties — never one opaque command string a transport would parse. Cancellation is deliberately NOT a stored field (a `CancellationToken` cannot be meaningfully persisted in an immutable record); it stays a parameter supplied at the point of execution, exactly matching `IProcessRunner.RunAsync(ProcessRequest, CancellationToken)`'s existing shape. Mechanically verified by `RemoteOperationTests.RemoteOperation_HasNoMethodOrFactoryTakingASingleRawCommandString` (reflection over every public/static member name) and `RemoteOperation_HasNoCancellationTokenProperty_OnlyExecutionTimeAcceptsOne` — the same style of proof Phase 10C's `NoArbitraryShellExecutionTests` already applies to `ITargetTransport`.
- **`OperationStatus` gained `NotInstalled`/`TransportUnavailable`/`ProtocolError` rather than a second, duplicate status enum**, per skill.md (Phase 10D-1) §5's explicit "reuse existing `OperationStatus` where appropriate" instruction. All three are meaningful outside a remote context too (e.g. a local `NotInstalled` tool lookup), so they live on the one shared enum every `ProcessResult`/`FileSystemResult<T>`/now `RemoteOperationResult` already uses — not a Remote-only parallel type. Adding enum members is additive and was confirmed non-breaking by the full regression (no exhaustive `switch` over `OperationStatus` anywhere in the codebase lacked a `default`/discard arm).
- **Secret-safe logging (skill.md §8) reuses the existing `ISecretRedactor` rather than inventing a second redaction mechanism.** `RemoteOperation.DescribeForLogging(ISecretRedactor)` passes target identity, executable, every argument, and path through the SAME redactor `SecretRedactor` (Phase 2/6E) already applies to discovered configuration content — so a credential accidentally embedded in a future remote operation's argument (e.g. `Password=...`) is redacted using already-tested rules, not a new bespoke one. Verified by `RemoteOperationTests.DescribeForLogging_RedactsASecretShapedArgument`/`_RedactsASecretShapedPath`.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §7): deliberately NO credential type/interface/provider was introduced.** Re-reviewed against the explicit instruction "if a future credential abstraction is necessary, define only the minimum provider boundary" — nothing in this phase constructs, stores, or even references a credential (`RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` throws before any authentication step could exist), so no provider boundary is "necessary" yet; adding one now would be exactly the kind of speculative, unconsumed abstraction skill.md's own architecture principles (and this project's own `CLAUDE.md`) warn against. `ScanTarget`/`RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationResult` were all re-verified (reflection-based negative tests, extending Phase 10C's `PublicProperties_NeverLookLikeACredential` pattern) to carry no password/token/key/secret/credential-shaped property.
- **`ScanTarget.Remote(...)` gained validation and an explicit `platform` parameter** (skill.md §6: "Remote targets must validate: non-empty host... supported TargetPlatform when required"). Empty/whitespace/null host names now throw `ArgumentException` — still pure data-shape validation, no DNS resolution, no connection attempt. The new optional `platform` parameter (defaulting to `Unknown`, same as before) lets a caller state a remote target's platform when already known, without probing for it — added as the SECOND parameter (before the pre-existing `displayName`) since every existing call site in the codebase and tests only ever passed a single positional `hostName` argument, confirmed by grep before the change.
- **`RemoteTargetTransportFactory` is the "Target → Transport selection" seam (skill.md §10), living in Infrastructure, never Analysis/Core.** `ResolveTransportKind(TargetPlatform)` is pure, deterministic mapping data (`Windows`→`WinRm`, `Linux`→`Ssh`, `Unknown`→throws rather than guessing) with zero I/O. `Create(ScanTarget)` always throws `NotSupportedException` for a `Remote`-kind target (after validating it isn't handed a `Local` one, which `LocalTargetTransport` already serves) — there is no code path anywhere that could construct a working remote `ITargetTransport`. Nothing in the codebase calls this factory yet (the CLI's own `--target` parsing, unchanged since Phase 10C, already rejects a remote target before `ITargetTransport` is ever resolved from DI) — it exists purely so Phase 10D-2+ has one obvious, already-tested place to plug in `WindowsRemoteTargetTransport`/`LinuxRemoteTargetTransport` later, rather than inventing this seam under implementation pressure at that point.
- **Filesystem/Process mapping (skill.md §13-14, documentation only — nothing implemented)**:
  - `IFileSystemReader.Exists`/`GetFileInfo` → a future transport's single-file metadata query (SSH: `sftp`-style `stat`/`lstat`; WinRM: a structured CIM/WS-Man `Get` on a file resource) — classified `FileRead`.
  - `IFileSystemReader.ReadTextAsync`/`ReadBytesAsync` → a future transport's single-file content fetch (SSH: `sftp` `get`/read; WinRM: a structured file-content retrieval, never a `type`/`cat` shell invocation) — classified `FileRead`.
  - `IFileSystemReader.EnumerateFiles`/`EnumerateDirectories` → a future transport's directory-listing operation (SSH: `sftp` `readdir`; WinRM: a structured CIM enumeration) — classified `DirectoryQuery`, kept distinct from `FileRead` since it is a broader-authorization operation.
  - `IProcessRunner.RunAsync(ProcessRequest, CancellationToken)` → a future transport's structured remote command invocation with Executable/Arguments kept discrete (SSH: an exec-channel request built from a discrete argv array, NEVER a single interpolated shell string; WinRM: `Invoke-Command` with a structured `-ArgumentList`, NEVER a string-built script block) — classified `ProcessQuery`. Phase 2's security guarantee (arguments are always discrete, no shell string is ever constructed) is the one invariant a future transport implementation must preserve exactly, mechanically re-verified by this phase's own tests.
- **Windows-specific discovery's future transport requirements (skill.md §15, documentation only)**: Registry (`WindowsRegistryReader`) and WMI/`System.Management`-based scanners (COM, some Service/Software paths) need a genuine `RemoteRegistry`/WMI-over-WinRM capability with no equivalent in today's `ITargetTransport` — NOT representable by `RemoteOperationKind` as it stands (see Known Limitations). IIS (`Microsoft.Web.Administration`, late-bound), Task Scheduler (late-bound COM), Certificates (`X509Store`), and `ServiceController`-based service enumeration all need the same WMI/WinRM-class capability, since none of them go through `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` today. Binary discovery / PE parsing would map cleanly onto `FileRead` (they already only read file bytes via `IFileSystemReader` today).
- **Linux-specific discovery's future transport requirements (skill.md §16, documentation only)**: `/proc`, `/proc/net`, cron files, ELF binaries, and Docker/Podman/`kubectl` config file reads all map onto `FileRead`/`DirectoryQuery` today (Linux scanners already read these exclusively through `IFileSystemReader`). systemd (`systemctl show ...`), `dpkg`/`rpm`/`apk` queries, and `docker`/`kubectl` CLI invocations all map onto `ProcessQuery` (already exclusively through `IProcessRunner` today) — meaning Linux discovery's remote transport requirement is, structurally, entirely satisfiable by the `RemoteOperationKind` vocabulary already defined in this phase, unlike several Windows scanner categories above.
- **No network connection was made anywhere in this phase** — verified structurally by `NoNetworkGuaranteeTests` (no Phase 10D-1 type's public member signature references `System.Net.Sockets`/`System.Net.Http`/`System.Net.WebSockets`/`System.Net.Security`; `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.csproj` still has exactly the same 2 `PackageReference`s Phase 10A already used, none SSH/WinRM/PowerShell-shaped) and by `RemoteTargetTransportFactoryTests.Create_NeverThrowsANetworkOrTimeoutRelatedException` (asserting the exact, immediate `NotSupportedException` rather than merely "it did not hang").
- **Local transport regression**: `LocalTargetTransport`/`ITargetTransport`/`AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` were not touched at all in this phase — `serversleuth scan`/`scan --target local` remain byte-for-byte the same code path as Phase 10C, confirmed by the full regression (every pre-existing test, including Phase 10C's own `LocalTargetTransportTests`, passes unmodified) and by re-running the real Windows/Linux acceptance scans below.

**Known Limitations**: `RemoteOperationKind` has no classification for Registry/WMI/COM/`ServiceController`-shaped operations — Windows scanner categories that depend on them (Registry, WMI-backed Service/Software paths, COM, IIS, Task Scheduler, Certificates) cannot be represented as a `RemoteOperation` yet; a future phase extending remote Windows support will need either a genuine cross-platform registry/WMI abstraction (mirroring how `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` already work) or a documented exception for those scanner categories remaining local-only. No credential provider boundary exists — a future phase implementing SSH/WinRM will need to design one from scratch, informed by whatever authentication mechanism is actually chosen (key file vs. agent vs. Windows-integrated auth), which was out of scope to guess at here. `IPortInspector` remains outside `ITargetTransport`, unchanged from Phase 10C's own documented limitation.

**Real Windows machine acceptance** (2026-08-25, same dev host, Release/net8.0-windows): `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch> --overwrite --verbose --target local` — 34,934 entities, `Target: local (Windows)`, 4/12 scanners partial, Discovery 17.55s + Analysis 1.36s, exit code **4**; `--target bogus-remote-host` rejected cleanly with exit code **2**, no discovery, no network contact. **Real Linux machine acceptance** (same reused WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 instance, built/run natively from the Windows-mounted path): `dotnet build`/`Cli.Tests` both succeeded natively (68/68 passed, both TFMs); `serversleuth scan --output /tmp/... --overwrite --verbose --target local` — 1,654 entities, `Target: local (Linux)`, 5/11 scanners partial, Discovery 2.53s + Analysis 0.06s, exit code **4**; `--target bogus-remote-host` rejected with exit code **2**. Both runs are semantically identical to Phase 10C's own local-target behavior (small entity-count/partial-scanner drift vs. Phase 10C's 34,939/1,642 is normal real-machine environment variance, not a regression — `LocalTargetTransport`/`ITargetTransport` were not touched in this phase). Full detail — including the complete console transcripts and the full regression breakdown — is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 10D-1 entry.

## Phase 10D-2 Addendum — Linux Remote Discovery over SSH (as built)

Fills Phase 10D-1's `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` seam ONLY for Linux/SSH. New:
`src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Remote/` (`SshCommandLineBuilder`, `RemoteCredential`,
`IRemoteCredentialProvider`, `StaticRemoteCredentialProvider`, `HostKeyVerificationResult`,
`IHostKeyVerifier`, `TrustedFingerprintHostKeyVerifier`, `SshConnectionOptions`,
`SshConnectResult`, `SshCommandExecutionResult`, `SshRemoteFileInfo`, `ISshSession`,
`SshNetSession`, `SshProcessRunner`, `SshFileSystemReader`, `SshRemoteTargetTransport`,
`RemoteTransportKind`). Changed: `IFileSystemReader`/`FileSystemReader` (`ReadLinkTarget`),
`LinuxProcProvider` (routes `/proc/<pid>/exe` through it instead of local `FileInfo`),
`ScanTarget` (`Host`/`Port`), `RemoteTargetTransportFactory` (`CreateSsh`),
`AddServerSleuthInfrastructure` (optional pre-built transport parameter),
`CompositionRoot`/`ScanOptions`/`ScanOptionsParser`/`ScanCommand`/`HelpText` (the CLI's
`--target`/`--ssh-*` surface). One new package: `SSH.NET` 2026.0.0.

```
Target → Transport → Structured Operation → Result  (the chain skill.md §1 asked for)

ScanTarget.Remote(host, Linux, port)
   ↓
RemoteTargetTransportFactory.CreateSsh(target, SshConnectionOptions)
   ↓
SshRemoteTargetTransport : ITargetTransport   (constructed unconnected; .Connect() explicit)
   ├── SshProcessRunner    : IProcessRunner     → ISshSession.ExecuteCommand(safely-quoted line)
   └── SshFileSystemReader : IFileSystemReader  → ISshSession.Sftp*(...)
              ↓ (real impl)
        SshNetSession : ISshSession  — wraps Renci.SshNet SshClient (exec) + SftpClient (files)
```

- **The "structured operation" chain already existed — `ProcessRequest`/`FileSystemResult<T>` — so no new operation type was introduced for scanners to call.** Phase 10D-1's own `RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationKind` remain pure, unconsumed data (still true after this phase) — the actual wiring uses the SAME `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` contracts every scanner already depends on, exactly as Phase 10C/10D-1 both anticipated ("swapping which transport is active would only ever be a composition-root concern"). `SshCommandLineBuilder.Build` is the one place `RemoteOperationKind.ProcessQuery`'s "Executable+Arguments, never a shell string" contract gets turned into the single command-line string the SSH exec channel protocol requires.
- **The SSH exec-channel protocol itself has no argv-style request — this is a documented protocol fact, not a design shortcut.** RFC 4254 §6.5's "exec" request carries exactly one opaque string, which sshd hands to the authenticated user's shell (typically `/bin/sh -c <string>`) entirely server-side, outside any client's control. `SshCommandLineBuilder.Build` is the ONE place, inside the transport only, that builds that string — via POSIX single-quote escaping (`'` → `'\''`) applied independently to the executable name and every argument, the same algorithm Python's `shlex.quote`/Ruby's `Shellwords.escape` use. Verified two ways: (1) 23 `[Theory]` cases in `SshCommandLineBuilderTests` round-trip every listed injection character (space/quote/`;`/`|`/`&`/backtick/`$()`/newline/CRLF/redirection/`&&`/`||`/`#`/glob/`~`/Unicode/empty-string) through an independently-written reference POSIX un-quoter; (2) a genuine `bash -c` round-trip against the real WSL2 Ubuntu host, including an active injection payload (`$(touch /tmp/PWNED)`) that did not fire — see PROGRESS.md for the transcript. `sudo`/`sh -c`/`bash -c` wrapping was never added by this codebase.
- **Host-key verification fails closed by construction (skill.md §5).** `IHostKeyVerifier.Verify` returns a closed two-value enum (`Trusted`/`Rejected`) — there is no third "ask interactively" option, since a scan never blocks on interactive input. `TrustedFingerprintHostKeyVerifier` is the only implementation: an explicit host:port→fingerprint allow-list, empty by default, case-insensitive fingerprint comparison, `Rejected` for anything not an exact match. `SshNetSession.Connect` wires this through Renci.SshNet's `SshClient.HostKeyReceived` event — the SHA-256 fingerprint of the OFFERED key is computed and handed to the verifier every single connection attempt; `e.CanTrust` is only ever set from the verifier's own verdict, never hard-coded `true`. Mechanically verified: `NoBlindTrustImplementationExists_AnywhereInTheAssembly` reflects over the whole `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` assembly for any `AcceptAny`/`TrustAll`/`AutoAccept`/`AlwaysTrust`-shaped member name.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §4): `IRemoteCredentialProvider`/`RemoteCredential` were added — the "if required by the chosen SSH implementation" condition Phase 10D-1 left open is now true.** `RemoteCredential` prefers a private key (`PrivateKeyBytes`+optional `PrivateKeyPassphrase`) with password as a secondary path; it is never a property of `ScanTarget`/`DiscoveryContext`/any domain or report model (unchanged from Phase 10D-1's boundary). A genuine near-miss was caught and fixed before shipping: a plain `sealed record`'s COMPILER-GENERATED `ToString()` prints every property's raw value (unlike a class) — `RemoteCredential.ToString()` is explicitly overridden to print only the username, verified by `RemoteCredentialTests.ToString_NeverPrintsThePassword`/`_NeverPrintsThePrivateKeyBytesOrPassphrase`. `StaticRemoteCredentialProvider` (holds one already-resolved credential in memory) is the only implementation — sufficient because the CLI resolves credential material once, up front, from exactly the sources the user named (`--ssh-key <path>`, `--ssh-key-passphrase-env <VAR>` — never a passphrase passed directly as an argument, which would appear in shell history/process listings). No credential is ever persisted to disk or serialized.
- **`IFileSystemReader.ReadLinkTarget` is a genuine, disclosed interface gap fix, not scope creep.** Inspecting `LinuxProcProvider` (skill.md §2's required first step) found it called `new FileInfo($"/proc/{pid}/exe").LinkTarget` directly on the LOCAL disk — bypassing `IFileSystemReader` entirely, which would have silently resolved the wrong machine's `/proc` for a remote target. Fixed by adding one new interface member (`FileSystemResult<string> ReadLinkTarget(string path)`), implementing it in the existing local `FileSystemReader` (unchanged behavior, still `FileInfo.LinkTarget`) and the new `SshFileSystemReader`, and routing `LinuxProcProvider.ResolveExeTarget` through it. This is the same category of change the Decisions Log already accepts for a "genuine data-contract blocker" (see the Phase 5C `RuntimeVersionReferences` precedent). Every pre-existing `LinuxProcProviderTests` case was unaffected (none asserted `ExecutablePath`); two new tests were added, plus one proving the SAME `LinuxProcProvider` — zero changes — works correctly against `SshFileSystemReader`.
- **A second, disclosed gap: Renci.SshNet 2026.0.0's public `SftpClient` has no SSH_FXP_READLINK operation** (confirmed by reading its own shipped XML doc comments — `SymbolicLink(path, linkPath)` only CREATES a link; nothing reads one). `SshNetSession.ReadLinkTarget` falls back to the single, well-known, read-only `readlink` utility, invoked through the exact same safely-quoted `ExecuteCommand` path as any other `ProcessQuery` — the same class of narrow, named-executable exception this codebase already accepted for `ldconfig -p` (native dependency discovery, Phase 6F) and `systemctl show` (Phase 6A). Documented at the point of implementation rather than silently worked around.
- **Remote process/filesystem mapping (skill.md §9-10)**: `SshProcessRunner` maps 1:1 onto `IProcessRunner` — one SSH exec channel per call, `ProcessResult.Ok(exitCode, ...)` for ANY exit code (matching the local `ProcessRunner`'s existing "Status=Success regardless of exit code, exit code carries the failure" semantics — verified by `SshProcessRunnerTests.RunAsync_NonZeroExit_StillStatusSuccess_ExitCodeCarriesTheFailure`), `TransportUnavailable`/`Timeout`/`Cancelled` mapped from the session result. `SshFileSystemReader` maps onto SFTP for everything BUT symlink-target reads (see above); `EnumerateFiles`/`EnumerateDirectories` reject `recursive: true` outright (`OperationStatus.InvalidInput`) rather than ever walking a remote subtree — skill.md §10/§18's "never recursively walk the entire remote filesystem" enforced structurally, not just by convention.
- **Linux scanner integration required ZERO scanner/provider changes** (beyond the disclosed `LinuxProcProvider` fix above) — skill.md §21's explicit goal. Every Linux provider (`SystemctlProvider`, `DpkgPackageProvider`/`RpmPackageProvider`/`ApkPackageProvider`, `ContainerCliRuntimeProvider`, `KubectlKubernetesProvider`, `LdconfigProvider`) already depended only on `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` — never on `ITargetTransport` directly — so making the COMPOSITION ROOT swap which implementation those two interfaces resolve to (see below) was sufficient. Locked in by `ReadOnlyCommandVocabularyTests` (source-scans each provider file for a mutating verb — `systemctl start/stop/restart/enable/disable/reload/mask/kill`, package `install/remove/upgrade/add/del/purge/erase`, `docker`/`podman exec/run/start/stop/rm/pull/push/build/kill/restart`, `kubectl exec/apply/create/delete/patch/edit/rollout/port-forward/scale/cordon` — none present) and by `ReadOnlyCommandVocabularyTests.LinuxScheduledTaskScanner_NeverExecutesADiscoveredCronCommand` confirming that scanner has no `IProcessRunner`/`ProcessRequest` reference at all (cron stays filesystem-read-only, skill.md §15).
- **Target-aware composition is the one real architectural change (skill.md §20-22).** Every provider/scanner is registered as an `AddSingleton` — the SAME `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` singleton was, before this phase, ALWAYS the local one, resolved once at process startup. `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure` gained one new optional parameter (`ITargetTransport? remoteTransport`): when supplied, `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` are registered as THAT transport's own instances instead of constructing `ProcessRunner`/`FileSystemReader`; `CompositionRoot.Build(ScanOptions)` builds (but does not connect) an `SshRemoteTargetTransport` when `options.Remote` is set, then registers `AddServerSleuthLinux()` based on the TARGET's platform for a remote scan (always Linux, since Windows/WinRM remains unimplemented) rather than the HOST's OS — the one place `#if SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS`/`OperatingSystem.IsWindows()` gets skipped for a remote scan, so a remote scan from a Windows machine never registers Windows scanners at all. The critical safety property (skill.md §22) — a remote target is never indistinguishable from local — is mechanically proven three ways: `SshRemoteTargetTransportTests.Transport_ProcessRunnerAndFileSystemReader_AreDistinctFromTheLocalSingletons` (unit level), `RemoteCompositionTests.Build_RemoteTarget_NeverReusesTheLocalSingletons` (DI level, comparing a local-composed provider's singletons against a remote-composed one's), and `RemoteCompositionTests.Build_RemoteTarget_RegistersTheSshTransport_NeverTheLocalOne`.
- **Connection timing (skill.md §6): `SshRemoteTargetTransport` is always constructed unconnected.** `CompositionRoot.BuildSshTransport` only builds the transport (reading the key file and target platform data needed to construct it); `ScanCommand.RunAsync` calls `transport.Connect(cancellationToken)` explicitly, immediately before discovery starts — never during composition, never in a constructor. A connection failure (host-key rejected, authentication denied, host unreachable, timeout) is caught there and surfaced as a concise CLI error (`CliExitCode.GeneralFailure`) before any scanner runs — never a partial/garbled report.
- **CLI surface (`scan --target <host> --ssh-user <u> --ssh-key <path> --ssh-host-fingerprint <fp> [--ssh-port <n>] [--ssh-key-passphrase-env <VAR>]`)**: `--target local`/omitted is unchanged from Phase 10C/10D-1. Any other `--target` value is now a real remote host, but `--ssh-user`/`--ssh-key`/`--ssh-host-fingerprint` are all required — omitting any one is rejected at parse time (before any file I/O or network activity) with a message naming exactly the missing option, never a silent fallback to local and never an attempted connection. `--ssh-host-fingerprint` is mandatory, not optional, because the default host-key verifier fails closed — there is no "skip verification" flag anywhere. A passphrase is only ever accepted via an environment-variable NAME (`--ssh-key-passphrase-env`), never as a direct CLI argument, so it can never appear in shell history or a process listing.
- **No network connection was made by anything OTHER than an explicit `Connect()` call** — the phase's one legitimate new network capability. Verified: `SshRemoteTargetTransportTests.Transport_DoesNotConnect_UntilConnectIsCalledExplicitly` (constructing a transport makes zero calls into the session) and `RemoteTargetTransportFactoryTests`' existing Phase 10D-1 `NotSupportedException` coverage (unchanged — `Create(ScanTarget)` alone still never connects to anything).
- **Dependency audit (skill.md §3, §30)**: exactly one new package, `SSH.NET` 2026.0.0 (+ its own transitive `BouncyCastle.Cryptography` 2.7.0), added to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` only. `SSH.NET` was chosen over shelling out to the system `ssh` executable specifically because skill.md §3 asks for structured authentication, explicit host-key verification, controlled cancellation/timeout, and structured output/error streams — a wrapped `ssh`/`scp` process invocation would reintroduce exactly the "build a shell command from untrusted data" risk this whole architecture exists to avoid, and gives strictly less control over host-key handling (no clean way to intercept and reject a key programmatically). Version `2026.0.0` was deliberately chosen over the initially-tried `2024.2.0` after `dotnet add package` surfaced an `NU1903` high-severity advisory (GHSA-q939-rpr3-3284) on that version; `2026.0.0` restored with no advisory. `SSH.NET` is MIT-licensed, has no telemetry, and makes no network call of its own beyond the connection this codebase explicitly requests. No WinRM/PowerShell-Remoting/HTTP-client package was added — Windows remains entirely unimplemented (`RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` still always throws for it). `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis` gained no new project or package reference at all — confirmed unchanged, and `RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationKind` (Core-adjacent, Infrastructure-resident) remain unconsumed by anything, exactly as Phase 10D-1 left them.
- **Real-machine validation**: a full successful remote login-and-discover run against a live SSH server was NOT performed in this phase — no explicitly-authorized reachable Linux host with a running SSH server and available credentials existed in this environment (the WSL2 Ubuntu instance otherwise used for local acceptance testing has no `sshd` installed, and installing one requires `sudo`, which needs a password not available here; per skill.md §28's own "do not guess/discover credentials, do not scan arbitrary hosts" instruction, this was not worked around). What WAS verified against a real network stack: (1) a genuinely malformed key file correctly fails key parsing before any connection attempt; (2) a real TCP connection attempt to a real closed port (`127.0.0.1:1`, guaranteed refused) produces a real OS-level "connection refused" error, correctly caught and classified as `TransportUnavailable`, surfaced as a concise CLI error with exit code `GeneralFailure` — no crash, no stack trace, no hang. Every other requirement of skill.md §26/§27's security test suite (host-key rejection, credential non-logging, command-injection resistance, mutation-verb absence, remote-vs-local distinctness, status-mapping for every failure mode) is covered deterministically via `ISshSession`'s fake-session seam, per skill.md §27's own instruction that a live server must never be required for the normal suite. See PROGRESS.md for the full transcript and the user's explicit decision to skip the live-login acceptance run rather than have it fabricated or blocked on unavailable credentials.

**Known Limitations** (all newly surfaced or still-open from Phase 10D-1): no live-server login-and-discover acceptance run was performed (see above — environment-blocked, not a design gap); `RemoteOperationKind` still has no Registry/WMI/COM classification (Windows/WinRM remains fully unimplemented, unchanged from Phase 10D-1); `SshNetSession.ReadLinkTarget` depends on the remote host having a `readlink` binbary on `PATH` (true for essentially every Linux distribution, but not mechanically guaranteed the way an SFTP-native operation would be); cancellation/timeout tear-down of a REAL `Renci.SshNet.SshCommand` (as opposed to the fake session used in the deterministic suite) could not be verified against a live server for the same credential-availability reason above — the implementation (`SshCommand.CancelAsync()` inside a `try`/`catch` swallow, `SshRemoteTargetTransport.Dispose()` disposing both the SSH and SFTP clients) follows Renci.SshNet's documented API shape but is unverified end-to-end; one remote SSH connection is reused for the whole scan (no per-operation reconnect), avoiding the N+1-connection anti-pattern skill.md §31 warns against, but no throughput/timing measurement against a real remote host could be taken for the same reason.

Full detail — including the complete regression breakdown, the real command-line acceptance transcripts, and the injection-test transcript — is in `PROGRESS.md`'s Phase 10D-2 entry.

## Phase 10D-3A Addendum — Windows Remote Capability Model (as built)

Prepares the architecture for future Windows/WinRM remote scanning WITHOUT implementing WinRM,
PowerShell Remoting, or any network transport — a model-only phase, explicitly barred from
touching Phase 10D-2's Linux/SSH code, `DiscoveryEngine`, Analysis, Risk, Migration, or
Reporting. New: `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Remote/` (`WindowsRemoteOperationKind`,
`WindowsRemoteOperationResult<T>`, `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities`,
`WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory`), plus one new type per existing Windows-domain folder:
`Registry/WindowsRegistryQuery`+`WindowsRegistryQueryResult`+`IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations`,
`Wmi/WindowsWmiQuery`+`WmiFilterClause`+`WmiComparisonOperator`+`IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations`,
`IIS/IWindowsRemoteIisOperations`, `ScheduledTasks/IWindowsRemoteTaskSchedulerOperations`,
`Certificates/IWindowsRemoteCertificateOperations`. Nothing in `ServerSleuth.Windows` was
modified — no existing scanner/provider/interface changed.

```
ScanTarget.Remote(host, Windows)
   ↓
WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create(target)   (ALWAYS throws — no WinRM transport exists)
   ↓ (future work)
IWindowsRemoteCapabilities                      (Target + 5 narrow capability interfaces)
   ├── IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations.Query(WindowsRegistryQuery)      → satisfies IWindowsRegistryReader
   ├── IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations.Query(WindowsWmiQuery)                → satisfies IProcessWmiProvider / INetworkTableProvider
   ├── IWindowsRemoteIisOperations.GetSnapshot()                        → satisfies IIisConfigurationProvider
   ├── IWindowsRemoteTaskSchedulerOperations.GetSnapshot()              → satisfies ITaskSchedulerProvider
   └── IWindowsRemoteCertificateOperations.Query(CertificateStoreSource) → satisfies ICertificateStoreProvider
```

- **Inspection first (skill.md §2) found the Windows-domain registry surface is already fully abstracted.** `IWindowsRegistryReader`/`ComClsidReader`/`ServiceRegistryDetailReader`/`SoftwareRegistryRowBuilder` all already read the registry exclusively through the one existing read-only interface — no scanner ever touches `Microsoft.Win32.Registry` directly. `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`'s detectors (`DotNetRuntimeDetector`, `JavaDetector`, etc.) and the binary/configuration scanners already depend only on the existing cross-platform `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` — meaning Phase 10D-1's `RemoteOperationKind` (`ProcessQuery`/`FileRead`/`DirectoryQuery`) already covers them, unchanged, exactly as it already does for Linux. `WindowsPortInspector` is already fully WMI-based (`INetworkTableProvider` over `MSFT_NetTCPConnection`/`MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint`).
- **Vocabulary sizing (skill.md §5): five members, not the seven skill.md offered as a starting menu — both omissions are documented decisions, not oversights.** `ServiceQuery` was folded into `WmiQuery`: `IServiceEnumerator`'s current local implementation uses `ServiceController.GetServices()` (an SCM RPC call), but the same information (`Name`/`DisplayName`/`State`/`StartMode`) is fully representable as a `Win32_Service` WMI class query, so no dedicated vocabulary member was added for it. `ComRegistryQuery` was folded into `RegistryQuery`: `ComClsidReader`/`ComRegistrationSource` already read COM registration ENTIRELY through `IWindowsRegistryReader` today — CLSID/ProgID/InprocServer32/LocalServer32/TypeLib/Version are all just registry key/value reads at specific paths, needing no vocabulary of their own.
- **`WindowsRemoteOperationKind` is a SEPARATE enum from Phase 10D-1/10D-2's `RemoteOperationKind`, deliberately, not an oversight.** The Linux/SSH-era enum (`ProcessQuery`/`FileRead`/`DirectoryQuery`) maps 1:1 onto the generic, OS-agnostic `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` primitives the SSH transport already implements; Registry/WMI/IIS/Task-Scheduler/Certificate concepts have no such generic shape and are Windows-domain concepts, so the new enum — and every other new type in this phase — lives in `ServerSleuth.Windows`, never `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`. This keeps Phase 10D-2's SSH code completely untouched (mechanically verified — see Architecture Tests below) and matches the project's own layering rule that platform-specific concepts stay out of the shared cross-platform layer.
- **Registry capability (skill.md §6): one request shape, not three.** `WindowsRegistryQuery` collapses `IWindowsRegistryReader`'s three existing methods (`GetSubKeyNames`/`GetValues`/`GetValue`) into one record — `IncludeSubKeys`/`IncludeValues`/`ValueNames` select which of the three behaviors is wanted, and both flags may be set together (a single round trip reading both a key's subkeys and its values, which `ComClsidReader` already effectively does today via two separate local calls). Reuses the existing `Microsoft.Win32.RegistryHive`/`RegistryView` BCL enums rather than inventing parallel ones. Structurally incapable of expressing a write/delete/rename/ACL-change/import/export — no such field exists on the type, `WindowsRegistryQuery_HasNoWriteDeleteRenameOrAclMember` locks this in via reflection.
- **WMI capability (skill.md §7): structured namespace/class/property-select/equality-filter, never raw WQL, never a method invocation.** `WindowsWmiQuery` represents exactly the shape every existing WMI caller already builds by hand as a string — `ProcessWmiProvider`'s `"SELECT ProcessId, ExecutablePath, CommandLine, ParentProcessId FROM Win32_Process"` and `NetworkTableProvider`'s `"... FROM MSFT_NetTCPConnection WHERE State = 2"` (root\StandardCimv2) — as `Namespace`/`ClassName`/`Properties`/`Filters`, never assembled into a WQL string by this phase (building that string safely, the way `SshCommandLineBuilder` builds an SSH exec string, is explicitly a future transport's job). `WmiFilterClause`'s `Operator` is a closed 2-value enum (`Equals`/`NotEquals`), sized to exactly the one filter (`State = 2`) any current Windows WMI caller needs — not a general WQL expression grammar. **The one genuine, disclosed interface gap this phase found (skill.md §21): `ProcessWmiProvider.TryGetOwner`'s `InvokeMethod("GetOwner", null)` call — populating `ProcessWmiInfo.OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` — cannot be represented by `WindowsWmiQuery`, because the model has NO WMI-method-invocation capability at all (a hard security requirement — skill.md §17/§19's test 6). No narrow "well-known read-only method" allow-list was invented to route around it, unlike Phase 10D-2's `readlink`-over-`ExecuteCommand` precedent for SSH — that precedent worked because SSH already has a general-purpose, already-permitted `ProcessQuery` exec path to fall back to; WMI method invocation has no equivalent already-permitted fallback in this model, and inventing one was judged out of scope for a model-only phase.** A remote `WmiQuery` will therefore never populate a process's owner domain/user — documented as a Known Limitation, not hacked around.
- **IIS/Task-Scheduler/Certificate capabilities (skill.md §9-11): each reuses its existing local DTO directly rather than duplicating it.** `IWindowsRemoteIisOperations.GetSnapshot()` returns the same `IisSnapshot` (`IisSiteRow`/`IisAppPoolRow`/`IisApplicationRow`/`IisBindingRow`) `IIisConfigurationProvider` already returns; `IWindowsRemoteTaskSchedulerOperations.GetSnapshot()` returns the same `ScheduledTaskRow` list `ITaskSchedulerProvider` already returns; `IWindowsRemoteCertificateOperations.Query(CertificateStoreSource)` takes and returns the exact same `CertificateStoreSource`/`CertificateRow` types `ICertificateStoreProvider` already uses. All three interfaces are deliberately read-only-by-shape — no `Start`/`Stop`/`Recycle`/`Run`/`Delete`/`Register`/`Export` method exists anywhere in this model (`NoCapabilityModelInterface_ExposesAServiceMutationMethod` mechanically verifies this across all five interfaces). `CertificateRow.HasPrivateKey` remains a boolean FLAG only — no `byte[]`/exportable-key member exists anywhere in the model (skill.md §11's "never expose private key material" made structurally impossible, verified by reflection, not just documented).
- **A second, smaller disclosed gap: `IProcessEnumerator`/`ProcessEnumerator` still hard-codes local `System.Diagnostics.Process.GetProcesses()`, bypassing every injectable abstraction — the Windows analogue of the `LinuxProcProvider` gap Phase 10D-2 found and fixed.** Unlike that case, this phase did NOT refactor `ProcessEnumerator`: the new `WindowsWmiQuery` model can, in principle, represent an equivalent `Win32_Process`-based query (`ProcessId`/`Name`/`CreationDate`) sufficient for `WindowsProcessScanner`'s needs, but nothing in this codebase yet consumes it remotely (no WinRM transport exists), so fixing it now would be unverifiable, speculative scope creep — left for whichever future phase actually wires a working transport, mirroring Phase 10D-2's own established precedent of only fixing such gaps once a real consumer needs them.
- **Capability composition (skill.md §14): five narrow, independently-testable interfaces bundled behind one aggregate injection point — not one "WindowsRemoteEverything" interface, and not five ungrouped floating interfaces either.** `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` (`Target`+`Registry`+`Wmi`+`Iis`+`TaskScheduler`+`Certificates`) mirrors `ITargetTransport`'s own `Target`/`ProcessRunner`/`FileSystemReader` bundle shape exactly, while each of the five sub-interfaces (`IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations`, `IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations`, `IWindowsRemoteIisOperations`, `IWindowsRemoteTaskSchedulerOperations`, `IWindowsRemoteCertificateOperations`) remains independently mockable/testable, mirroring the FIVE EXISTING local interfaces (`IWindowsRegistryReader`/`IProcessWmiProvider`/`IIisConfigurationProvider`/`ITaskSchedulerProvider`/`ICertificateStoreProvider`) this whole model was designed against 1:1.
- **`WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create` always throws — the same seam shape `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` had before Phase 10D-2 filled it in for SSH (skill.md §3, §14, §18, §27).** No implementation of `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` exists anywhere in the production `ServerSleuth.Windows` assembly — only a test-only `FakeWindowsRemoteCapabilities` recording double exists, in the test project, mechanically verified by `NoConcreteImplementationOfIWindowsRemoteCapabilities_ExistsInTheProductionAssembly` (skill.md §17's "cannot silently fall back to local execution" requirement — there is nothing to fall back TO, by construction).
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §16): unchanged from Phase 10D-1/10D-2 — deliberately not extended.** `ScanTarget` gained no new property; no credential type/interface was introduced in this phase at all, since nothing in the capability MODEL (pure request/result data) needs to authenticate anything. A future WinRM implementation will need its own injected credential provider, the same shape `IRemoteCredentialProvider` took for SSH — explicitly left as an open boundary for that future phase, not designed here.
- **Security guarantees (skill.md §17) verified structurally, not just by convention** — 36 new tests in `WindowsRemoteCapabilityModelTests`/`WindowsRemoteCapabilityArchitectureTests`, covering all 16 of skill.md §19's numbered requirements: registry/WMI read-only shape, no `Execute`/`RunScript`/`PowerShell`/`Shell`/`Invoke`-named member anywhere in the model, no registry/WMI/service/task/IIS mutation method, no certificate private-key export member, no COM activation member, no credential property on `ScanTarget`/`WindowsRemoteOperationResult<T>`/any query type, no concrete production implementation (no silent local fallback), pure-data construction (no network call), and no credential-looking text in a `System.Text.Json` serialization of every query type. All run against `FakeWindowsRemoteCapabilities` — zero live Windows remote host required anywhere, matching Phase 10D-2's own §27 "deterministic suite never needs a live server" precedent.
- **Architecture tests (skill.md §20)**: the new capability-model types reference no `ServerSleuth.Reporting`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis`/GUI namespace in any public member signature; `ServerSleuth.Windows.csproj` has no project reference to Reporting/Analysis and gained no new `PackageReference` (still exactly its pre-existing 5 packages — `Microsoft.CSharp`/`Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions`/`System.Management`/`System.Reflection.Metadata`/`System.ServiceProcess.ServiceController`); `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.csproj` still has exactly the 3 packages Phase 10D-2 left it with (SSH.NET unchanged, confirming the Linux/SSH implementation was not touched); `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`'s compiled assembly defines no `WindowsRemote*`-named type at all (the capability model never leaked out of the Windows-domain project).
- **Existing scanner compatibility (skill.md §21): zero scanners modified.** Every Windows scanner/provider file is byte-for-byte unchanged from Phase 10D-2. The two disclosed gaps above (`ProcessWmiProvider.TryGetOwner`, `ProcessEnumerator`) were found during inspection but deliberately left as documented Known Limitations rather than "hacked around" with an unsafe capability — per this phase's own explicit instruction.
- **Local/regression validation**: full solution rebuild (0 warnings/errors) and full `dotnet test` run confirm **1642/1642 passed** (the pre-existing 1606-test baseline plus these 36 new tests), 0 failed/skipped. No real-machine remote-Windows testing was performed or fabricated (no WinRM transport exists to test against — skill.md §23's own explicit prohibition); existing real-machine Windows-local/Linux-local acceptance results are unaffected since no scanner code changed.

**Known Limitations**: `WindowsWmiQuery` cannot represent WMI method invocation of any kind — `ProcessWmiInfo.OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` (sourced today via `Win32_Process.GetOwner()`) has no remote-capability equivalent in this model; a future phase would need to either accept that gap permanently or design a narrow, explicitly-justified read-only-method allow-list before it could be closed. `IProcessEnumerator`/`ProcessEnumerator` still bypasses every injectable abstraction (hard-coded local `System.Diagnostics.Process.GetProcesses()`) — representable in principle by a future `Win32_Process` `WindowsWmiQuery`, but not refactored since nothing consumes it remotely yet. No live WinRM/remote-Windows testing exists or was attempted, by this phase's own explicit scope — `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create` still always throws for every target.

**Phase 10D-3B readiness**: the capability model is architecturally complete and exercised by 36 deterministic tests; a future phase implementing real WinRM would need to (a) choose a WinRM/WS-Man client library (the Phase 10D-2 `SSH.NET`-selection process — inspect existing deps, evaluate a maintained library over hand-rolled protocol code, document the choice — is the template to follow), (b) design the injectable credential boundary this phase deliberately left open, (c) implement `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` against that library, satisfying the five interfaces this phase defined without changing them, (d) decide whether to accept the two disclosed gaps permanently or close them, and (e) wire `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory`/`CompositionRoot`/CLI `--target`/`--winrm-*` options the same way Phase 10D-2 wired the SSH equivalents.

## Phase 10D-3B Addendum — Windows Remote / WinRM Transport (as built)

Fills Phase 10D-3A's always-throwing `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create` seam with a real
WinRM/WS-Man transport — the first working remote Windows discovery path, mirroring Phase
10D-2's SSH milestone for Linux. Explicitly did NOT touch Phase 10D-2's Linux/SSH code,
`DiscoveryEngine`, Analysis, Risk, Migration, or Reporting.

```
ScanTarget.Remote(host, Windows)
   ↓
WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.CreateWinRm(target, WinRmConnectionOptions, credentialProvider)
   ↓
WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities : IWindowsRemoteCapabilities   (constructed unconnected; .Connect() explicit)
   ├── WinRmRegistryOperations : IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations   → StdRegProv (root\default) method calls
   ├── WinRmWmiOperations      : IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations        → allow-listed WQL SELECT/WHERE queries
   ├── WinRmIisOperations / WinRmTaskSchedulerOperations / WinRmCertificateOperations   → disclosed gaps, always NotInstalled
              ↓ (real impl, all sharing ONE session)
        CimWinRmTransport → ICimSession → CimNetSession : wraps Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure.CimSession

WinRmWindowsProviderSet — the target-aware composition seam: wraps the capabilities above as
the SAME NINE local interfaces every Windows scanner already depends on (IWindowsRegistryReader,
IProcessWmiProvider, IProcessEnumerator, INetworkTableProvider, IProcessNameResolver,
IServiceEnumerator, IIisConfigurationProvider, ITaskSchedulerProvider, ICertificateStoreProvider)
   ↓
WindowsRemoteTargetTransport : ITargetTransport   (registered by CompositionRoot; AddServerSleuthWindowsRemote)
```

- **Package decision (skill.md §3, §30): `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure` 3.0.0 — the native CIM/WS-Man client library PowerShell's own `Get-CimInstance -CimSession`/`Invoke-CimMethod` cmdlets are built on.** Evaluated against skill.md §3's checklist: maintained (owned by Microsoft/PowerShellTeam, 33M+ downloads, actively released), MIT-licensed, no telemetry, no `System.Management.Automation` (PowerShell script engine) dependency anywhere — confirmed by reflecting the whole `ServerSleuth.Windows` assembly for any reference to that namespace (`ServerSleuthWindowsAssembly_ReferencesNoPowerShellAutomationNamespace`). It exposes `WSManSessionOptions` (a genuinely WS-Man-protocol session, distinct from the DCOM-based `DComSessionOptions` the same package also offers but this codebase never uses), structured typed `CimInstance`/`CimMethodParameter`/`CimMethodResult` objects (never a raw command/script string), explicit TLS/certificate-validation flags, and per-operation `CimOperationOptions.Timeout`/`CancellationToken`. `dotnet add package` surfaced no vulnerability advisory (unlike Phase 10D-2's SSH.NET, which needed a version bump to clear one). Exactly ONE new package reference in the whole solution — `ServerSleuth.Windows.csproj` grew from 5 to 6 `PackageReference`s; its own transitive `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure.Runtime.Win`/`Runtime.Unix` are not separately referenced. No WinRM/WS-Man/PowerShell-scripting package was added to `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` — all confirmed unchanged.
- **The absolute security rule (skill.md §4) is enforced structurally, not just by convention.** No member anywhere in the new types is named `ExecutePowerShell`/`ExecuteCommand`/`RunScript`/`RunShell` (`TransportType_HasNoExecuteRunScriptPowerShellOrShellMember`, run against `ICimSession`/`CimWinRmTransport`/`WinRmRegistryOperations`/`WinRmWmiOperations`). `WindowsWmiMethodAllowList` is a hard-coded, closed (namespace, class, method) allow-list — `CimNetSession.InvokeMethod`/`CimWinRmTransport.Query` both re-check it independently before any network call, so even a coding mistake elsewhere could never reach an unapproved WMI class or method.
- **Registry capability (skill.md §9) is `StdRegProv` (root\default), invoked as a structured CIM method call — the one Microsoft-documented, WS-Man-reachable, non-PowerShell way to read a remote Windows registry.** `WinRmRegistryOperations` maps `WindowsRegistryQuery` onto `StdRegProv`'s `EnumKey`/`EnumValues`/`GetStringValue`/`GetExpandedStringValue`/`GetDWORDValue`/`GetBinaryValue`/`GetMultiStringValue` — the READ subset only; `StdRegProv` also exposes `SetStringValue`/`CreateKey`/`DeleteKey`/etc., none of which appear on the allow-list, so a write method could never be reached even by a coding mistake (`Registry_OnlyEverInvokesStdRegProvReadMethods`, `StdRegProvReadMethods_ContainsNoWriteDeleteOrCreateVerb`). **Disclosed, honest limitation: `RegistryView.Registry32` is rejected outright (`OperationStatus.Unsupported`), never silently answered with the 64-bit view instead** — `StdRegProv` has no WOW6432Node-redirection parameter the way the local `Microsoft.Win32.RegistryKey.OpenBaseKey(hive, view)` API does, so there is no way to honor a 32-bit-view request at all; `WindowsComScanner`'s `ComRegistrationSource.LocalMachine32` probe therefore finds fewer 32-bit-only COM registrations on a remote scan than a local one.
- **WMI capability (skill.md §10) is a hard allow-list of exactly four (namespace, class) pairs — `Win32_Process`/`Win32_Service` (root\cimv2), `MSFT_NetTCPConnection`/`MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint` (root\StandardCimv2) — covering every WMI class any existing Windows scanner queries.** `WinRmWmiOperations` builds the `SELECT ... FROM ... WHERE ...` WQL string itself (the one place, mirroring `SshCommandLineBuilder`'s role) from `WindowsWmiQuery`'s already-structured `Properties`/`Filters`; a filter's string VALUE is always single-quote-escaped before being embedded, verified with an actual SQL/WQL-injection-shaped payload (`Wmi_Query_BuildsAParameterizedWhereClause_NeverConcatenatesAnUnquotedStringValue`).
- **The `Win32_Process.GetOwner()` gap Phase 10D-3A disclosed is resolved DELIBERATELY, not silently worked around (skill.md §11).** `WindowsWmiMethodAllowList` DOES include `Win32_Process.GetOwner` — proving the capability CAN be represented structurally as a typed instance-method invocation (`ICimSession.InvokeMethod` with `instanceKeyProperties` identifying one process by its `Handle`/ProcessId key). But `WinRmProcessWmiProvider` (the adapter `WindowsProcessScanner` actually consumes) NEVER calls it from its bulk `GetAll()` path: doing so would turn one bulk `Win32_Process` query into 1+N remote calls per scan, exactly the N+1 anti-pattern skill.md §29 forbids trading against two optional fields. `ProcessWmiInfo.OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` are therefore ALWAYS `null` for a remote scan — a permanent, documented limitation, never a fallback to local `System.Diagnostics.Process`.
- **IProcessEnumerator gap (skill.md §12) resolved via option B — routed through `IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations` (`Win32_Process`), never through local `System.Diagnostics.Process.GetProcesses()`.** `WinRmProcessEnumerator` queries `ProcessId`/`Name`/`CreationDate` and parses the CIM datetime string via `System.Management.ManagementDateTimeConverter` (reused from the pre-existing `System.Management` package reference — no new dependency). The LOCAL `ProcessEnumerator` itself is untouched; a remote Windows scan simply never registers it (`AddServerSleuthWindowsRemote` registers `WinRmProcessEnumerator` instead) — proven by `Build_RemoteWindowsTarget_NeverResolvesAnyLocalWindowsInterfaceImplementation`.
- **IIS/Task Scheduler/Certificates (skill.md §13-15) are DISCLOSED capability gaps, not hacked around with PowerShell/appcmd/schtasks.exe text parsing — per this phase's own §34 instruction to stop and report rather than weaken the read-only/no-arbitrary-execution guarantee.** IIS's optional `root\WebAdministration` WMI provider WOULD be reachable through the same `CimWinRmTransport`, but its class/property schema could not be verified against a real host with the provider installed (none was available in this environment) — implementing it from memory risked silently wrong site/binding data, worse than reporting nothing. Task Scheduler 2.0 has no WMI provider at all (`Win32_ScheduledJob` only covers the deprecated `AT` command) and no WS-Man binding on its COM API. Certificate stores are local-only (`X509Store`); the registry-BLOB-parsing alternative uses an undocumented, unverified internal serialization format this phase declined to guess at. `WinRmIisOperations`/`WinRmTaskSchedulerOperations`/`WinRmCertificateOperations` all return `OperationStatus.NotInstalled` with a diagnostic explaining exactly why — never fabricated data (`DisclosedGapImplementations_AlwaysReportFailure_NeverFabricateData`).
- **Service/COM/Port (skill.md §16-18) required ZERO new capability types — both Phase 10D-3A folding decisions held.** `WinRmServiceEnumerator` queries `Win32_Service` (root\cimv2) through the exact same `IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations` allow-list; `MapState`/`MapServiceType` translate its space-containing display strings (`"Start Pending"`, `"Own Process"`) into the same PascalCase tokens the local `ServiceController`-backed enumerator already produces, a disclosed best-effort (not guaranteed exhaustive for every possible WMI value). `WindowsComScanner` needed no change at all — it already reads COM registration entirely through `IWindowsRegistryReader`, now WinRM-backed. `WinRmNetworkTableProvider`/`WinRmProcessNameResolver` reuse the identical `MSFT_NetTCPConnection`/`MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint` query the LOCAL `NetworkTableProvider` already issues; `WinRmProcessNameResolver` caches one bulk `Win32_Process` lookup per scan rather than resolving one process name per port (avoiding N+1).
- **Remote configuration/filesystem/process (skill.md §19) is a DISCLOSED gap, resolved by EXCLUSION rather than a wrong or silently-local answer — the single most consequential architectural decision in this phase.** No WS-Man-reachable, non-PowerShell structured filesystem or process-execution bridge exists for Windows (SFTP has no WinRM equivalent). `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`, `WindowsConfigurationScanner`, `WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner` (all three depend on `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`) and `WindowsOsScanner` (reads local `Environment.*` via `EnvironmentSnapshot.Capture()`, with no remote bridge either) are ALL EXCLUDED from `AddServerSleuthWindowsRemote`'s scanner registry — proven never registered by `Build_RemoteWindowsTarget_ExcludesTheDisclosedLocalFallbackRiskScanners`. `WindowsComScanner`/`WindowsScheduledTaskScanner`/`IisScanner` still take a hard `IFileSystemReader` dependency (for OPTIONAL physical-path-verification enrichment, a pre-existing degraded mode every one of their mapping methods already accepted a `null` reader for) — satisfied by `UnavailableRemoteFileSystemReader`, which returns `Unsupported` for every member WITHOUT EVER calling `System.IO`, so these three scanners degrade to "path verification unavailable" instead of silently checking the wrong (scanning) machine's disk.
- **Target-aware composition (skill.md §20) is the Windows-domain counterpart to Phase 10D-2's own finding.** `AddServerSleuthWindowsRemote(WinRmWindowsProviderSet)` (new) registers the nine local-shaped interfaces as the WinRM-backed instances instead of `AddServerSleuthWindows()`'s local ones; `CompositionRoot.Build` branches on `options.WindowsRemote is not null` (checked before the pre-existing SSH/local branch), building a `WindowsRemoteTargetTransport` and wiring it through `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure` exactly like `SshRemoteTargetTransport` already is. **A genuine, disclosed platform-of-origin limitation**: this whole branch is compiled only under `SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS` (the pre-existing `net8.0-windows`-only constraint Phase 10A's own multi-targeting already established) — a Windows/WinRM remote scan can only be INITIATED from a Windows-hosted `serversleuth` binary; the plain `net8.0` (Linux-hosted) build has no reference to `ServerSleuth.Windows` at all and throws a clear `NotSupportedException` if `--winrm-user` is used there. Linux/SSH remote scanning is unaffected either direction (it already worked from either host OS, since the SSH transport lives in the cross-platform `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` project).
- **No local fallback (skill.md §21, §25 items 1-6, 25) is the mandatory, structurally-verified guarantee.** `Build_RemoteWindowsTarget_NeverResolvesAnyLocalWindowsInterfaceImplementation` asserts, by concrete type, that all nine interfaces resolve to their `WinRm*` implementation, never `WindowsRegistryReader`/`ProcessWmiProvider`/`ProcessEnumerator`/`NetworkTableProvider`/`IisConfigurationProvider`/`ServiceEnumerator`/`TaskSchedulerProvider`/`CertificateStoreProvider`. `Build_RemoteWindowsTarget_NeverReusesTheLocalSingletons` compares against a genuinely local composition's `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` singletons — never the same instance, and the remote ones are the inert `UnavailableRemoteProcessRunner`/`UnavailableRemoteFileSystemReader`, never able to touch a resource of either kind (local OR remote) since no remote bridge exists. `ExactlyOneConcreteImplementationOfIWindowsRemoteCapabilities_ExistsInTheProductionAssembly_AndItIsWinRmBacked` confirms `WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities` is the ONLY production implementation of `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` anywhere in the `ServerSleuth.Windows` assembly.
- **Failure handling (skill.md §22) reuses the shared `OperationStatus` enum, mapped from `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure.CimException.NativeErrorCode`** — `AccessDenied → AccessDenied`, `InvalidOperationTimeout → Timeout`, `NotFound → NotFound`, `InvalidNamespace`/`InvalidClass`/`NotSupported`/`MethodNotFound`/`MethodNotAvailable → NotInstalled`, `InvalidQuery`/`QueryLanguageNotSupported`/`InvalidParameter → ProtocolError`, everything else `→ ExecutionFailed`; a raw (non-CIM) exception during connect or an operation `→ TransportUnavailable`. Verified partially by inspection of the mapping code (no live server to exercise every `NativeErrorCode` value against), fully for the two cases a real network stack COULD exercise — see Real-machine validation below.
- **A genuine, real-network-stack finding corrected mid-implementation: `CimSession.Create` alone does NOT eagerly connect.** Unlike SSH.NET's `SshClient.Connect()`, a WS-Man CIM session is lazily connected on its first real operation — an initial implementation that called only `Create()` returned "successfully" even against a guaranteed-closed port (`127.0.0.1:1`), with the real failure only surfacing later as scanner-level `PartiallySupported` statuses (0 entities, 4/8 scanners partial) rather than an upfront, clean connect failure. Fixed by calling `CimSession.TestConnection(out _, out var exception)` immediately after `Create()` inside `CimNetSession.Connect` — this forces the real WS-Man round trip eagerly, matching skill.md §8's "never connect until the scan begins, but DO fail cleanly at that point" expectation, and matches Phase 10D-2's own SSH connect-timing behavior. Re-verified against the same real closed port after the fix: `serversleuth.exe scan --target 127.0.0.1 --winrm-user tester --winrm-password-env <VAR> --winrm-port 1` now fails immediately with a genuine WS-Man "client cannot connect to the specified destination" message (the exact text WinRM's own error strings produce), exit code `GeneralFailure` — no crash, no hang, no partial report.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §5-6): `WindowsRemoteCredential`/`IWindowsRemoteCredentialProvider`/`StaticWindowsRemoteCredentialProvider`, mirroring the SSH-era shapes exactly.** `Password` is a `SecureString` (the exact type `CimCredential`'s constructor requires) — no intermediate plain-`string` password exists between "read from the environment variable" and "handed to the CIM library." `WindowsRemoteCredential.ToString()` is explicitly overridden (the same near-miss Phase 10D-2 caught for `RemoteCredential`) to print only the username, verified by `WindowsRemoteCredential_ToString_NeverPrintsThePassword`. Three authentication mechanisms supported — `Negotiate` (default), `Kerberos`, `CredSsp` — `Basic`/`Digest` deliberately NOT exposed (send credentials in a plaintext-equivalent or weakly-hashed form unless already TLS-protected, and are not a common real Windows Server configuration). CLI surface: `scan --target <host> --winrm-user <u> --winrm-password-env <VAR> [--winrm-domain <d>] [--winrm-port <n>] [--winrm-no-ssl] [--winrm-auth negotiate|kerberos|credssp]` — a password is only ever read from an environment-variable NAME, never a direct argument; `--target`+`--ssh-user`+`--winrm-user` all together is rejected as ambiguous at parse time.
- **TLS/server-certificate validation (skill.md §7) fails closed by construction, with no injectable bypass at all — deliberately a STRICTER design than SSH's trust-on-first-use fingerprint model, because WinRM-over-TLS uses standard PKI, not a single host key.** `WinRmConnectionOptions.UseSsl` defaults to `true`; whenever it is `true`, `CimNetSession.Connect` hard-codes `WSManSessionOptions.CertCACheck`/`CertCNCheck`/`CertRevocationCheck` to `true` — no field, flag, or code path anywhere sets any of them to `false`. `NoEncryption` is hard-coded `false` UNCONDITIONALLY (message-level Negotiate/Kerberos encryption remains mandatory even on the non-TLS 5985 listener, which is a legitimate, common real WinRM configuration — `--winrm-no-ssl` only ever affects `UseSsl`, never encryption). Verified structurally: `ServerSleuthWindowsAssembly_HasNoCertificateBypassMember` reflects the WHOLE assembly (public and non-public members) for any `TrustAll`/`AcceptAny`/`SkipCertificateValidation`/`IgnoreSslErrors`/`AlwaysTrust`-shaped name — none found, mirroring Phase 10D-2's `NoBlindTrustImplementationExists_AnywhereInTheAssembly` precedent exactly.
- **Fake transport (skill.md §26): `FakeCimSession`** — records every query/method invocation, independently re-checks `WindowsWmiMethodAllowList`, and can simulate connect failure/timeout/cancellation via injectable handlers. The entire 18-test `WinRmTransportSecurityTests` suite plus the 6-test `WindowsRemoteCompositionTests` suite run against it — zero live WinRM host required for the deterministic suite (skill.md §27's own instruction).
- **Real Windows remote acceptance (skill.md §27): NOT performed against a genuine authorized live host, by explicit environment constraint — no explicitly-authorized WinRM-reachable Windows host with available credentials existed in this environment.** What WAS verified against a REAL network stack on the actual dev host: (1) a missing `--winrm-password-env` environment variable is rejected cleanly before any connection attempt; (2) a real TCP/WS-Man connection attempt to a real closed port (`127.0.0.1:1`, guaranteed refused, WinRM not listening) produces the genuine OS/WinRM-level "client cannot connect to the specified destination... WS-Management" error, correctly classified `TransportUnavailable`, surfaced as a concise CLI error with exit code `GeneralFailure` — no crash, no stack trace, no hang; (3) this same real check is what surfaced and led to fixing the `TestConnection()` eager-connect gap documented above. No credentials were guessed, discovered, or fabricated; no arbitrary host was scanned — the same discipline Phase 10D-2 followed for its own equivalent SSH limitation.
- **Local regression** (2026-08-25, same dev host, Debug build): local Windows scan — **34,942 entities**, `Target: local (Windows)`, 4/12 scanners partial, exit code **4** (PartialDiscovery) — statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10D-3A's own 1642-baseline-era local runs; confirms none of this phase's changes altered local Windows scanning in any way (no local scanner/provider file was modified). Linux local and Linux/SSH regressions were not independently re-run in this phase (no Linux-domain file was touched at all — `ServerSleuth.Linux`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Remote` are both untouched, confirmed by `git`-equivalent file-scope review of every change this phase made).
