- **Performance (skill.md §29)**: one `ICimSession` is constructed and connected once per remote Windows target, reused for every query/method invocation any scanner makes (`WinRmWindowsProviderSet`/`WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities` share one `CimWinRmTransport`) — no N+1 connection pattern. `WinRmProcessNameResolver` caches one bulk `Win32_Process` query rather than one per port; `WinRmProcessWmiProvider` deliberately never issues the N+1 `GetOwner()` pattern (see above). No throughput/timing measurement against a real remote host could be taken (see the live-acceptance limitation above) — local-scan timing is unaffected since no local code path was touched.
- **Timeout/cancellation (skill.md §23-24) — a disclosed simplification, not a full per-capability breakdown.** `WinRmConnectionOptions` distinguishes `ConnectTimeout` (applied to `CimSessionOptions.Timeout` during `Connect`) from one shared `OperationTimeout` (applied via `CimOperationOptions.Timeout` to every registry/WMI call) — meeting "at minimum distinguish connection timeout from operation timeout," but NOT skill.md §24's fuller ask of a separate timeout per capability type (registry/WMI/IIS/task-scheduler/certificate) — IIS/task-scheduler/certificate have no real operation to time at all (disclosed gaps), and registry/WMI share one transport-level value in this phase. Per-call `CancellationToken` support exists at the `ICimSession`/`CimWinRmTransport` level, but `IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations.Query`/`IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations.Query`'s own signatures (fixed by Phase 10D-3A, not modified here) carry no `CancellationToken` parameter — the same coarse-grained limitation the pre-existing LOCAL `IWindowsRegistryReader`/`IProcessWmiProvider` interfaces already have, not a regression this phase introduced. `Connect()` itself does honor a caller-supplied `CancellationToken` end to end.

**Known Limitations** (all newly surfaced or carried forward from Phase 10D-3A): `Win32_Process.GetOwner()` is structurally representable but never invoked from the bulk process path — `OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` are permanently unavailable for a remote scan. `RegistryView.Registry32` reads are unsupported remotely (`StdRegProv` has no WOW6432Node equivalent) — fewer 32-bit COM registrations found remotely than locally. IIS/Task Scheduler/Certificate discovery are fully unimplemented for remote scanning (always `NotInstalled`/`Failed`, never fabricated). `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`/`WindowsConfigurationScanner`/`WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner`/`WindowsOsScanner` never run for a remote Windows target at all (excluded, not degraded). A Windows/WinRM remote scan can only be initiated from a Windows-hosted `serversleuth` binary. `WinRmServiceEnumerator`'s WMI-string-to-`ServiceController`-string mapping is a disclosed best-effort, not exhaustively verified against every possible `Win32_Service` value. No genuine live-server login-and-discover acceptance run was performed (environment-blocked, not a design gap — see above). Per-query cancellation is not threaded through the Phase-10D-3A-fixed interface signatures.

**Phase 10E readiness**: Windows remote discovery over WinRM is functionally real (Registry/WMI/Process/Port/Service/COM all work end-to-end against an actual WS-Man endpoint) and exercised by 31 new deterministic tests (38 additional test executions in the multi-TFM regression total, since 7 of them run under both Cli.Tests TFMs) plus genuine real-network-stack verification; only a live-server login-and-discover run remains outstanding, blocked purely on host/credential availability. A future phase could: (a) obtain an explicitly-authorized WinRM-reachable Windows host and run the full genuine acceptance scan skill.md's own §27/§28-equivalent describes; (b) investigate and implement the `root\WebAdministration` IIS WMI provider mapping against a real IIS host with it installed; (c) design a narrow, explicitly-justified allow-list if remote WMI-method-based process-owner resolution is ever judged worth the N+1 cost; (d) thread per-query `CancellationToken` support through `IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations`/`IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations` (and their LOCAL counterparts) if fine-grained mid-query cancellation is ever required.

## Phase 10E-1 Addendum — Remote End-to-End Pipeline Integration (as built)

Verifies (and, only where a genuine gap existed, completes) the integration of the already-real
Linux/SSH (Phase 10D-2) and Windows/WinRM (Phase 10D-3B) remote transports with the complete,
single, pre-existing pipeline — no new production architecture was needed, since Phase 10A/10C/
10D-2/10D-3B had already built this correctly. This phase is almost entirely test-only.

- **Audit finding (skill.md §1): the integration was already complete.** `ScanCommand.RunAsync`/`ScanPipelineRunner` (Phase 10A) have never had a target-specific branch beyond the one-time `Connect()` dispatch for `SshRemoteTargetTransport`/`WindowsRemoteTargetTransport` — `DiscoveryContext.Target`, `AggregateDiscoveryResult`, `CorrelationEngine`, `ApplicationBoundaryEngine`, `DependencyExpansionEngine`, `GraphValidator`, `RiskRuleEngine`, `RiskAggregator`, `MigrationAssessmentEngine`, `MigrationPlanEngine`, `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine`, `ReportDtoMapper`/`JsonReportRenderer`/`HtmlReportRenderer`/`ReportArtifactFactory`/`LocalFileReportExporter` are all invoked identically regardless of `ScanTarget.Kind`. `RemoteCompositionTests`/`WindowsRemoteCompositionTests` (Phase 10D-2/10D-3B) already proved the transport-selection half of this at the DI level. **No production code changed as a result of this audit** — only a small test-harness extension (below) was needed to make the ALREADY-real integration exercisable deterministically.
- **The one genuine gap found: the CLI test harness (`CliTestRunner`/`TestServiceProviderFactory`) could not inject a REMOTE `ITargetTransport`.** It always built a local `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` composition regardless of the `--target`/`--ssh-*`/`--winrm-*` args passed to it, so no existing CLI-level test could observe a remote target flowing through the real pipeline end to end. Fixed additively: `TestServiceProviderFactory.Build`/`CliTestRunner.RunAsync` gained an optional `ITargetTransport?` parameter (defaulting to the previous local behavior — every pre-existing call site is unaffected) that, when supplied, is threaded through the SAME `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure(transport)` overload Phase 10D-2 already built for exactly this purpose.
- **`FakeRemoteTargetTransport` (test-only, `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests`) is deliberately NOT a real `SshRemoteTargetTransport`/`WindowsRemoteTargetTransport`.** It carries a `Remote`-kind `ScanTarget` and inert `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` doubles that throw if ever invoked. Because `ScanCommand`'s `Connect()` dispatch only pattern-matches those two CONCRETE types, this double skips straight to discovery — deliberately NOT re-testing real SSH/WinRM connect semantics (host-key verification, TLS, credential handling), which Phase 10D-2's/10D-3B's own fake-session suites already exhaustively cover and this phase does not duplicate (skill.md §14).
- **`RemotePipelineIntegrationTests` (12 tests, `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests`)** feeds the SAME 17-entity ERP fixture every prior phase's own end-to-end tests already use (`ScanCommandErpEndToEndTests`) through the real CLI, with a `Remote` Linux and a `Remote` Windows target, plus disclosed-gap scanner statuses (`linux-container-scanner`/`linux-kubernetes-scanner` `NotInstalled` for Linux; `windows-iis-scanner`/`windows-scheduled-task-scanner`/`windows-certificate-scanner` `NotInstalled` for Windows, matching Phase 10D-3B's own documented gaps exactly) — proving the SAME migration/risk numbers a local scan of that fixture already produces, the correct `Target: remote:<host> (<Platform>)` identity in console output, and that a disclosed-gap scanner's honest status reaches the console/report rather than being silently dropped or faked as success.
- **No separate pipeline exists — verified structurally, not just by inspection.** `NoSeparateRemotePipelineType_ExistsAnywhereInTheLoadedAssemblies` reflects over the `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis`/`ServerSleuth.Cli` assemblies for any `Remote*Engine`/`RemoteScanPipeline`-shaped type name — none exist, and none were created by this phase.
- **Analysis remains platform-agnostic — verified structurally.** `AnalysisAssembly_ReferencesNoPlatformOrTransportSpecificType` confirms `ServerSleuth.Analysis`'s own referenced-assembly list contains no `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`/`Renci.SshNet`/`Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure`/raw-networking-namespace reference — unchanged from every prior phase, since this phase touched no Analysis file.
- **Failure semantics (skill.md §9)**: a partial scanner failure (`AccessDenied`/`Failed`/`PartiallySupported`) over a remote target still yields `CliExitCode.PartialDiscovery` with a full report written — the SAME existing contract `ScanCommandPartialDiscoveryTests` already proves for local, now proven for remote too, reusing the same exit-code logic (`ScanCommand`'s `hasPartialCoverage` check reads `discovery.ScannerStatuses` — target-agnostic by construction).
- **Security (skill.md §10)**: `RemoteScan_NeverLeaksCredentialShapedValues_IntoTheExportedReport` injects a password-, SSH-fingerprint-, and PEM-private-key-shaped string into a discovered entity's metadata and confirms none of them appear in `report.json`/`report.html`/console output for a remote scan — the SAME `ISecretRedactor`-backed redaction path every local scan already goes through, since nothing in the pipeline treats a remote-origin entity differently.
- **Determinism (skill.md §11)**: `RemoteLinuxTarget_IdenticalInput_ProducesByteIdenticalReports_AcrossTwoRuns` runs the identical fake remote discovery result through the CLI twice and asserts the exported JSON/HTML are byte-identical once the one legitimate non-deterministic field (a wall-clock "generated at" timestamp, present in three different encodings across JSON/HTML — raw `+00:00`, JSON's `+00:00`, HTML's `&#x2B;00:00`) is normalized out — everything the pipeline itself computes (IDs, ordering, correlation edges, boundaries, risk/migration results) is confirmed identical.
- **Performance (skill.md §12)**: not separately measured — the SAME ERP fixture Phase 10A/10B/10D-x's own performance-adjacent tests already exercise (and the SAME synthetic 10k/35k-entity fixtures `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests` already use) apply identically regardless of `ScanTarget.Kind`, since no pipeline stage branches on it; no new N×M behavior was introduced because no pipeline stage was modified.
- **Real-machine validation (skill.md §13): NOT performed against a genuine live remote host — no explicitly-authorized SSH Linux host or WinRM Windows host was available in this environment, the same constraint Phase 10D-2/10D-3B already disclosed for their own real-acceptance runs.** No arbitrary host was scanned, no credential was guessed, no remote service was installed to manufacture a result. Existing real local Windows/Linux validation was not re-run in this phase since no production scanner/composition file was modified.
- **Regression**: full solution rebuild (0 warnings/errors) and full `dotnet test` confirm **1704/1704 passed** (the pre-existing 1680-test baseline plus 24 additional executions — `RemotePipelineIntegrationTests`' 12 tests run under both `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` TFMs), 0 failed/skipped.

**Known Limitations**: real live-remote (SSH or WinRM) end-to-end acceptance was not performed, for the same environment reason as Phase 10D-2/10D-3B. `FakeRemoteTargetTransport` intentionally does not exercise real transport connect/failure-classification semantics (already covered elsewhere) — this phase proves PIPELINE integration, not transport-layer correctness. Performance was not independently re-measured against a new large-scale remote-specific fixture (the existing local-fixture performance coverage was judged sufficient, since no pipeline stage differs by target).

**Phase 10E-2 readiness**: the full pipeline is proven target-agnostic end to end for both fake-remote-Linux and fake-remote-Windows discovery data, with no separate pipeline anywhere and no credential leakage. A future phase could pursue a genuine live-remote acceptance run (Linux SSH and/or Windows WinRM) once an authorized host becomes available, or begin whatever Phase 10E-2 scope is defined next — neither was started here, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.

## Phase 10E-2 Addendum — Remote Security & Failure Hardening (as built)

Hardening-only phase: audited the already-real SSH (10D-2) and WinRM (10D-3B) transports for
failure/cancellation/disposal/credential-leakage/local-fallback gaps, found the transports
themselves already extensively tested, fixed ONE genuine defect, and added focused coverage for
what was genuinely untested. No remote architecture was redesigned.

- **Audit finding: SSH-side hardening was already extensive.** `TrustedFingerprintHostKeyVerifierTests` already covers matching/mismatched/unknown-host/empty-allow-list fingerprint rejection and the whole-assembly blind-trust tripwire; `RemoteCredentialTests` already covers `ToString()` never printing secrets; `SshProcessRunnerTests` already covers success/non-zero-exit/not-connected/timeout/cancellation/no-raw-shell-string; `SshCommandLineBuilderTests` already covers 23 injection-character cases; `SshRemoteTargetTransportTests` already covers construction/target-identity/local-singleton-distinctness/connect-once/dispose-once. Nothing here needed rewriting.
- **The one genuine defect found and fixed: `CimWinRmTransport.Query`/`InvokeAllowedMethod` had no `try`/`catch` around the call into `ICimSession`.** The REAL `CimNetSession` implementation already catches `OperationCanceledException` internally and returns `OperationStatus.Cancelled` (never throws), so this was never reachable in production — but `ICimSession`'s own interface contract does not require that of every implementation, so `CimWinRmTransport` itself had no defense-in-depth: a differently-behaved (even a future or test) `ICimSession` implementation that let `OperationCanceledException` propagate would have surfaced as an unhandled exception at the transport boundary — exactly skill.md (Phase 10E-2) §10's "cancellation gets converted into fake success" AND "no unhandled exception escapes the intended boundary" both being at risk simultaneously. Fixed minimally: both methods now wrap their `session` call in `try { ... } catch (OperationCanceledException) { return Failure(Cancelled); }` — no behavior change for the real `CimNetSession` path (which never throws that exception anyway), purely defense-in-depth. Verified by `WinRmFailureHardeningTests.Query_CancelledToken_ReturnsCancelledStatus_NeverThrowsNeverFakesSuccess`/`InvokeAllowedMethod_SessionThrowsOperationCanceled_ReturnsCancelledStatus_NeverThrowsUnhandled`, which use a `FakeCimSession` configured to throw exactly that exception — a scenario the pre-fix code would have let escape uncaught.
- **SSH failure-classification coverage extended (skill.md §2, §9)**: `SshFailureHardeningTests` adds the full connect-failure classification matrix (`HostKeyRejected`→`TransportUnavailable`, `AuthenticationFailed`→`AccessDenied`, `Unreachable`→`TransportUnavailable`, `TimedOut`→`Timeout`, `Cancelled`→`Cancelled`) as one parameterized test — a genuine finding surfaced while writing it: `SshConnectResult.HostKeyRejected()` maps to `OperationStatus.TransportUnavailable`, NOT `AccessDenied` (a defensible existing classification — the connection itself was refused at the protocol level before any authentication was attempted — but worth having explicit, named test coverage for rather than leaving implicit). Also adds: dispose-after-failed-connect, no-retry-storm (exactly one session call per failed connect attempt), determinism of repeated identical failures, `ProcessRunner`/`FileSystemReader` never swapped for a local implementation after a failed connection, one remote command's failure never affecting an independent command over the same shared session, and a credential-material sweep of a connect failure's error text.
- **WinRM failure-classification/hardening coverage added (skill.md §3, §9, §11, §16)**: `WinRmFailureHardeningTests` covers connect-failure classification variety, the cancellation fix above, `CimWinRmTransport.Dispose` disposing the underlying session exactly once (including after a failed connect), no local fallback after a failed connection (every provider in `WinRmWindowsProviderSet` remains WinRM-shaped even when `Connect` throws), a credential-material sweep of connect-failure text plus a structural check that `WinRmConnectException` has no field capable of holding a `WindowsRemoteCredential`, full `OperationStatus` propagation-through-transport audit (`AccessDenied`/`NotFound`/`NotInstalled`/`ProtocolError`/`Timeout`/`TransportUnavailable` all pass through `CimWinRmTransport.Query` unchanged), determinism of repeated identical failures, no-retry-storm, and one WMI class's query failure never affecting an independent class's query over the same shared session.
- **Local fallback prevention (skill.md §7)**: re-verified rather than re-built — Phase 10D-2's `SshRemoteTargetTransportTests`/`RemoteCompositionTests` and Phase 10D-3B's `WindowsRemoteCompositionTests` already prove, at both the unit and DI-composition level, that a remote target never resolves a local provider type. This phase adds the FAILURE-PATH variant of that same guarantee: `WinRmFailureHardeningTests.FailedConnection_NeverFallsBackToLocal_ProviderSetHasNoLocalImplementationToFallBackTo` and `SshFailureHardeningTests.FailedConnection_NeverSwapsProcessRunnerOrFileSystemReaderForALocalImplementation` prove the object graph remains 100% remote-shaped even AFTER a connection attempt has already failed — not just before one is attempted.
- **CLI-level remote cancellation/failure integration (skill.md §10, §14, §15)**: `RemoteFailureIntegrationTests` (Cli.Tests) extends Phase 10E-1's pipeline-integration suite with cancellation-specific scenarios over a `FakeRemoteTargetTransport`-carried remote target — pre-cancelled token, cancellation mid-discovery, an unexpected discovery exception, and determinism of a repeated cancellation scenario — all reusing the exact same `CliExitCode`/no-partial-report contract `ScanCommandCancellationTests`/`ScanCommandGeneralFailureTests` already establish for local, now proven identical for remote.
- **Credential leakage (skill.md §4)**: no new redaction mechanism was needed — `RemoteCredential.ToString()`/`WindowsRemoteCredential.ToString()` (10D-2/10D-3B) already redact, and this phase's new tests sweep actual connect-failure exception text (not just the report) for sentinel secret values, finding none. `SshConnectResult`/`WinRmConnectException` were both additionally confirmed structurally incapable of holding a credential object at all (no property/field of the credential type exists on either).
- **SSH/WinRM injection hardening (skill.md §5-6)**: re-verified, not re-built — `SshCommandLineBuilderTests`' 23 cases and `WinRmTransportSecurityTests`' allow-list/parameterized-WHERE-clause tests (10D-2/10D-3B) already cover every adversarial input class skill.md §5-6 lists. No new WMI method or SSH command surface was added by this phase, so the existing allow-lists needed no changes.
- **Resource disposal (skill.md §11)**: verified with explicit counters — `FakeSshSession.DisposeCallCount`/`FakeCimSession.Disposed`, both already present from 10D-2/10D-3B, are now asserted specifically for the FAILURE path (dispose after a failed connect), not just the success path those phases already covered.
- **Status mapping (skill.md §9)**: audited, not redesigned. No duplicate status enum was introduced — every test in this phase reuses the existing shared `OperationStatus` (and, for SSH, `SshConnectResult`'s own existing `Status` property, itself backed by `OperationStatus`).
- **Real-machine validation (skill.md §17)**: a REAL closed-port WinRM check was re-run after the `CimWinRmTransport` fix (`127.0.0.1:1`) — identical genuine WS-Man "client cannot connect to the specified destination" error, exit code `GeneralFailure`, no crash — confirming the cancellation-handling fix did not alter the connect-failure path. Real local Windows regression was re-run (`serversleuth.exe scan`, exit code 4, unaffected). **No genuine live-remote (SSH or WinRM) acceptance run was performed** — no explicitly-authorized host was available in this environment, the same disclosed constraint as every prior remote-phase (10D-2/10D-3B/10E-1).
- **Regression**: full solution rebuild (0 warnings/errors) and full `dotnet test` confirm **1743/1743 passed** (the pre-existing 1704-test baseline plus 39 additional executions — 12 `SshFailureHardeningTests` (Infrastructure.Tests), 19 `WinRmFailureHardeningTests` (Windows.Tests), 4 `RemoteFailureIntegrationTests` × 2 Cli.Tests TFMs), 0 failed/skipped.

**Known Limitations**: no genuine live-remote acceptance run was performed (environment-blocked, not a design gap). The `CimWinRmTransport` cancellation fix is defense-in-depth only — it was never reachable through the real `CimNetSession` path, so no observable production behavior change occurred; it protects against a hypothetical future/alternate `ICimSession` implementation, not a bug a live host could have exposed. Per-query cancellation still does not thread all the way from a scanner's own token through `IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations`/`IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations` (a pre-existing, disclosed Phase 10D-3B limitation, unchanged by this phase — hardening the transport's OWN exception safety was in scope; redesigning that interface's cancellation-parameter shape was not, per this phase's own "do not redesign unless a concrete defect requires it" instruction).

**Phase 10E-3 readiness**: both remote transports now have explicit failure/cancellation/disposal/determinism/no-local-fallback coverage at both the transport-unit level and the CLI-pipeline-integration level, with one genuine defense-in-depth defect found and fixed. A future phase could pursue genuine live-remote acceptance once an authorized host is available, or thread real per-query cancellation through the Windows remote-operation interfaces if that granularity is ever required — neither was started here, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.

## Phase 10E-3 Addendum — Remote Production-Safety Verification (as built)

Verification-only phase: audited the SSH/WinRM transports for the specific gaps Phase 10E-2's
own hardening pass had not yet targeted (multi-capability session reuse, partial-failure
session-survival, double-dispose safety) plus one structural credential-shape check Phase 10E-1/
10E-2 hadn't done at this granularity. **No production defect was found — zero production code
was changed in this phase.**

- **Session reuse across MULTIPLE, DIFFERENT operations (skill.md §A) — not previously tested explicitly.** Phase 10D-2/10E-2 proved "exactly one session call for a single failing operation" (no-retry-storm) and "connect called exactly once" in isolation; this phase adds `SshSessionLifecycleTests.MultipleDifferentOperations_AllReuseTheSameSession_NeverReconnect`/`WinRmSessionLifecycleTests.MultipleDifferentCapabilities_AllReuseTheSameSession_NeverReconnect`, which issue a PROCESS query + a FILE read + a DIRECTORY listing (SSH) / a REGISTRY method call + a WMI query + a SERVICE (also-WMI) query (WinRM) — the actual mix a real scan makes — through the same transport, and confirm `Connect` was still only ever called once. Both transports already did this correctly by construction (one `ISshSession`/`ICimSession` instance is shared via constructor injection, never re-created per call) — this phase proves it explicitly rather than leaving it implicit in the object graph.
- **Partial failure never disposes or invalidates the shared session (skill.md §B) — a genuine gap.** Neither prior phase had a test proving a FAILED operation leaves the session usable for a SUBSequent, independent operation. `SshSessionLifecycleTests.OneFailedOperation_NeverDisposesOrInvalidatesTheSharedSession_LaterOperationsStillWork` and `WinRmSessionLifecycleTests.OneFailedCapabilityCall_NeverDisposesTheSharedSession_LaterCallsStillWork` both prove this: a `NotFound` file read / a thrown WMI query failure is followed by an unrelated, successful call on the SAME transport, confirming the session was never disposed as a side effect of the failure (both transports already behaved this way — neither `SshFileSystemReader`/`SshProcessRunner` nor `CimWinRmTransport` ever call `Dispose` on any failure path; this test makes that guarantee explicit and permanent).
- **Repeated disposal is harmless and deterministic (skill.md §B) — a genuine gap, now closed for both transports.** Neither prior phase tested calling `.Dispose()` twice. `SshRemoteTargetTransport.Dispose`/`CimWinRmTransport.Dispose`/`WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities.Dispose`/`WinRmWindowsProviderSet.Dispose` are all simple straight-through delegations with no internal disposed-guard state — calling any of them twice simply calls the underlying `ISshSession`/`ICimSession`'s own `Dispose()` twice. `SshSessionLifecycleTests.DisposingTwice_IsHarmless_...`/`WinRmSessionLifecycleTests.DisposingTwice_IsHarmless_...` prove this raises no exception at the ServerSleuth layer — resting on the standard, industry-wide `IDisposable` contract expectation that a well-behaved disposable (both `Renci.SshNet.SshClient` and `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure.CimSession` are long-established, widely-used libraries) tolerates a repeated `Dispose()` call as a no-op. No new disposed-guard was added to ServerSleuth's own wrapper types — none was needed once this expectation was confirmed to hold for the fakes, and adding one would be speculative hardening against a failure mode neither underlying library is known to exhibit (skill.md's own "do not introduce an abstraction the existing architecture doesn't need" instruction).
- **`FakeCimSession` upgraded from a `bool ConnectCalled`/`Disposed` flag to an `int` call counter (`ConnectCallCount`/`DisposeCallCount`)** — a small, additive, backward-compatible test-fixture change (the old `bool` properties are now computed from the counters, so every pre-existing `WinRmFailureHardeningTests`/`WinRmTransportSecurityTests`/`WindowsRemoteCompositionTests` assertion against them is unaffected) — needed because a bool cannot distinguish "called once" from "called twice," which the new lifecycle tests above specifically need to assert. Mirrors `FakeSshSession`'s own pre-existing `ConnectCallCount`/`DisposeCallCount` shape exactly (10D-2), closing a fixture-parity gap between the two fake sessions.
- **Credential-shaped property structural check (skill.md §G) — a genuinely new granularity of check.** Phase 10E-1/10E-2 already swept RENDERED report text (JSON/HTML/console output) for injected sentinel secret VALUES, and Phase 10E-1 already proved `ServerSleuth.Analysis` references no Windows/Linux/SSH/WinRM assembly at all. This phase adds `NoCredentialShapedPropertyTests`, which instead inspects the TYPE SHAPE of `RiskFinding`/`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck`/`ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` directly — proving none of them has ever grown a property whose NAME suggests a credential slot (password/credential/privatekey/passphrase/apikey/bearertoken/secretvalue), and that `RiskFinding.Metadata` remains typed as `IReadOnlyDictionary<string, string>` (never wideneable to hold a strongly-typed credential object without the type itself changing, which this test would then catch).
- **`--verbose` credential safety (skill.md §D)** — Phase 10E-1's `RemoteScan_NeverLeaksCredentialShapedValues_IntoTheExportedReport` test now runs with `--verbose` included (previously it ran in the CLI's default, non-verbose mode) — the extra per-scanner/stage-duration console detail verbose mode prints is now also swept for the same sentinel secret values, closing a gap where verbose-specific output had never been checked.
- **Invalid remote target rejection before transport/network (skill.md §D)**: re-verified, not re-built — the pre-existing `ScanCommandTargetOptionTests`/`ScanCommandWindowsRemoteOptionTests` (10D-2/10D-3B) already reject a malformed remote target at `ScanOptionsParser.Parse` time, which runs entirely before `CompositionRoot.Build`/any transport construction — no network-capable code path exists between argument parsing and that rejection.
- **Real-machine validation (skill.md §Step 4)**: **no genuine live SSH or WinRM host was available in this environment** (the same WSL2 Ubuntu instance used for local Linux testing throughout this project still has no `sshd` installed, and provisioning one would require `sudo` access not available here — explicitly NOT worked around, per this phase's own "do not install sshd/WinRM... merely to manufacture acceptance" instruction). Since this phase made ZERO production changes to either transport's connect/query code path, the real closed-port network-stack verifications already performed and recorded in Phase 10D-2 (SSH, `127.0.0.1:1`), Phase 10D-3B (WinRM, `127.0.0.1:1`), and re-confirmed unchanged in Phase 10E-2 remain the current, valid evidence for that code path — they were not redundantly re-run in this phase since nothing they exercise was touched. **Live SSH/WinRM acceptance was NOT executed** in Phase 10E-3, for the same disclosed reason as every prior remote phase.
- **Regression**: full solution rebuild (0 warnings/errors) and full `dotnet test` confirm **1757/1757 passed** (the pre-existing 1743-test baseline plus 14 additional tests — 4 SSH lifecycle, 4 WinRM lifecycle, 6 credential-shape structural), 0 failed/skipped.

**Known Limitations**: no genuine live-remote acceptance run was performed (environment-blocked, unchanged from every prior remote phase). Repeated-disposal safety rests on the documented/conventional `IDisposable` behavior of `Renci.SshNet.SshClient`/`Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure.CimSession` (both long-established libraries), verified against the fakes but not against a live double-dispose of the REAL underlying client objects (no live host to construct one against). No new production capability, abstraction, or architecture was added — this phase strictly added test coverage.

**Phase 10F readiness**: both remote transports now have session-reuse, partial-failure-survival, double-dispose-safety, and structural credential-shape coverage in addition to Phase 10E-2's failure/cancellation/disposal/determinism hardening — the remote-target architecture has no further known verification gap this project's own audit process has identified. A future phase could pursue genuine live-remote acceptance once an authorized host becomes available; nothing else was started here, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.

## Phase GUI-1 Addendum — Desktop GUI Foundation (as built)

The first GUI code in this repository — a foundation/shell phase only, per its own strict scope
(skill.md GUI-1's explicit exclusion of real scan configuration, live progress, results
visualization, and report export from this phase). No prior GUI code existed anywhere in the
repository (confirmed by inspection: no `WPF`/`Avalonia`/`WinUI`/`MAUI`/`ViewModel`/`App.xaml`
reference anywhere in `src/`/`test/`/the solution file before this phase).

```
ServerSleuth.slnx
  /src/ServerSleuth.Gui/            (new)
      App/            App.xaml, App.xaml.cs           — entry point, DI startup, exception boundary wiring
      Composition/    CompositionRoot.cs               — GUI-only DI container (no scanner/discovery registration)
      Models/         GuiApplicationState.cs            — one immutable state snapshot
      Navigation/     NavigationPage.cs, INavigationService.cs, NavigationService.cs
      Services/       IApplicationStateService(+impl), IGuiExceptionHandler(+impl)
      ViewModels/      ObservableObject, RelayCommand, MainViewModel, NavigationItemViewModel, PlaceholderPageViewModel
      Views/           MainWindow.xaml(+.cs), PlaceholderView.xaml(+.cs)
      Resources/       Theme.xaml, RiskSeverityBrushConverter.cs
  /test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/     (new, 44 tests)
```

- **Framework decision: WPF, chosen over Avalonia/WinUI 3/MAUI — the smallest-dependency option, not merely the most familiar one.** Explicitly evaluated against skill.md GUI-1's own "the GUI does not have to be cross-platform... do not introduce a framework merely because it is cross-platform" instruction: this whole solution already only builds/runs/tests on Windows (`ServerSleuth.Cli`'s own multi-targeting exists SOLELY to let its `net8.0` build compile without the Windows-only `ServerSleuth.Windows` project — the actual Windows-capable executable people run is the `net8.0-windows` one), so Avalonia's cross-platform reach buys nothing here while adding a third-party dependency (SkiaSharp-backed rendering, its own styling system to learn) this project doesn't need. WinUI 3 was rejected for its heavier packaging/deployment model (MSIX, Windows App SDK runtime dependency) relative to what a GUI-1 shell needs. MAUI was rejected as mobile/cross-platform-first, a poor fit for a desktop-only sysadmin tool. WPF ships in-box with the already-installed .NET SDK's Windows Desktop workload (confirmed via `dotnet new list wpf` before committing to the decision) — **zero additional NuGet packages were needed for the UI framework itself**, the smallest possible dependency footprint of any candidate, and it is a mature, long-stable MVVM-friendly framework with no licensing cost or restriction.
- **No third-party MVVM toolkit package either (e.g. CommunityToolkit.Mvvm).** `ObservableObject`/`RelayCommand` (≈25 lines combined) are hand-written — the handful of properties/commands GUI-1's placeholder ViewModels need did not justify a new package dependency (skill.md GUI-1 §3: "do not add third-party NuGet packages unless genuinely required"). A future phase with real, larger ViewModels may reconsider this.
- **Dependency direction (skill.md GUI-1's Critical Dependency Rule, mechanically verified by `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`)**: `ServerSleuth.Gui` → `ServerSleuth.Core` (for `ScanTarget`, the credential-free domain type `GuiApplicationState.Target` reuses directly) + `ServerSleuth.Analysis` (for `RiskSeverity`, the domain enum the theme's severity-color mapping is built against) — and NOTHING else. `GuiAssembly_ReferencesNoPlatformOrScannerOrCliAssembly` confirms zero reference to `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Cli`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting` at the ASSEMBLY level — the strongest possible guarantee, since an unreferenced assembly cannot be reached from any GUI code path regardless of what any single class happens to call. No registry/filesystem/process/network API is reachable from `ServerSleuth.Gui` at all in this phase.
- **MVVM architecture**: `MainViewModel` owns the navigation-item list (a plain, deterministically-ordered array — `Pages`, never a `Dictionary`/`HashSet`) and the currently-displayed `PlaceholderPageViewModel`; `MainWindow.xaml` binds to it with zero code-behind logic beyond `DataContext` assignment (`MainWindow.xaml.cs` is 8 lines). **One reusable `PlaceholderPageViewModel`/`PlaceholderView` pair serves all six pages** (Dashboard/Scan/Results/Migration/Reports/Settings) rather than six near-identical empty ViewModel/View pairs — skill.md GUI-1's own "avoid premature abstractions" instruction; a later phase replaces the placeholder for one page at a time with a real, feature-specific ViewModel, not built on top of this disposable type.
- **Navigation (skill.md §6)**: `INavigationService`/`NavigationService` — one explicit `CurrentPage` field (never inferred from which View happens to be visible), a `CurrentPageChanged` event, and a no-op (never re-raises the event) when navigating to the already-current page — deterministic by construction, verified by `NavigationServiceTests`' explicit "navigate to each of the six pages" theory plus a same-page-is-a-no-op test. No View ever navigates another View directly — every navigation request flows through `MainViewModel.NavigateCommand` → `INavigationService.NavigateTo`.
- **Application state (skill.md §5)**: `GuiApplicationState` is ONE immutable `sealed record` snapshot (`CurrentPage`/`Target`/`IsScanRunning`/`HasResults`/`LastErrorMessage`/`IsExportAvailable`) — every transition produces a NEW instance via a `with` expression through the single `IApplicationStateService.Update(Func<state,state>)` seam, never in-place mutation, matching the same "immutable snapshot, never a mutable god object" convention every domain/result type in this solution already uses. **`Target` reuses the EXISTING `ScanTarget` domain type directly** rather than a raw display-name string — `ScanTarget` already carries zero credential fields (mechanically verified since Phase 10C, re-confirmed here by `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`) and gives the GUI a real value to eventually bind target selection to, rather than reinventing a parallel "target display" concept. `GuiApplicationState.Initial` (the DI-registered starting snapshot) is the literal "nothing has happened yet" state: no target, not scanning, no results, no error, nothing exportable — matching skill.md §12's "opening the application must not start a scan... must not contact a remote host" requirement structurally, not just by convention.
- **Theme/Resources (skill.md §7)**: `Theme.xaml` declares a palette, typography (`Text.Title`/`Text.PageTitle`/`Text.Body`/`Text.Muted`), spacing (`Spacing.Small`/`Medium`/`Large`), a common button style, a navigation-item style (with a selected-state trigger), a status-indicator style, and five `Risk.*` brush resources. **The five `Risk.*` keys are named EXACTLY after `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Risk.Models.RiskSeverity`'s five real enum members (`Info`/`Low`/`Medium`/`High`/`Critical`) — no sixth "None" brush exists, because no such member exists on the real enum**, even though skill.md GUI-1's own §7 example text mentioned "None" — `RiskSeverityBrushConverterTests.RiskSeverity_HasNoNoneValue_MatchingTheRealDomainEnum` locks in that the GUI was built against the ACTUAL domain enum, not the spec's own approximate paraphrase of it. `RiskSeverityBrushConverter` (not yet wired into any View — no risk display exists in GUI-1) maps a `RiskSeverity` value to its `Risk.<Name>` brush via `Application.Current.TryFindResource`, proven correct by reading `Theme.xaml`'s own source text directly rather than requiring a live WPF `Application`/STA thread in the test suite (see Testing below).
- **Error boundary (skill.md §8)**: `App.xaml.cs` wires all three .NET/WPF unhandled-exception surfaces — `Dispatcher.UnhandledException` (UI thread, marks `e.Handled = true` so the process never crashes), `AppDomain.UnhandledException` (anything the dispatcher didn't catch), `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` (a faulted background `Task` nobody awaited, `SetObserved()`'d) — each delegating to `IGuiExceptionHandler.Handle`. **The sanitization logic was deliberately extracted into `GuiExceptionHandler`, a plain class with no WPF `Application`/STA dependency**, specifically so it is unit-testable (WPF permits only one live `Application` instance per process, on a dispatcher thread, which would make inline sanitization logic untestable). `GuiExceptionHandler.Handle` logs the FULL exception via the EXISTING `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging` infrastructure (no second logging framework, matching skill.md §8's explicit instruction) and publishes a FIXED, generic, credential-free string (`GuiExceptionHandler.GenericUserFacingMessage`) into application state — never `exception.Message`/`exception.StackTrace`, a stricter posture than `ServerSleuth.Cli.CliApplication`'s own "no stack trace, but do show `ex.Message`" convention, chosen because a GUI's error boundary has no way to know in advance what kind of exception a future page's future feature code might throw, unlike the CLI's small, closed set of anticipated failure categories.
- **Testing strategy (skill.md §9)**: 44 tests, entirely at the ViewModel/service/composition/architecture layer — NO live WPF `Application`/STA-thread UI test was written, a deliberate choice GUI-1's own instructions explicitly sanction ("if [headless UI testing] is not [available], keep the GUI unit-test layer focused on ViewModels/services and add only lightweight shell construction tests"). `NavigationServiceTests`/`ApplicationStateServiceTests`/`GuiExceptionHandlerTests`/`MainViewModelTests` cover navigation determinism, state-transition immutability, exception sanitization, and shell-ViewModel behavior — all plain C# objects, no dispatcher needed. `CompositionRootTests` proves DI composition succeeds, resolves `MainViewModel`, shares singletons correctly, and completes in well under 2 seconds (skill.md's "DI composition should not execute discovery" performance requirement, verified with an actual `Stopwatch` bound rather than left as an unverified claim). `RiskSeverityBrushConverterTests` reads `Theme.xaml`'s own source text (see above) rather than requiring STA. `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`/`NoDuplicatePipelineEngineTests`/`NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests` are the three structural/reflection architecture tests skill.md §10 asks for.
- **Launch verification (skill.md §11, performed, not fabricated)**: `dotnet build` produced `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe`; started via `Start-Process`, the process remained running (not exited/crashed) for 3 full seconds before being deliberately terminated — a real signal that DI composition, `MainWindow` construction, XAML resource loading (`Theme.xaml`'s merged dictionary, every `StaticResource` reference in `MainWindow.xaml`/`PlaceholderView.xaml`), and the WPF message loop all started successfully with no immediate startup exception (a XAML parse error, missing resource key, or DI resolution failure would have caused a near-instant exit). No interactive verification of the rendered visual output was performed or claimed — this environment has no interactive Windows desktop session available to Claude Code's tool calls, and skill.md GUI-1 §11 itself says "do not require an interactive GUI session if the environment cannot provide one" and "do not fabricate a GUI launch result." Process cleanup was confirmed (`Get-Process` found no orphan after `Stop-Process`).
- **Security (skill.md §12)**: GUI startup is passive by construction — `App.OnStartup` builds the composition root (registers 4 GUI-only services, no scanner/discovery/transport type), resolves `MainViewModel`, and shows `MainWindow`; nothing in that path constructs a `DiscoveryEngine`, contacts a remote host, or touches the registry/filesystem/process APIs (there is nothing in the referenced-assembly graph capable of doing so at all — see Dependency direction above). `GuiApplicationState.Initial`'s all-false/all-null starting values are the mechanical proof that nothing is "running" the instant the application opens.
- **Performance (skill.md §13)**: `CompositionRootTests.Build_CompletesQuickly_NoDiscoveryOrRemoteConnectionAttempted` asserts composition-and-first-resolution completes in under 2 seconds (real local/remote discovery takes multiple seconds to tens of seconds by comparison) — a concrete, checked bound, not an unverified assumption.
- **A build-configuration finding, disclosed rather than silently worked around**: the WPF SDK's implicit item globbing only recognizes a PROJECT-ROOT `App.xaml` as the `ApplicationDefinition` item (the one that gets the generated `Main()` entry point); this project deliberately keeps `App.xaml` under `App/` (per GUI-1's own suggested folder structure), which produced `CS5001: Program does not contain a static 'Main' method` on the first build attempt. Fixed by explicitly reclassifying the item in `ServerSleuth.Gui.csproj` (`<Page Remove="App\App.xaml" /><ApplicationDefinition Include="App\App.xaml">...`) rather than moving the file to the root or abandoning the suggested folder layout.

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual verification of the rendered UI was performed (no interactive desktop session available to this environment) — only process-survival-after-launch was verified. No headless WPF UI-automation test exists (none was added, per the phase's own explicit tolerance for this). `RiskSeverityBrushConverter` is unused by any View in this phase (nothing displays risk data yet). The GUI cannot yet do anything beyond navigate between placeholder pages — by design, per GUI-1's own strict scope.

**GUI-2 readiness**: the shell, navigation, application-state, composition, theming, and error-boundary foundations are in place and tested. A future GUI-2 phase would replace one or more `PlaceholderPageViewModel`/`PlaceholderView` pairs with real feature ViewModels/Views (starting most likely with Scan configuration and target selection, per the existing `ScanTarget`/`--target`/`--ssh-*`/`--winrm-*` CLI surface this GUI would eventually need to expose), consuming `ServerSleuth.Cli`'s existing `ScanPipelineRunner`/`CompositionRoot`-equivalent orchestration rather than reimplementing it — nothing about that integration was started in GUI-1, per its own strict-stop instruction.

## Phase GUI-2 Addendum — Scan Configuration & Target Setup (as built)

Replaces the Scan page's placeholder with a real Scan Configuration UI/ViewModel/service layer.
Per its own strict scope, this phase produces a validated, credential-free `ScanRequest` and
STOPS there — no scan is ever executed, no `DiscoveryEngine`/`ScanPipelineRunner` is referenced
anywhere in the new code.

```
src/ServerSleuth.Gui/
    Models/  ScanConfigurationState.cs (non-sensitive)  ScanCredentialInput.cs (transient/sensitive)
             ScanRequest.cs (credential-free output)  ScanConfigurationValidationResult.cs
             ScanTransportKind.cs / ScanOutputFormat.cs / ScanOverwritePolicy.cs / ScanWinRmAuthenticationMechanism.cs (GUI-local mirrors)
    Services/  IScanConfigurationValidator(+impl)  IScanRequestFactory(+impl)
    ViewModels/  ScanConfigurationViewModel.cs  IPageViewModel.cs (new common shape)
    Views/  ScanConfigurationView.xaml(+.cs)
    Resources/  OverwritePolicyToBoolConverter.cs
```

- **Inspection first**: confirmed no `ScanConfiguration`/`ScanConfigurationViewModel`/`TargetConfiguration`/GUI-2 marker existed anywhere before this phase — genuinely new work.
- **The central architectural tension this phase resolves, explicitly**: skill.md GUI-2 itself says both "do not create a second credential model if `RemoteCredential` already represents the required transport input" AND (restating GUI-1's own established boundary) "no direct reference to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Windows`." `RemoteCredential` lives in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`; `WindowsRemoteCredential` lives in `ServerSleuth.Windows`; `ReportFormatOption` lives in `ServerSleuth.Cli`; `ReportOverwritePolicy` lives in `ServerSleuth.Reporting` — **all four are structurally unreachable from `ServerSleuth.Gui` without violating GUI-1's own mechanically-verified dependency boundary**, which this phase does not relax (re-confirmed by the unmodified `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`). The resolution: GUI-local, presentation-layer MIRROR types (`ScanTransportKind`/`ScanOutputFormat`/`ScanOverwritePolicy`/`ScanWinRmAuthenticationMechanism`/`ScanCredentialInput`) — same member names, same meaning, zero logic of their own, deliberately named DIFFERENTLY from the real types so no genuine duplicate-with-the-same-name exists (verified by `NoDuplicateDomainModelTests`). This is exactly the "GUI-specific adapter genuinely required" carve-out skill.md GUI-2's own Step 2/Step 6 anticipate, not a violation of "do not create duplicate representations." `ScanTarget` itself, by contrast, IS genuinely reused directly — it lives in the ALLOWED `ServerSleuth.Core` dependency, so `ScanRequestFactory.Create` calls `ScanTarget.Local`/`ScanTarget.Remote` directly, never reimplementing their normalization.
- **The credential boundary (skill.md §5, §8)**: `ScanConfigurationState` (non-sensitive, freely bindable, safe to eventually persist) is a SEPARATE type from `ScanCredentialInput` (transient, sensitive — `Username`+`Password`, the latter a `SecureString`, never a plain `string`) — deliberately never merged into one object, so a future change accidentally serializing/logging/persisting the "safe" half could never drag the sensitive half along with it. `ScanConfigurationViewModel` never exposes `Password` as a bound property AT ALL — `ScanConfigurationView`'s code-behind reads WPF's `PasswordBox.SecurePassword` directly (WPF's `PasswordBox` deliberately has no bindable `Password` property, precisely to prevent this class of leak) and hands it to `ScanConfigurationViewModel.SetPassword(SecureString)`, a write-only method — mechanically verified by `ScanConfigurationViewModel_HasNoPasswordProperty_OnlyAWriteOnlyMethod`. `ScanConfigurationViewModel` has NO dependency on `IApplicationStateService` at all (verified structurally, by constructor-parameter reflection) — meaning it has no way to write into `GuiApplicationState` even in principle, closing off that leak path architecturally rather than merely by convention. `SshHostFingerprint`/`SshPrivateKeyPath`/`SshPrivateKeyPassphraseEnvironmentVariable`/`Domain` are deliberately part of the NON-sensitive `ScanConfigurationState`, not `ScanCredentialInput` — a host-key fingerprint is public by design, a file PATH is not the key bytes, an environment-variable NAME is not the passphrase VALUE it names, and a domain is organizational metadata, matching exactly how the EXISTING CLI already treats every one of these as an ordinary, loggable argument (`--ssh-host-fingerprint`/`--ssh-key`/`--ssh-key-passphrase-env`/`--winrm-domain`) — `ScanConfigurationState_SshMetadataProperties_AreStringPathsOrNames_NeverRawKeyMaterial` locks in that these remain plain `string`, never a raw-secret-shaped type.
- **SSH is private-key-only in this GUI, deliberately narrower than the theoretical `RemoteCredential.ForPassword` backend capability.** The EXISTING CLI's own `--ssh-*` surface (`ScanOptionsParser`) never wires up SSH password authentication at all — only `--ssh-user`/`--ssh-key`/`--ssh-host-fingerprint`/`--ssh-key-passphrase-env` exist. `ScanConfigurationValidator` mirrors that REAL, actually-supported surface exactly (skill.md §4: "do not expose transport combinations the existing backend does not support") rather than the broader theoretical capability — SSH requires Username + `SshPrivateKeyPath` + `SshHostFingerprint`; no SSH password field exists anywhere in the UI. WinRM, by contrast, DOES accept a direct password via `PasswordBox` (unlike the CLI's env-var-only `--winrm-password-env` convention) — a deliberate, disclosed GUI-specific improvement: a WPF `PasswordBox` has none of the shell-history/process-listing leak risk a CLI argument would, so the stricter env-var-only CLI convention doesn't need to carry over.
- **Platform → transport mapping is STRUCTURALLY derived, never independently choosable (skill.md §4)** — `ScanConfigurationViewModel.TransportKind` has a PRIVATE setter; the only way its value ever changes is `ApplyDeterministicTransportForPlatform()`, called whenever `Platform` changes for a remote target (Linux → SSH, Windows → WinRM, unconditionally). This makes an invalid (platform, transport) combination structurally UNREACHABLE through the ViewModel's own public surface — stronger than "validated and rejected," which is how skill.md's own suggested test list (items 9-10, "invalid Linux + WinRM"/"invalid Windows + SSH") was originally framed; `TransportKind_HasNoPublicSetter_InvalidCombinationsAreStructurallyImpossible` and `EveryPlatformValue_ProducesOnlyItsOwnSupportedTransport_ForAllTransitionOrders` prove this directly. The `ScanConfigurationValidator` STILL contains the (now practically unreachable via the GUI, but defensively present) transport/platform check, since `ScanConfigurationState` itself is a plain, freely-constructible record any future caller (including a test) could build with a mismatched combination.
- **Validation (skill.md §7) is deterministic, in-memory, and side-effect-free by construction** — `ScanConfigurationValidator` performs no I/O of any kind: no DNS lookup, no ping, no TCP/SSH/WinRM connection, no certificate validation, no directory creation, no file write. The output-directory check is SHAPE-only (`Path.GetInvalidPathChars()`), never an existence/writability probe. Every `ScanConfigurationValidationError.Message` is a FIXED, pre-written string — the validator never reads `ScanCredentialInput.Password`'s CONTENT, only its PRESENCE (`Length == 0`), so a credential value can never be echoed into a message even accidentally; `PasswordValue_NeverAppearsInValidationErrors`/`PasswordValue_NeverAppearsInAnyValidationErrorMessage` prove this with an actual sentinel secret swept through every produced message.
- **The Start Scan boundary (skill.md §9-10)**: `ScanConfigurationViewModel.StartScanCommand` re-validates, and ONLY on success calls `IScanRequestFactory.Create` and raises `ScanRequested` — never for an invalid configuration (`StartScan_WithInvalidConfiguration_NeverRaisesScanRequested`). `ScanRequest` itself structurally cannot carry a credential (no such field exists at all — `ScanRequestFactoryTests.ScanRequestType_HasNoCredentialShapedProperty`); a future GUI-3 phase is expected to combine an emitted `ScanRequest` with a SEPARATELY-supplied credential (converted from `ScanCredentialInput` into the real `RemoteCredential`/`WindowsRemoteCredential` at ITS OWN composition boundary, which — unlike GUI-2 — is allowed to reference `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Windows`). Nothing in `ScanConfigurationViewModel` references `DiscoveryEngine`/`ScanPipelineRunner`/any orchestration type — verified not just by absence-of-use but by REFLECTING every constructor parameter, field, and method signature on the class (`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`), a stronger guarantee than "we didn't happen to call it."
- **UI (skill.md §11)**: `ScanConfigurationView.xaml` — Local/Remote radio buttons, a Platform combo (enabled only when Remote), a Remote Host text box (enabled only when Remote), a read-only derived Connection label (never a user-editable transport combo — see above), a Credentials section (enabled only when Remote, showing EITHER the WinRM password/domain/TLS fields OR the SSH key-path/passphrase-env/fingerprint fields depending on platform, via `IsWindowsRemote`/`IsLinuxRemote`), Output directory/format/overwrite/verbose controls, a validation-error list, and Validate/Start Scan/Cancel buttons. `Cancel` clears validation state and the transient credential (`ScanCredentialInput.Empty`) and never contacts anything (there is nothing in `CancelCommand`'s implementation capable of a network call).
- **Testing (skill.md §12-14)**: 58 new tests — `ScanConfigurationValidatorTests` (14), `ScanRequestFactoryTests` (5), `ScanConfigurationViewModelTests` (25-ish covering the full suggested list: default/local/remote-Linux/remote-Windows configuration, Local↔Remote switching, deterministic transport derivation in both directions, empty/valid hostname, every output format, overwrite/verbose toggles, validation-error population, successful validation, credential-value-never-in-messages, credentials-never-in-`GuiApplicationState`, Start-Scan success/failure/repeatability, and a `Stopwatch`-bound no-side-effects check), plus GUI-1's existing `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`MainViewModelTests` extended with GUI-2-aware cases, and three new architecture-test files (`NoDuplicateDomainModelTests`, `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`, plus the extended credential-shape tests).
- **`MainViewModel`/`IPageViewModel` (necessary, minimal GUI-1 extension)**: `MainViewModel.CurrentPageViewModel`'s type widened from `PlaceholderPageViewModel` to a new, minimal `IPageViewModel` marker interface (`NavigationPage Page { get; }`), implemented by both `PlaceholderPageViewModel` and `ScanConfigurationViewModel` — `MainViewModel`'s constructor now also takes `ScanConfigurationViewModel` (DI-registered as a singleton) and `ApplyCurrentPage` special-cases `NavigationPage.Scan` to show it; the other five pages remain placeholders, completely unchanged. `MainWindow.xaml` gained one more `DataTemplate` (for `ScanConfigurationViewModel` → `ScanConfigurationView`) alongside the existing placeholder one.
- **Launch verification (performed, not fabricated)**: `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` was rebuilt and confirmed to stay running (no crash) for 3 seconds before deliberate termination, with no orphan process afterward — the SAME evidence class GUI-1 established, re-confirmed after this phase's changes. **The Scan page's own XAML was NOT interactively exercised** (no interactive desktop session is available in this environment, and clicking a navigation button cannot be scripted without UI-automation tooling this environment does not provide) — only compile-time XAML validation (0 build warnings/errors, which does catch unresolved `x:Type`/many `StaticResource` reference errors) and the 25 `ScanConfigurationViewModelTests` (which exercise 100% of the ViewModel logic the View binds to) provide assurance here; this is disclosed as a Known Limitation, not silently glossed over.
- **Security (skill.md §14)**: no network call, no filesystem write, no scanner construction occurs during validation or Start Scan — mechanically bounded by `Stopwatch`-timed tests (`Validate_AndStartScan_CompleteQuickly_NoFilesystemOrNetworkAccess`) using an intentionally-unreachable hostname/nonexistent key path, proving the "invalid target rejected before any transport creation" guarantee holds even for a plausible-looking remote configuration.
- **Regression**: full solution rebuild (0 warnings/errors) and full `dotnet test` confirm **1859/1859 passed** (the pre-existing 1801-test baseline plus 58 new GUI tests), 0 failed/skipped.

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual verification of `ScanConfigurationView`'s actual rendered layout was performed (same environment constraint as GUI-1, now also covering the new page specifically). No headless WPF UI-automation test exists. A future GUI-3 phase still needs to design the actual `ScanCredentialInput` → `RemoteCredential`/`WindowsRemoteCredential` conversion boundary (not started here — GUI-2 only produces the credential-free `ScanRequest` half). The GUI-local mirror enums (`ScanTransportKind`/`ScanOutputFormat`/`ScanOverwritePolicy`/`ScanWinRmAuthenticationMechanism`) will need an explicit 1:1 mapping function at that same future composition boundary — not yet written, since nothing consumes it yet.

**GUI-3 readiness**: a fully tested Scan Configuration workflow exists, producing a deterministic, credential-free `ScanRequest` via the `ScanRequested` event. A future GUI-3 phase would subscribe to that event, convert the accompanying (separately-held) `ScanCredentialInput` into the real transport credential type at its own composition boundary, and begin wiring actual scan execution/progress — none of which was started in GUI-2, per its own strict-stop instruction.

## Phase GUI-3 Addendum — Scan Execution & Live Progress (as built)

Connects GUI-2's `ScanRequested` event to the REAL, unmodified scan pipeline — Discovery through
Export — and produces live, non-fabricated progress. Per its own strict-stop scope, this phase
stops at a completion summary; no Results Dashboard/report viewer/PDF/HTTP API/migration
execution exists anywhere in the new code.

```
src/ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts/          (new project — shared DTOs/interfaces only)
    Models/  ScanRequest.cs ScanCredentialInput.cs ScanProgressState.cs ScanCompletionState.cs
             ScanStage.cs ScanExecutionStatus.cs ScannerProgressInfo.cs
             ScanTransportKind.cs / ScanOutputFormat.cs / ScanOverwritePolicy.cs / ScanWinRmAuthenticationMechanism.cs
    Services/  IGuiScanExecutor.cs

src/ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost/      (new project — the composition/execution boundary)
    GuiScanExecutor.cs  DefaultGuiScanComposition.cs  GuiScanComposition.cs

src/ServerSleuth.Gui/
    Models/  ScanExecutionState.cs (ViewModel-bound aggregate)  ScanRequestedEventArgs.cs
    ViewModels/  ScanExecutionViewModel.cs
    Views/  ScanExecutionView.xaml(+.cs)

src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Orchestration/  (moved from ServerSleuth.Cli.Pipeline)
    ScanPipelineRunner.cs  ScanPipelineResult.cs  PipelineStage.cs (new)
```

- **The central architectural tension this phase resolves, explicitly**: skill.md GUI-3 requires BOTH "reuse the existing `ScanPipelineRunner`/transports/`ReportArtifactFactory`, do not duplicate the pipeline" AND (restating GUI-1/GUI-2's own boundary) "do not introduce direct GUI references to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Cli`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting`." These are jointly unsatisfiable inside a single project: actually running a scan genuinely requires those four assemblies somewhere in the executable's dependency graph, but `ServerSleuth.Gui.csproj` itself must still have ZERO direct reference to them (mechanically re-verified, unweakened, by `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`). The resolution mirrors how `ServerSleuth.Cli` itself is structured — a **separate composition/execution project**, `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost`, that IS allowed to reference Infrastructure/Windows/Linux/Reporting (it is not "the GUI" in the sense that boundary protects: no XAML, no ViewModel, no View, only `IGuiScanExecutor`'s real implementation and its own transport/service composition, structurally identical in role to `ServerSleuth.Cli.Composition.CompositionRoot`+`ScanCommand`). Introducing a straight `ServerSleuth.Gui` → `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` reference alone would have created a CIRCULAR reference (`ExecutionHost` needs `ScanRequest`/`ScanCredentialInput`/`IGuiScanExecutor`, all of which the GUI layer also needs to depend on) — resolved by extracting exactly those shared, non-platform-specific shapes into a third, small project, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` (references `ServerSleuth.Core` only), forming a clean DAG: `ServerSleuth.Gui` → `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts`, `ServerSleuth.Gui` → `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` → `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` (never the reverse). Namespaces for the relocated types remain `ServerSleuth.Gui.Models`/`ServerSleuth.Gui.Services` — a namespace is not required to equal its assembly's name — specifically so every pre-existing `using ServerSleuth.Gui.Models;`/`using ServerSleuth.Gui.Services;` statement across `ServerSleuth.Gui`/`ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` kept compiling unchanged; only the physical file location and owning `.csproj` moved. **Verified, not merely asserted**: `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests.GuiAssembly_ReferencesNoPlatformOrScannerOrCliAssembly` (unchanged) still passes — `ServerSleuth.Gui.dll`'s own `AssemblyRef` table genuinely contains no direct reference to Infrastructure/Windows/Linux/Cli/Reporting, because no source file compiled into that specific assembly ever imports a type from any of them (a C#/.NET compiler only emits an `AssemblyRef` for an assembly whose members are actually USED in the compiled IL — a transitively-available `ProjectReference` alone does not add one). `GuiAssembly_ReferencesOnlyCoreAndAnalysis_AmongServerSleuthAssemblies` was extended (not weakened) to also expect exactly `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts`/`ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` — both deliberately outside the forbidden list, for the reasons above.
- **`ScanPipelineRunner`/`ScanPipelineResult` moved from `ServerSleuth.Cli.Pipeline` to `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Orchestration`** — the single most consequential decision for CLI/GUI equivalence. This is a relocation, not a rewrite: the type only ever depended on `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis` types (never anything Infrastructure/Windows/Cli/Reporting-specific), so the move changes no dependency direction, and both `ServerSleuth.Cli.Commands.ScanCommand` and `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.GuiScanExecutor` now call the LITERAL SAME class — the strongest possible guarantee of "CLI and GUI use the same pipeline semantics" (skill.md's own Step 5 requirement), proven directly by `CliGuiEquivalenceTests`. `ScanPipelineRunner.Analyze` gained one additive, optional parameter — `Action<PipelineStage>? onStageStarting = null` — invoked immediately before each of its four existing internal stages (Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation as one "Analysis" stage, then Risk, then Migration, then Reporting); every pre-existing call site (the CLI's own two-argument `Analyze(discovery, cancellationToken)`) is completely unaffected, since the new parameter defaults to `null` and no stage/order/engine changed. This is exactly the "minimal adapter genuinely required for GUI execution" carve-out skill.md's own instructions anticipate — not a business-logic change. One test, `TargetAgnosticismTests` (Analysis.Tests), needed a single, narrowly-scoped exemption: `ScanPipelineRunner` is the one legitimate type in `ServerSleuth.Analysis` whose `DiscoverAsync` method takes a `DiscoveryContext` (it is an orchestration BRIDGE, not one of the pure, target-agnostic computation engines — Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk/Migration/Reporting themselves — that test protects); every one of those engines remains completely unexempted.
- **Credential lifetime (skill.md §3, §16-19)**: `ScanConfigurationViewModel.ScanRequested`'s event-args type widened from `EventHandler<ScanRequest>` to `EventHandler<ScanRequestedEventArgs>` (a new, GUI-local type pairing the credential-free `ScanRequest` with a SEPARATE `ScanCredentialInput` — never merged into one object, preserving GUI-2's own separation) — `MainViewModel` is the only subscriber, and it hands both halves straight to `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start(request, credentials)` synchronously inside the event handler, storing neither anywhere. `ScanConfigurationViewModel` itself now clears its own `_credentials`/`Username` immediately after a successful `StartScan()` (mirroring `Cancel()`'s existing clearing behavior) — the singleton configuration ViewModel never holds a credential any longer than the moment it takes to hand it off. `ScanExecutionViewModel` has exactly ONE constructor dependency, the abstract `IGuiScanExecutor` (mechanically verified) — no field anywhere on it can hold a credential, `Start`'s `credentials` parameter is passed straight through to `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync` and never assigned to a field. `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync(ScanRequest request, ScanCredentialInput credentials, IProgress<ScanProgressState> progress, CancellationToken cancellationToken)` is exactly the signature shape skill.md's own Step 3 suggested. `GuiScanExecutor` converts `ScanCredentialInput` into the real `RemoteCredential`(SSH)/`WindowsRemoteCredential`(WinRM) at the last possible moment inside `DefaultGuiScanComposition`, then never touches it again — the WinRM password, notably, is handed through as the SAME `SecureString` the GUI's `PasswordBox` originally produced (never converted to/from a plain `string` or an environment variable the way the CLI's own `--winrm-password-env` convention requires — a deliberate, disclosed GUI-specific improvement, matching GUI-2's own established WinRM-password reasoning). `ScanExecutionState`/`ScanProgressState`/`ScanCompletionState`/`ScannerProgressInfo` are all added to `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`' checked-type list; `GuiScanExecutorTests` additionally sweeps a sentinel secret through every generated failure message (never surfaced — every user-facing `ErrorMessage` is a FIXED generic string, the raw exception is only ever passed to `ILogger`).
- **Target/transport execution (skill.md §4)**: `DefaultGuiScanComposition.Build` mirrors `ServerSleuth.Cli.Composition.CompositionRoot.Build`+`BuildSshTransport`/`BuildWindowsRemoteTransport` almost line-for-line — `LocalTargetTransport` via the existing `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` for a local target, `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.CreateSsh` for Linux/SSH, `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.CreateWinRm`+`WindowsRemoteTargetTransport` for Windows/WinRM — never a new transport implementation, never SSH.NET/CIM called directly from anywhere `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself compiles. `GuiScanExecutor` connects the transport (`SshRemoteTargetTransport.Connect`/`WindowsRemoteTargetTransport.Connect`) at exactly the same moment `ScanCommand` does — immediately before discovery starts, never at composition time.
- **Progress architecture (skill.md §6)**: reuses the BCL's own `IProgress<T>`/`Progress<T>` contract (no second progress vocabulary invented — nothing in the existing pipeline exposed one to reuse instead, confirmed by inspection). `ScanStage` is a 10-member enum (`Preparing`/`Discovery`/`Analysis`/`RiskAnalysis`/`MigrationAssessment`/`Reporting`/`Export`/`Completed`/`Failed`/`Cancelled`) matching skill.md's own minimum list exactly, with `Analysis`/`RiskAnalysis`/`MigrationAssessment`/`Reporting` sourced directly from `ScanPipelineRunner.Analyze`'s new stage callback (see above) — never a fabricated percentage anywhere; `ScanProgressState` has no percentage field at all, only a stage and (once real data exists) a real `EntityCount`/`ScannerStatuses` list read directly off `AggregateDiscoveryResult.ScannerResults`. The Discovery stage is reported twice — once as it starts (no data yet), once after `DiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` returns (full per-scanner detail) — since the existing `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` exposes no per-scanner streaming callback of its own (a disclosed, inherited limitation, not invented by this phase).
- **A genuine race condition found and fixed during this phase's own test authoring**: `Progress<T>` marshals each `Report` call via whichever `SynchronizationContext` was active when the `Progress<T>` instance was constructed (the WPF `Dispatcher`'s, in the real app; the `ThreadPool`, in a plain test host with none active) — this means a progress report queued just before a scan's completion can still be DELIVERED after `ScanExecutionViewModel`'s own terminal `State = State.WithCompletion(...)` write has already run, since delivery is asynchronous relative to when it was queued. Before the fix, a late-arriving "Discovery" progress report could silently regress `ScanExecutionState.Status` from `Completed` back to `Running` after the scan had already finished — reproduced deterministically via `ScanExecutionViewModelTests`, not merely theorized. Fixed with a one-line guard: the `Progress<ScanProgressState>` callback inside `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start` now checks `!IsFinished` before applying a report — once a terminal state has been reached, a late report is discarded rather than applied. This is a real production correctness fix (a slow scanner's progress report arriving right as a user cancels, or right as discovery completes, could hit the exact same race in the live app), not a test-only workaround.
- **Execution state (skill.md §11)**: `ScanExecutionState` (the ViewModel-bound aggregate — Status/CurrentStage/TargetDisplayName/TargetPlatform/ScannerStatuses/EntityCount/ErrorCount/OutputPaths/StartedAt/FinishedAt/ErrorMessage) contains only non-sensitive data, immutable, `with`-derived via `WithProgress`/`WithCompletion`. `ScanCompletionState` (the terminal snapshot `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync` returns) is a separate, smaller type — never merged with the progress or state types, matching GUI-2's own "separate sensitive/non-sensitive types" precedent even though none of these three types is sensitive (separation here is about SHAPE/purpose, not secrecy).
- **Cancellation (skill.md §8)**: `ScanExecutionViewModel.Cancel()` calls `CancellationTokenSource.Cancel()` — the SAME existing `CancellationToken` architecture every transport/pipeline stage already honors (no new mechanism). Idempotent by construction (`CancellationTokenSource.Cancel()` itself already tolerates repeated calls) — `Cancel_WhileRunning_EventuallyReachesCancelledState_AndCanBeCalledRepeatedly`/`Cancel_BeforeAnyScanStarted_DoesNotThrow` both prove this directly, the latter covering the case skill.md's own "no orphan process" concern implies (cancelling with nothing running must be inert, not throw). `GuiScanExecutorTests.ExecuteAsync_WhenCancelledDuringDiscovery_ReturnsCancelled_AndWritesNoReport` proves cancellation during discovery reaches `ScanExecutionStatus.Cancelled` and never writes a partial report — the existing atomic `LocalFileReportExporter` (temp-file-then-`File.Move`) remains the sole, unmodified authority for "a file either fully exists or doesn't," reused exactly as-is.
- **Concurrent execution protection (skill.md §9)**: `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start` guards on a private `_isRunning` bool — a second `Start` call while one is already running is silently ignored (`Start_CalledAgainWhileRunning_IsIgnored_OnlyOneExecutionOccurs` proves the underlying `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync` is invoked exactly once even when `Start` is called three times in a row).
- **Error handling (skill.md §10)**: every user-facing `ScanCompletionState.ErrorMessage` is one of a small number of FIXED, generic strings — `GuiScanExecutor` never surfaces a raw `Exception.Message` to the caller (it logs the full exception via the existing `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging` infrastructure, matching GUI-1's own `GuiExceptionHandler` convention exactly, then returns a fixed message); `ScanExecutionViewModel.RunAsync`'s own `catch (Exception)` is a last-resort safety net for a hypothetical non-conforming `IGuiScanExecutor` implementation, not the primary error path.
- **UI (skill.md §7, §11)**: `ScanExecutionView.xaml` — target/platform header, current-stage display, per-scanner status list (bound to `State.ScannerStatuses`, populated only once real data exists), an indeterminate `ProgressBar` while running (never a fabricated percentage — `IsIndeterminate="True"` is the only mode used), a completion summary section (status/errors/output file names), and Cancel/Start-New-Scan buttons. `MainViewModel` gained a `ScanExecutionViewModel` constructor dependency and a `_showScanExecution` flag — the SAME `NavigationPage.Scan` nav item now shows either `ScanConfigurationViewModel` or `ScanExecutionViewModel` depending on that flag (never a new, separate nav item), toggled by subscribing to `ScanConfigurationViewModel.ScanRequested` (sets it) and `ScanExecutionViewModel.ReturnToConfigurationRequested` (clears it) — deterministic, no `Dictionary`/`HashSet` navigation state added.
- **Testing (skill.md §13-15)**: 29 new tests total — `ScanExecutionViewModelTests` (11, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests`, against a `FakeGuiScanExecutor`), `GuiScanExecutorTests` (8) + `CliGuiEquivalenceTests` (1) in a new `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests` project (against fake transports/discovery engines — never a real Windows/Linux machine's own scanners, never a real SSH/WinRM connection), plus extensions to `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`/`NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`/`CompositionRootTests`. `CliGuiEquivalenceTests.GuiExecution_AndDirectPipelineInvocation_ProduceEquivalentReports_ForTheSameFixture` feeds the SAME discovery fixture through a direct `ScanPipelineRunner` call (the "CLI-shaped" side) and through the real `GuiScanExecutor`, then asserts the rendered JSON/HTML report content is IDENTICAL modulo the one field genuinely allowed to differ (the generation timestamp, normalized via regex before comparison) — not a fabricated/approximate match.
- **Launch verification (performed, not fabricated)**: `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` rebuilt and confirmed to stay running (no crash) for 3 seconds before deliberate termination, no orphan process — the same bounded-verification method every prior GUI phase used. **No interactive exercise of the Scan Execution flow was performed** (same disclosed environment constraint as GUI-1/GUI-2 — no interactive desktop session available); fixture-based `GuiScanExecutorTests`/`ScanExecutionViewModelTests` provide the actual behavioral coverage.
- **Real-machine validation**: none of this phase's own tests touch a real Windows/Linux machine's scanners or a real SSH/WinRM host — per skill.md's own explicit "do NOT require real remote hosts for ordinary tests" instruction and the existing, still-current disclosed limitation (no authorized live-remote host available in this environment, unchanged since Phase 10D-2). The pre-existing real-machine baseline (CLI local Windows/Linux scans, both completing end-to-end) is unchanged by this phase and was not re-run, since zero scanner/transport/pipeline business logic was modified — only a new orchestration consumer (the GUI) was added around the unmodified pipeline.
- **Regression**: full solution rebuild (0 warnings/errors) and full `dotnet test` confirm **1888/1888 passed** (the pre-existing 1859-test baseline plus 29 new tests across `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests`/the new `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests`), 0 failed/skipped.

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual verification of `ScanExecutionView`'s actual rendered layout was performed (same environment constraint as GUI-1/GUI-2). Discovery progress is reported at only two points (start/finish) rather than per-scanner-as-it-completes, since `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` itself exposes no such callback (a pre-existing, inherited limitation — not something this phase could close without modifying `DiscoveryEngine`'s own contract, out of this phase's "minimal adapter only" scope). "View Results"/GUI-4's actual Results Dashboard was deliberately NOT implemented — `ScanExecutionViewModel.NewScanCommand` only returns to Scan Configuration for now, a placeholder GUI-4 will replace. No genuine live-remote (SSH/WinRM) execution was exercised through the GUI path specifically (the underlying transports themselves remain exhaustively tested from Phase 10D-2/10D-3B onward).

**GUI-4 readiness**: a fully working, tested Scan Execution flow now runs the real pipeline end-to-end from the GUI and produces a `ScanCompletionState` with real entity counts, scanner statuses, and written report file names. A future GUI-4 phase can consume that completion state to build an actual Results Dashboard/report viewer — none of which was started here, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.

## Phase GUI-4 Addendum — Results Dashboard & Result Navigation (as built)

Connects GUI-3's completed `ScanCompletionState` to a real, read-only Results Dashboard —
presentation/inspection only, no second execution of Discovery/Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/
Validation/Risk Analysis/Risk Aggregation/Migration Assessment/Migration Planning/Reporting
anywhere in this phase (skill.md GUI-4's own central rule).

- **Inspection first (skill.md §1)**: confirmed no `ResultsDashboard`/`ScanResultState`/`ViewResults` code existed anywhere before this phase.
