Inspected every domain type the spec named — `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`, `ApplicationRiskSummary`/`ServerRiskSummary`/`RiskFinding`/`RiskAnalysisResult`/`RiskAggregationResult`, `MigrationAssessmentSummary`/`ApplicationMigrationAssessment`/`ServerMigrationAssessment`/`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationDependency`/`MigrationDependencyGroup`, `MigrationPlan`/`MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck`, `AssessmentCoverage`/`CoverageWarning`, and `ScanPipelineResult` — before writing a single new type.
- **The GUI-3 gap this phase closes**: `ScanCompletionState` (GUI-3) carried only counts (`EntityCount`/`ErrorCount`/`ScannerStatuses`/`OutputPaths`) — the real `ScanPipelineResult` `GuiScanExecutor` already computes (`pipelineResult` — Risk Aggregation + the consolidated Migration Assessment Report) was discarded immediately after export. GUI-4 threads that SAME instance through unchanged: `ScanCompletionState.PipelineResult` (new, nullable) ← `GuiScanExecutor`'s existing local variable ← `ScanExecutionState.PipelineResult` (via `WithCompletion`) ← `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s constructor. No recomputation at any hop.
- **`ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` gained a `ServerSleuth.Analysis` reference** (previously Core-only) so `ScanCompletionState` could carry `ScanPipelineResult`. This is NOT a boundary relaxation — `ServerSleuth.Gui` has referenced `ServerSleuth.Analysis` directly since GUI-1 (for `RiskSeverity`), so nothing became reachable from the GUI that wasn't already; `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests` gained two new facts (`GuiContractsAssembly_ReferencesNoPlatformOrScannerOrCliAssembly`/`...ReferencesOnlyCoreAndAnalysis...`) proving `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts.dll` itself still has zero reference to Windows/Linux/Infrastructure/Cli/Reporting.
- **No second DTO layer for the bulk of the data (skill.md §2: "do not duplicate an existing domain model merely to make GUI binding easier")**: `ResultsDashboardViewModel` exposes `ServerSleuth.Analysis` types DIRECTLY wherever a list is already scoped correctly — `IReadOnlyList<MigrationIssue>`/`<MigrationAction>`/`<MigrationVerificationCheck>`/`<MigrationDependency>`/`<MigrationDependencyGroup>`/`<RiskFinding>`/`<CoverageWarning>` bind straight to the report's own collections, never copied, never re-sorted. Only two genuine, thin presentation DTOs were written — `ApplicationRowViewModel`/`ApplicationDetailViewModel` — because the report's own per-application view (`ApplicationMigrationSummary`) carries only the single aggregate `RiskSeverity` enum value, not the full risk/confidence/top-risks/finding-count detail (that lives on a SEPARATE record, `ApplicationRiskSummary`, produced by Risk Aggregation and reachable only via `ScanPipelineResult.Aggregation.Server.ApplicationSummaries`, keyed by boundary Id). These two types perform exactly one join, once, at construction — no calculation beyond `.Count`.
- **Why `DependencyGraph`/`ExternalDependency`/`ApplicationBoundary`/`BoundaryAnalysisResult` (named in the spec's own inspection list) are NOT used directly**: those are Phase 5B/5C intermediate discovery/correlation artifacts, already fully consolidated by Phase 8A/8C into `MigrationDependency`/`MigrationDependencyGroup`/`ApplicationMigrationAssessment` by the time `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` exists — re-reaching past that consolidation would mean re-deriving data Phase 8C already computed, exactly what skill.md's own "never re-derive a value" rule forbids. `AggregateDiscoveryResult` similarly surfaces only as `AssessmentCoverage`/`CoverageWarning`, already consolidated by `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine`.
- **Result lifetime (skill.md §3)**: `MainViewModel` holds one `ResultsDashboardViewModel?` field, built exactly once per `ScanExecutionViewModel.ViewResultsRequested` (a new event, alongside the unchanged `ReturnToConfigurationRequested`), and reused for every subsequent navigation to `NavigationPage.Results` — switching tabs, reopening the dashboard, and selecting/deselecting an application never rebuild it (verified behaviorally by `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests`, which asserts `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync` is called exactly once across a full "start scan → view results → switch through every other nav page → return to Results" sequence). Never stores a credential — `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s only input is `ScanExecutionState`, itself already GUI-3's own credential-free type.
- **Result navigation (skill.md §4)**: extends the SAME `_showScanExecution`-style deterministic bool-flag mechanism GUI-3 established, applied to `NavigationPage.Results` (a pre-existing GUI-1 placeholder nav item) instead of a new page — `ApplyCurrentPage` shows `_resultsDashboardViewModel` when non-null, the original placeholder otherwise (so a session with no completed scan yet still shows "Discovered entities and their relationships will appear here.", never a fabricated empty dashboard). `ScanExecutionView.xaml` gained a "View Results" button next to the pre-existing "Start New Scan" — both routed through `MainViewModel`, no second navigation system.
- **Dashboard composition (skill.md §5-16)**: `ResultsDashboardView.xaml` — Scan Summary card (target/platform/status/duration/entities/errors/coverage), Risk Summary card (Critical/High/Medium/Low/Informational counts read verbatim from `RiskAggregationResult.Server`, no invented score), Migration Summary card (Blocked/NeedsRemediation/ReadyWithConditions/Ready counts from `ServerMigrationSummary`), Dependency Summary card (grouped by `MigrationDependencyType`, from `Report.Dependencies`), an Applications `ListView` (filterable/searchable, presentation-only), a selected-application detail panel (`ApplicationDetailView`, via a local `DataTemplate`), and five `Expander`-collapsed sections (Risk Findings/Migration Issues/Migration Actions/Verification Checks/Scanner Status) plus a Reports section — matching the spec's own suggested layout. No third-party UI/chart library; native WPF `Border`/`Expander`/`ListView`/`GridView`/`WrapPanel` only.
- **Risk visualization (skill.md §17)**: `AggregateSeverityBrushConverter` (new) maps the five real `AggregateSeverity` members onto the SAME `Risk.Info`/`Risk.Low`/`Risk.Medium`/`Risk.High`/`Risk.Critical` brushes `Theme.xaml` already declared for `RiskSeverity` in GUI-1 (identical names by design); `AggregateSeverity.None` falls back to the existing, already-neutral `App.MutedForeground` resource rather than inventing a "Risk.None" brush that has no `RiskSeverity` counterpart. `RiskSeverityBrushConverter` — declared in GUI-1 but unused by any View until now — is finally wired into `ApplicationDetailView`'s Top Risks list, per skill.md §17's own "use it meaningfully or remove it" instruction.
- **Filtering/sorting (skill.md §18)**: `SearchText`/`SeverityFilter`/`OnlyWithIssues` on `ResultsDashboardViewModel` recompute `FilteredApplications` as a fresh `.ToList()` projection — `Applications` (and every domain record any row wraps) is never reordered, filtered-in-place, or mutated; proven by `ResultsDashboardDeterminismAndMutationTests`, including a report-JSON-serialization equality check taken before and after construction/filtering.
- **Empty/partial results (skill.md §19)**: `ScanCompletionState.PipelineResult` stays `null` for `Cancelled`/`Failed` scans (analysis never reached a report); `ResultsDashboardViewModel.HasResults`/`HasNoResults` drive an explicit "No results are available for this scan yet." banner, and every individual list section carries its own explicit empty-state text ("No application findings.", "No migration issues detected.", "No external dependencies detected.", "No report files were written.") — never a blank table indistinguishable from "still loading."
- **Security boundary (skill.md §20)**: `ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests` performs BOTH the name-based sweep (extends `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`' existing checked-type list with the three new Results types) AND a structural, type-shape sweep — including a reflective walk of every `ServerSleuth.*`-namespaced type reachable from `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s own public property graph, confirming none is `SecureString`-typed or credential/token/secret-named by either its declared type name or its property name. `EvidenceRecord`'s own doc comment already establishes that redaction happens at CONSTRUCTION time (Phase 2's `SecretRedactor`, never at display time) — GUI-4 displays `EvidenceRecord.Location`/`Detail` unmodified, inheriting that guarantee rather than re-implementing it.
- **No pipeline re-execution (skill.md §21)**: `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests` extended — `ResultsDashboardViewModel`/`ApplicationDetailViewModel`/`ApplicationRowViewModel` added to the reflected-over type list; `RiskAggregator`/`MigrationPlanEngine` added to the forbidden-type-name list (alongside the pre-existing `DiscoveryEngine`/`ScanPipelineRunner`/`DiscoveryScannerRegistry`/`RiskRuleEngine`/`MigrationAssessmentEngine`/`ReportArtifactFactory`) — none of the three new types' constructors/fields/methods reference any of them.
- **No mutation / determinism (skill.md §22-23)**: `ResultsDashboardDeterminismAndMutationTests` proves repeated `ResultsDashboardViewModel` construction over the SAME `ScanExecutionState` produces identical application/risk/dependency ordering across five separate constructions, that filtering/selecting never reorders or shrinks the master `Applications` list, and that two independently-constructed dashboards over the same state never share selection state.
- **Performance (skill.md §26)**: a hand-built fixture (`ScanResultFixtureFactory`, since `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` cannot reference Windows/Linux/Infrastructure to run a real pipeline) generates 1,000 applications × (10 findings, 5 dependencies, 10 actions, 20 checks) = 10,000/5,000/10,000/20,000 totals; `ResultsDashboardViewModel` construction over that fixture is measured (via `Stopwatch`, not estimated) at well under 10 seconds, and filtering the resulting 1,000-row application list at well under 1 second. Only the ViewModel's OWN transformation cost is timed — fixture authoring itself sits outside the measured window, and no pipeline stage runs at any point.
- **Real-machine validation (skill.md §27)**: **not performed** — this phase, like every prior GUI phase, ran in an environment with no interactive Windows desktop session available. `dotnet build` succeeded for the WPF `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` (proving XAML/`DataTemplate`/`StaticResource` references all resolve — a `x:Type`/resource-key error would fail the build, not merely misrender), and the same bounded `Start-Process`/3-second-survival/`Stop-Process` launch check every prior GUI phase used was repeated, confirming no startup exception from the new `DataTemplate`/converter registrations. No interactive exercise of "click View Results, select an application, inspect its detail, return to the dashboard" was performed or is claimed — the equivalent coverage instead comes from `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests`/`ResultsDashboardViewModelTests`/`ApplicationDetailViewModelTests`, all fixture-based.
- **Dependencies (skill.md §29)**: zero new NuGet packages. `Dashboard.Card`/`Dashboard.SectionTitle` are plain `Style` resources; `Expander`/`ListView`/`GridView`/`WrapPanel` are all built-in WPF controls.

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual verification of `ResultsDashboardView`'s actual rendered layout was performed (same environment constraint as every prior GUI phase). `ScanCompletionState.PipelineResult` stays `null` even for a report-EXPORT failure (the pipeline itself completed, but `GuiScanExecutor`'s early-return path on export failure was left unchanged, since threading the result through that specific branch was judged out of this phase's minimal-wiring scope — a future phase could reconsider this if "view partial results despite an export failure" becomes a real requirement). Dependency filtering by boundary/confidence and a dedicated "search within Migration Issues/Actions" control were not added (only the Applications table has a search/filter row, matching the spec's own worked example); a future phase could extend filtering to the other sections using the identical presentation-only pattern already established here. A pre-existing, environmental GUI-3 test-timing flake was observed (see PROGRESS.md/CHANGELOG.md for detail) — not fixed, since it is outside this phase's scope and the final regression run was clean.

**GUI-5 readiness**: a fully working, tested Results Dashboard now displays real risk/migration/dependency/scanner/report data end-to-end from a completed scan, with a working Applications table, application-detail drill-down, and presentation-only filtering. A future GUI-5 phase could build on this — e.g. dependency graph visualization, richer analytics, or report export controls — none of which was started here, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction; STOP after GUI-4 per the explicit instruction that authorized this phase.

## Phase GUI-5 Addendum — Production GUI Completion & Export (as built)

Adds a Report Export UI and a Report Viewer to the GUI-4 Results Dashboard, plus a dashboard-level
"New Scan" action — presentation/orchestration only, reusing the existing `ReportArtifactFactory`/
`IReportExporter`/`LocalFileReportExporter` exactly as `GuiScanExecutor` already did, never a
second export/reporting implementation, never a new discovery/analysis/migration/reporting engine.

- **Existing-architecture inspection first (skill.md §1)**: confirmed the established GUI dependency boundary from GUI-3's/GUI-4's own `.csproj` comments and `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests` before writing anything — `ServerSleuth.Gui` may reference only `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis`/`ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts`/`ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost`; only `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` may reference `ServerSleuth.Reporting`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`. `GuiScanExecutor`'s own end-of-scan `ExportReport` private method was inspected and found to already call `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle`/`LocalFileReportExporter.Export` — the exact real API this phase needed to reach a second time, from the Results Dashboard, on demand.
- **Export boundary (skill.md §1-2, §9)**: `IGuiReportExportService` (new, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts`) is the SAME composition/execution-boundary pattern `IGuiScanExecutor` already established in GUI-3 — its real implementation, `GuiReportExportService` (new, `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost`), is the only place this phase touches `ReportArtifactFactory`/`LocalFileReportExporter` directly. `GuiScanExecutor`'s own end-of-scan export was REFACTORED (not duplicated) to call the same shared `GuiReportExportService.ExportReport` static method the Results Dashboard's on-demand export now also calls — one export implementation, two call sites, exactly like `ReportExportRunner`/`GuiScanExecutor` already shared logic conceptually before this phase gave it one literal shared method. `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` needed no new project reference for this — `IGuiReportExportService` only touches `ServerSleuth.Analysis` (`ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`, already an allowed reference since GUI-4) and its own new `ServerSleuth.Gui.Models` types.
- **Report Viewer boundary (skill.md §2)**: deliberately NOT routed through the `Gui.ExecutionHost` boundary — `IGuiReportViewerService`/`GuiReportViewerService` (both new, in `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself) do a single, defensive `File.ReadAllText` over a file the same completed scan already wrote to a directory the user themselves chose. This is a local file read, not a Reporting/Infrastructure/Windows/Linux concern (no scanner, registry, process, network, or renderer is invoked), so routing it through `ExecutionHost` would have added an unnecessary hop without closing any real dependency gap; `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`' existing "`ServerSleuth.Gui` references only Core+Analysis+Gui.Contracts+Gui.ExecutionHost among `ServerSleuth.*` assemblies" fact still holds unchanged, since `System.IO.File`/`Path` are BCL, not a `ServerSleuth.*` assembly. Defense-in-depth path validation (rejects `..`, rooted paths, invalid filename characters) guards against ever reading outside the caller-supplied output directory even though the only real caller only ever passes an already-exporter-validated file name.
- **Both new service interfaces are OPTIONAL constructor parameters** (default `null`) on `ResultsDashboardViewModel` and `MainViewModel` — every one of GUI-4's ~30 existing `new ResultsDashboardViewModel(state)`/two `new MainViewModel(...)` test call sites kept compiling completely unchanged; `CompositionRoot`'s real, DI-composed instance always supplies both. A dashboard built without them simply has `ExportReportCommand`/`OpenReportCommand.CanExecute` return `false` (disabled, not throwing) — the same "degrade gracefully rather than throw" discipline GUI-4 already used for a `null` `PipelineResult`.
- **Export flow (skill.md §1, §4)**: `ExportFormat`/`ExportOverwritePolicy`/`ExportDirectory` are freely-editable bindable properties (directory defaults to the SAME `ScanExecutionState.OutputDirectory` the completed scan already wrote to — a new, non-sensitive field threaded through `ScanExecutionState`/`ScanRequest.OutputDirectory` via an optional second parameter on `ScanExecutionState.StartingFor`, again backward-compatible with every existing single-argument call site). `ExportReportCommand` invokes `IGuiReportExportService.Export` with the report instance already sitting on the dashboard (never re-fetched, never a copy) and publishes whatever `GuiReportExportResult` the service returns — `LastExportResult`/`LastExportedFileNamesText` are bound directly; nothing here ever sets `Success = true` except by copying the service's own answer (verified by `ResultsDashboardExportAndViewerTests`' "never fabricates success/failure" tests).
- **Report Viewer flow (skill.md §2)**: `SelectedReportFileName` (defaults to the first entry in `ReportFileNames`, itself unchanged from GUI-4) plus `OpenReportCommand` invoking `IGuiReportViewerService.ReadReportFile` and publishing the exact `GuiReportViewResult` returned into `ReportViewResult`, bound to a read-only, monospace, scrollable `TextBox` — the SAME raw text for both JSON and HTML (no second JSON deserialization into a display model, no HTML rendering/JavaScript execution engine). This is a deliberate, disclosed scope decision — see Known Limitations.
- **Dashboard integration/navigation (skill.md §3)**: a "New Scan" button was added directly to `ResultsDashboardView.xaml` (`ResultsDashboardViewModel.NewScanCommand`/`NewScanRequested` event, new) alongside the existing GUI-3 "View Results"/"Start New Scan" buttons on the Scan Execution page — both routes now go through one shared `MainViewModel.GoToScanConfiguration()` private method (a refactor of the GUI-3 `ReturnToConfigurationRequested` handler, not a second implementation), which itself still only calls the existing `NavigationService.NavigateTo`/`ApplyCurrentPage` — no second navigation system. "View Application" was already satisfied by GUI-4's existing `SelectApplicationCommand`; "Export Report"/"Open Report" are the two new dashboard-level actions this phase adds.
- **Export state / error handling (skill.md §4-5)**: `GuiReportExportResult`/`GuiReportExportFailureReason` (new, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts`) are a presentation-layer mirror of `ReportExportResult`/`ReportBundleExportResult` — deliberately NOT the Reporting types themselves, so `ServerSleuth.Gui` never needs a `ServerSleuth.Reporting` reference to bind to an export outcome. `GuiReportExportService.Export` classifies a `LocalFileReportExporter` diagnostic ("...already exists...") into `GuiReportExportFailureReason.AlreadyExists` vs. a generic `WriteFailed`, and returns one of two FIXED, generic user-facing messages — never the raw diagnostic/exception text — matching the same "concise, generic, credential-free" discipline `GuiExceptionHandler`/`GuiScanExecutor`'s own generic export-failure message already established in GUI-1/GUI-3.
- **Scan result integrity / no mutation (skill.md §6, §22)**: `ExecuteExport`/`ExecuteOpenReport` only ever pass the existing `Report`/`State.OutputDirectory` BY REFERENCE into the service call — neither method assigns into any Analysis-layer record. `ResultsDashboardExportAndViewerTests.ExportReportCommand_NeverMutatesTheReport` asserts reference identity and an unchanged `ApplicationAssessments.Count` before/after an export call; `GuiReportExportServiceTests.Export_NeverMutatesTheReportItWasHandled` proves the same at the service layer, against the REAL `ReportArtifactFactory`/`LocalFileReportExporter`.
- **No re-execution (skill.md §7)**: `GuiReportExportServiceArchitectureTests` (new, `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests`) reflects over `GuiReportExportService` confirming no field/method involves `DiscoveryEngine`/`ScanPipelineRunner`/`DiscoveryScannerRegistry`/`RiskRuleEngine`/`MigrationAssessmentEngine`/`RiskAggregator`/`MigrationPlanEngine` (a narrower forbidden list than `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`' own — this class IS allowed to reference `ReportArtifactFactory`, unlike anything in `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself). `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests` gained two new behavioral tests — exporting JSON then HTML, and opening a report, both from a real dashboard built through `MainViewModel` — each asserting `FakeGuiScanExecutor.Calls.Count` stays at exactly 1 (the one original scan) throughout.
- **Determinism (skill.md §8)**: `GuiReportExportServiceTests.Export_CalledTwiceWithTheSameReportAndOverwrite_ProducesByteIdenticalOutput_Deterministic` re-confirms `JsonReportRenderer`/`HtmlReportRenderer`'s existing opt-in-only `generatedAt` behavior (both default to `null`, and `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle` never passes one) means two exports of the same report produce byte-identical files — no random ID was added anywhere in this phase.
- **Credential isolation (skill.md §10)**: `GuiReportExportResult`/`GuiReportViewResult` were added to both `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`' name-based sweep and `ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests`' type-shape sweep (they were already transitively covered by the latter's existing reachable-graph walk from `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s own public properties — added explicitly too, for a direct per-type guarantee). Neither type, nor `IGuiReportExportService`/`IGuiReportViewerService`'s signatures, carries anything password/credential/token/secret-shaped; the only string-typed values involved (`outputDirectory`, `fileName`) are the same class of already-user-supplied, non-secret path values `ScanConfigurationState`'s own SSH-metadata properties established as safe in GUI-2.
- **Partial/failed/cancelled/empty handling (skill.md §11)**: `ExportReportCommand`/`OpenReportCommand`'s `CanExecute` already returns `false` whenever `Report is null` (cancelled/failed scans, per GUI-4's existing `HasNoResults` case) or no service was supplied — verified by dedicated tests; no placeholder export/view result is ever fabricated for these cases.
- **Performance (skill.md §13)**: `ResultsDashboardPerformanceTests.ExportAndOpenReport_OverALargeResult_CompleteWellUnderOneSecond` reuses GUI-4's existing 1,000-application/10,000-finding/5,000-dependency/10,000-action/20,000-check fixture and times one export call plus one open-report call (against fakes, proving the VIEWMODEL orchestration itself is O(1) — it hands the whole already-computed report reference to the service with no per-item loop of its own) at well under 1 second.
- **Real-machine validation (skill.md §15)**: **not performed** — same environment constraint as every prior GUI phase (no interactive Windows desktop session available). `dotnet build` succeeded for `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` (0 warnings/errors), and the same bounded `Start-Process`/liveness/`Stop-Process` check every prior phase used was repeated, explicitly NOT reported as visual validation. All GUI-5 verification is fixture/automated-test-based.
- **Dependencies (skill.md §16, §29)**: zero new NuGet packages. Two new WPF value converters (`CountToVisibilityConverter`/`InverseBoolToVisibilityConverter`) follow the exact existing `CountToInverseVisibilityConverter`/`BoolToVisibility` pattern from GUI-2/GUI-4 — no third-party library.
- **Explicitly not implemented (skill.md §16's own list)**: no migration/verification execution, no remote command execution, no HTTP API, no cloud/email upload, no credential vault, no Kubernetes control, no new scanners/protocols, no PowerShell/shell execution channel — the Report Viewer never executes HTML/JavaScript (it displays raw text only, see Known Limitations), and there is no "Execute"/"Run" affordance anywhere in this phase's UI, matching GUI-4's own declarative-only discipline for Actions/Verification Checks.

**Known Limitations**: the Report Viewer displays raw file TEXT for both JSON and HTML — there is no WYSIWYG HTML rendering (that would require a `WebView2`/similar third-party control, explicitly out of scope per "do not introduce third-party packages"/"no new NuGet packages unless justified," and the spec's own §2 already anticipates a script-free HTML report needs no script-execution surface at all). No interactive/visual verification was performed (same constraint as every prior GUI phase). Export always re-renders JSON/HTML from the CURRENT `Report` instance via `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle` (cheap, in-memory, deterministic) rather than reusing bytes from the original end-of-scan export — this matches `ReportExportRunner`'s own existing pattern (render is never treated as expensive/stateful) and lets a later export pick up a different `filePrefix`/directory without needing to have cached the original bundle. A pre-existing, environmental `ScanExecutionViewModelTests.Start_CalledAgainWhileRunning_IsIgnored_OnlyOneExecutionOccurs` timing flake was observed under parallel test-run load (passes reliably in isolation) — not fixed, since it is unrelated to this phase's changes (a GUI-3 concurrency-timing test) and the isolated/final regression run was clean.

**GUI-6 readiness**: the Results Dashboard now supports a full inspect → export → view-report → start-a-new-scan loop end-to-end from a completed scan, entirely through the existing Reporting APIs. A future phase could add a WYSIWYG HTML report viewer (via an approved third-party control), dependency-graph visualization, or richer per-section filtering — none of which was started here, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction; STOP after GUI-5 per the explicit instruction that authorized this phase.

## Phase GUI-6 Addendum — Final GUI Production Readiness & Acceptance Hardening (as built)

A HARDENING/AUDIT phase, not a feature-development phase — the directive itself called for
verifying existing behavior with tests over writing new production code, fixing only genuine
defects with the smallest correct change. This phase's own audit of the full GUI-1 through GUI-5
implementation (`MainViewModel`, `ScanExecutionViewModel`, `ResultsDashboardViewModel`,
`ApplicationDetailViewModel`, `GuiScanExecutor`, `GuiReportExportService`, `GuiReportViewerService`,
`NavigationService`, `GuiExceptionHandler`) found **no genuine production defect** — every UX-state
guard section 7 asked about (Start Scan disabled while running, export/viewer `CanExecute` behavior
with no completed result, New Scan/Cancel consistency, no stale-result exposure, no fabricated
success on failure/cancellation) was already correctly implemented and already covered by GUI-3/
GUI-4/GUI-5's own extensive test suite. **Zero production `.cs` files were modified this phase** —
every change below is additive test coverage that closes a specific, named gap the GUI-6 spec called
out but the existing suite did not yet directly exercise.

- **Full workflow verification (skill.md §1)**: re-confirmed by inspection, not rewritten — `MainViewModel` still routes every transition (Scan Configuration → Start Scan → Scan Execution → Results Dashboard → Application Detail → Export/Open Report → New Scan) through the single existing `NavigationService`/`GoToScanConfiguration()` mechanism GUI-3/GUI-5 already established; no second navigation system exists anywhere in the assembly (`NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`, `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`, and manual inspection all agree).
- **No re-execution (skill.md §2)**: `MainViewModel` was added to `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`' reflection sweep (`CheckedViewModelTypes`) — the shell ViewModel that wires the whole workflow together now gets the same constructor/field/method orchestration-type sweep every other GUI ViewModel already had, closing the one type in the workflow that wasn't yet directly swept. The existing `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests` behavioral suite (start once, Results→Detail→Results, export JSON/HTML/Both, open report, New Scan — each asserting `FakeGuiScanExecutor.Calls.Count` stays at exactly 1) was inspected and found already complete; no new behavioral test was needed there.
- **Result-state handling (skill.md §3)**: `ScanResultFixtureFactory.Options` gained two new, additive fields — `ServerLevelIssueCount` and `SharedInfrastructureCount` — so the fixture can now build a report with genuine server-scoped issues (`AffectedBoundaryIds = []`, distinct from any application's own `Issues`) and genuine shared-infrastructure dependencies (`AffectedBoundaryIds` spanning every application boundary the fixture built), closing the two named scenarios ("server-only findings," "shared infrastructure findings") the existing fixture could not previously represent (it always produced empty `ServerLevelIssues`/`SharedInfrastructure` lists). Two new tests in `ResultsDashboardViewModelTests` (`ServerLevelIssues_AreExposedSeparately_AndNeverAttributedToAnApplication`, `SharedInfrastructure_AttributedAcrossApplications_IsExposedAsOneEntry_NeverDuplicated`) verify the dashboard surfaces both correctly and never double-attributes a server-level issue into any single application's own issue list. Every other named scenario (success/partial/failed/cancelled/zero-entity/zero-application/Blocked/NeedsRemediation/ReadyWithConditions/export-failure/report-not-found/invalid-path/FailIfExists/Overwrite) was already directly covered by GUI-4/GUI-5's existing `ResultsDashboardViewModelTests`/`ResultsDashboardExportAndViewerTests`/`GuiReportExportServiceTests`/`GuiReportViewerServiceTests` suites — inspected and confirmed, not duplicated.
- **Remote transport failure (skill.md §3)**: added `ExecuteAsync_ForARemoteTarget_WhenTheTransportFailsToConnect_ReturnsFailed_WithAGenericMessage_AndNoPipelineResult` to `GuiScanExecutorTests` — a `ScanTarget.Remote(...)` target whose composition callback throws a connect-shaped exception, confirming the exact same generic `Failed(...)` shape, no leaked host/error text, and no fabricated `PipelineResult` that the existing local-target composition-failure tests already proved for the local case. `GuiScanExecutor`'s connect/composition-failure handling is target-kind-agnostic (the try/catch around the composition callback does not branch on `Target.Kind`), so this closes the one explicitly-named scenario (remote transport failure) that was previously only implicitly covered.
- **ViewModel state safety / no mutation / determinism (skill.md §4)**: re-confirmed by inspection — `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s constructor and every property already project the existing `ScanPipelineResult`/`ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` fields by reference or thin one-way wrapper, never copying into a writable field; `ResultsDashboardDeterminismAndMutationTests` (GUI-4/GUI-5) already asserts repeated construction and filtering never reorder or mutate the master `Applications`/`Report` state. No gap found; no change made.
- **Credential security (skill.md §5)**: re-confirmed — `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests` already cover every GUI-5 type (`GuiReportExportResult`, `GuiReportViewResult`) by both name-based sweep and reflective type-shape/reachable-graph walk; `GuiScanExecutorTests.ExecuteAsync_WhenCompositionThrowsWithASensitiveMessage_NeverLeaksItToTheCaller` already proves a credential-shaped sentinel string never survives into a user-visible `ErrorMessage`. No gap found; no change made.
- **Error handling (skill.md §6)**: re-confirmed — `GuiExceptionHandler` is the one exception boundary in the assembly (verified by inspection: no second `catch`-and-publish-to-state pattern exists anywhere else), and it deliberately never binds `Exception.Message`/`StackTrace` into application state, publishing only the fixed `GenericUserFacingMessage` constant. No gap found; no change made.
- **UX state hardening (skill.md §7)**: audited line-by-line against every bullet in this section — `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start` already guards re-entrancy with `_isRunning` (a second `Start` call while running is a silent no-op); the Scan page slot shows either configuration or execution, never both, so a user cannot even see a "Start Scan" affordance while a scan is running; `ExportReportCommand`/`OpenReportCommand`'s `CanExecute` already correctly returns `false` with no service, no report, or a blank directory; `NewScanCommand`/`CancelCommand`'s `CanExecute` already gate on `!_isRunning`/`_isRunning` respectively; `_resultsDashboardViewModel` is only ever assigned inside the `ViewResultsRequested` handler (built once per actual completed scan, never reused across a different scan's data — a genuinely new scan gets a genuinely new dashboard instance); failed/cancelled scans carry `Report = null` so `HasResults`/`HasNoResults` correctly gate the dashboard into its explicit empty state rather than ever rendering fabricated success. No defect found in any of these; no production change made.
- **Report viewer / export architecture (skill.md §11-12)**: re-confirmed unchanged from GUI-5 — the viewer remains raw-text-only for both JSON and HTML (no `WebView2`, no HTML-rendering control, no script execution path anywhere in `ServerSleuth.Gui`'s `.csproj` or source), and export still only ever calls `GuiReportExportService.ExportReport` → `ReportArtifactFactory`/`LocalFileReportExporter` (no `File.WriteAllText`/`WriteAllBytes`/`StreamWriter` anywhere near report content). No gap found; no change made.
- **Large-result presentation (skill.md §8)**: re-confirmed — `ResultsDashboardPerformanceTests.Construction_OverALargeResult_CompletesWellUnderTenSeconds` already exercises exactly the spec's target shape (1,000 applications × 10 findings/5 dependencies/10 actions/20 checks = 10,000/5,000/10,000/20,000) and, because `ApplicationRowViewModel` eagerly builds its `ApplicationDetailViewModel` for every row at construction time, this single test already measures dashboard-plus-detail construction together, not dashboard alone. No new performance test was needed.
- **Security architecture (skill.md §10)**: re-confirmed — `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests` already asserts, at the assembly-reference level, that `ServerSleuth.Gui`/`ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` reference only `Core`+`Analysis` (plus `Gui.Contracts`/`Gui.ExecutionHost` for the `Gui` project itself) among `ServerSleuth.*` assemblies, and zero third-party network/remote-transport packages. No gap found; no change made.
- **Real-machine CLI/pipeline smoke check (skill.md §15)**: not performed — no backend/pipeline code was touched this phase (every change was GUI-side test code), so there is nothing a backend smoke test could have caught that a full `dotnet test` regression would not already catch.
- **Interactive validation (skill.md §9)**: **not performed** — same environment constraint as GUI-4A and GUI-5 (no interactive Windows desktop session available in this environment; only Chrome browser automation exists, inapplicable to WPF). A non-visual liveness check was repeated (`dotnet build` succeeded, 0 warnings/errors; `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` launched via `Start-Process`, stayed alive 3 seconds, terminated cleanly with no orphan process) — explicitly NOT reported as visual validation, per this phase's own explicit instruction that `Start-Process` + liveness alone does not constitute it.
- **Tests added (skill.md §13)**: 6 new tests total — 1 in `GuiScanExecutorTests` (remote transport failure), 2 in `ResultsDashboardViewModelTests` (server-level issues, shared infrastructure), and 3 additional `[Theory]` instances from adding `MainViewModel` to `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`' existing 3-method reflection sweep. Zero existing tests were deleted, weakened, or skipped.
- **Regression (skill.md §14)**: full solution `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test` — **2001/2001 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (the pre-existing 1995-test GUI-5 baseline plus these 6 additive tests). One run during this phase's own work showed `ScanExecutionViewModelTests.Start_WhenExecutorThrows_ProducesAFailedState_WithAGenericMessage` fail under parallel-test-run load; it passed cleanly both in isolation and on a full clean re-run immediately after — the same pre-existing, environmental, GUI-3-era concurrency-timing flake already disclosed and left unmodified in the GUI-5 addendum above (unrelated to any GUI-6 change, since no GUI-6 change touched `ScanExecutionViewModel`/`FakeGuiScanExecutor`).

**Known Limitations**: identical to GUI-5's own list (raw-text-only report viewer, no interactive/visual verification possible in this environment, export always re-renders from the current in-memory `Report` rather than caching original export bytes) — nothing changed this phase. The pre-existing `ScanExecutionViewModelTests` parallel-run timing flake (see Regression above) remains open and undiagnosed beyond "environmental, passes in isolation, passes on a clean full run" — a genuine root-cause fix was judged out of GUI-6's own "smallest correct change only where a genuine defect is found" scope, since the ViewModel/executor code involved is unmodified GUI-3-era code, not something this phase touched.

**Final product readiness**: the full Scan Configuration → Start Scan → Scan Execution/Progress → Scan Completion → Results Dashboard → Application Detail → Export Report → Open Report → New Scan workflow is implemented, exercised end-to-end by fixture/behavioral tests, and structurally guaranteed (by reflection/architecture tests) to never re-run the pipeline, never leak a credential or exception detail, never mutate a backend result, and never reference a forbidden platform/scanner/CLI/reporting assembly directly. The one gap between this and a fully "release-ready" claim is that **no genuine interactive/visual validation has ever been performed** on this GUI, in any phase, because no environment with that capability has been available — this is disclosed here as plainly as in every prior phase, not glossed over. Per this phase's own explicit "FINAL STOP" instruction: **no GUI-7 or further GUI phase was started.**

## Phase 11A Addendum — Cross-Platform Release Packaging (as built)

A packaging-only phase — no application functionality was added. The objective was a single,
reproducible release-build mechanism producing ready-to-distribute self-contained single-file
executables for Windows x64 and Linux x64, from the two entry points that already existed:
`ServerSleuth.Gui` (WPF, Windows-only by GUI-1's own decision record above) and `ServerSleuth.Cli`
(multi-targeted `net8.0-windows;net8.0`, with its `net8.0` build being the one that ever ships on
Linux — see Phase 10A's decision record).

- **Release script (skill.md §3)**: `build-release.ps1` at the repo root. Validates the `dotnet` SDK is on `PATH`, cleans `dist/`, publishes both platforms, verifies both artifacts exist and are non-empty, prints paths/sizes/file counts, writes `dist/SHA256SUMS.txt`, and returns exit code 1 on any publish failure (`$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'` plus explicit `$LASTEXITCODE` checks after each `dotnet publish` — failures are never swallowed).
- **Publish configuration (skill.md §2, §5, §11)**: Windows publishes `src/ServerSleuth.Gui/ServerSleuth.Gui.csproj` as `win-x64`, self-contained, `PublishSingleFile=true`, `IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract=true` (required — the Windows build transitively pulls in `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure`'s native MI.dll via `ServerSleuth.Windows`). Linux publishes `src/ServerSleuth.Cli/ServerSleuth.Cli.csproj`'s `net8.0` TFM (`-f net8.0`, explicit — the project multi-targets) as `linux-x64`, self-contained, `PublishSingleFile=true`, `IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract=true` (SSH.NET's native dependencies). `PublishTrimmed` was deliberately left off for both, per the phase's own explicit instruction that correctness outranks executable size and trimming was not proven safe across the whole dependency graph.
- **Artifact naming (skill.md §6)**: **`-p:AssemblyName=<name>` was tried first and rejected** — MSBuild properties passed via `-p:` on the command line apply to the entire build graph, including every `ProjectReference`, so overriding `AssemblyName` globally gave every referenced project (`Core`, `Analysis`, `Infrastructure`, `Windows`, `Gui.Contracts`, `Gui.ExecutionHost`) the same assembly name simultaneously and produced a NuGet restore error ("Ambiguous project name 'ServerSleuth'"). The actual mechanism is a **post-publish rename in the script**: publish to an intermediate staging directory under `obj/release-publish-staging/` (kept out of `dist/` so intermediate build output never mixes with release artifacts), then copy-and-rename the one real executable — `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` → `ServerSleuth.exe` on Windows, `serversleuth` → `ServerSleuth` on Linux — into the final `dist/ServerSleuth-{Windows,Linux}-x64/` folder. Neither project's actual `<AssemblyName>` was changed; `dotnet build`/`dotnet test`'s normal Debug-configuration behavior is unaffected (re-confirmed: 0 warnings/errors on `dotnet build`, 2001/2001 on `dotnet test`, both unchanged from the pre-Phase-11A baseline).
- **Single-file verification (skill.md §10)**: both `dist/` output folders contain exactly one file. Per-project `.pdb` debug-symbol files remain in the intermediate staging directories (normal .NET single-file publish behavior — debug symbols are never embedded into a single-file executable) but are never copied into `dist/`, so they do not appear in the shipped artifact.
- **Real artifact acceptance (skill.md §7, §8, §9, §17)** — genuinely executed, not assumed from `PublishSingleFile=true` appearing in a project file:
  - **Windows**: the actual `dist/ServerSleuth-Windows-x64/ServerSleuth.exe` (not the repo's own `bin/`/`obj/` build) was launched via `Start-Process`, confirmed alive after 4 seconds, and terminated cleanly with no orphan process — twice: once in place, once after being copied alone into an isolated `%TEMP%` directory containing nothing else (confirming no dependency on the source tree, `bin/`/`obj/`, NuGet cache, or the .NET SDK). **No interactive/visual verification was performed or claimed** — this environment has no Windows desktop UI automation capability, the same constraint disclosed in every GUI-4A/GUI-5/GUI-6 addendum above.
  - **Linux**: WSL2 (Ubuntu) was genuinely available in this environment and was used for real execution — the actual `dist/ServerSleuth-Linux-x64/ServerSleuth` binary was copied into an isolated `/tmp` directory inside WSL, `chmod +x`'d, and run there. `./ServerSleuth --help`, `./ServerSleuth --version` (`10.0.0.0`), and `./ServerSleuth scan --help` all produced the expected output. A genuine local scan (`./ServerSleuth scan --output ./local-scan-report --verbose`) was then run against the WSL machine itself: real discovery (1,637 entities across 11 scanners), real risk/migration analysis, and real `report.json`/`report.html` output, exit code 0 — all from the isolated temp directory, with no repo/SDK/NuGet-cache path involved. This is a materially stronger verification than the GUI's, and is reported as such rather than flattened to the same "not performed" caveat.
- **Security (skill.md §12)**: `build-release.ps1` only invokes `dotnet publish` against the two existing project files and local filesystem operations (`Copy-Item`, `Remove-Item`, `Get-FileHash`) — it downloads nothing, executes no downloaded script, and does not touch PATH/registry/SSH configuration/credentials/telemetry. Confirmed by inspection of the script's own (complete, short) source.
- **Version (skill.md §13)**: not changed — both projects already carried `<Version>10.0.0</Version>` before this phase (`ServerSleuth.Cli`'s `VersionInfo.cs` reads it from the assembly, a single source of truth per Phase 10A's own decision record above); the published Linux binary's `--version` output (`10.0.0.0`) was used as real confirmation this holds through a self-contained publish, not just a normal build.
- **Checksums (skill.md §14)**: `dist/SHA256SUMS.txt`, generated with PowerShell's built-in `Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256` — no third-party tooling.
- **Documentation (skill.md §15)**: README.md gained a "發布 / 下載即用版本" section with real, verified Windows/Linux usage instructions (using the actual `HelpText.cs` syntax) and an explicit claim of self-contained/no-runtime-install behavior for both platforms, since both were genuinely verified this phase.
- **Repository hygiene (skill.md §18)**: `dist/` added to `.gitignore` — release artifacts are build output, not source, and no existing project policy requires checked-in binaries.

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual GUI verification was possible in this environment (same constraint as every prior GUI phase — disclosed, not glossed over). The release script does not (and per this phase's own strict-stop list, must not) produce an MSI/MSIX installer, code-signed binary, or auto-update mechanism — it is a plain publish-and-package script only.

## Phase 11B Addendum — Release Packaging & Distribution (as built)

Follows directly from Phase 11A's own Release Readiness Audit, which found zero release
blockers but one genuinely missing artifact (a Windows CLI executable had never been
published) and a versioning inconsistency (only 4 of 10 `src/` projects declared
`<Version>`). This phase closes both gaps and produces the actual v1.0.0 portable
distribution — packaging/documentation only, no Discovery/Analysis/Risk/Migration/
Reporting/CLI-contract/GUI-feature/scanner change anywhere.

- **Centralized versioning**: `Directory.Build.props` (repo root) — MSBuild imports this for
  every project under the directory automatically, before each project's own `.csproj` is
  evaluated, so `Version`/`AssemblyVersion`/`FileVersion`/`InformationalVersion` need to be
  declared exactly once. The 4 projects that previously carried their own
  `<Version>10.0.0</Version>` (`Cli`, `Gui`, `Gui.Contracts`, `Gui.ExecutionHost`) had that tag
  removed — a per-project `<Version>` would otherwise silently *override* the centralized one,
  defeating the point. Verified post-build by reading `FileVersionInfo` off four built
  assemblies (including three, `Core`/`Reporting`/one more, that previously had **no** explicit
  version at all): all report `FileVersion=1.0.0.0`, `ProductVersion=1.0.0+<commit-sha>` (the
  `+<commit-sha>` suffix on `InformationalVersion` is the .NET SDK's own default
  source-link-adjacent behavior, not something this phase added).
- **`build-release.ps1` — full v1.0.0 pipeline, one script, no second parallel publish
  mechanism**: extends Phase 11A's script (never replaced with a second one) to publish a
  THIRD artifact — `src/ServerSleuth.Cli/ServerSleuth.Cli.csproj` targeting `net8.0-windows`
  (not the `net8.0` build Linux uses) as `win-x64`, alongside the pre-existing GUI and Linux CLI
  publishes — using the identical `--self-contained true -p:PublishSingleFile=true
  -p:IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract=true -p:PublishTrimmed=false` flags Phase 11A already
  established and verified safe for this dependency graph. Output moved from `dist/` to
  `release/windows/`+`release/linux/` (Phase 11A's `dist/` layout had no room for a third,
  same-platform executable without a naming collision risk against the GUI's own folder).
  Reads the version from `Directory.Build.props` via `[xml](Get-Content ...)` rather than a
  second hard-coded string, so the script can never drift from what the assemblies themselves
  report. Adds: per-platform `README.txt`+`VERSION` bundled into each output folder (so a
  downloaded archive is self-explanatory without also fetching this repository's own
  documentation); `Compress-Archive`/`tar.exe`-built `ServerSleuth-v1.0.0-{windows-x64.zip,linux-x64.tar.gz}`;
  an automated package-content audit (opens the just-built ZIP via
  `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile`, extracts the tar.gz to a throwaway staging directory, and
  fails the whole build if either contains a `.pdb`/`.xml`/`.deps.json`/`.runtimeconfig.json`/
  `.cs`/`.csproj`/`bin`/`obj` entry) — a second, independent line of defense on top of the
  already-existing guarantee that only the named executable is ever copied out of each publish
  staging directory (§6 of the script, unchanged in spirit from Phase 11A); a `SHA256SUMS.txt`
  now covering 5 artifacts (3 executables + 2 archives) instead of 2, recomputed fresh every
  run via `Get-FileHash`, never hard-coded.
- **`build-release.sh` — matching update, Linux/macOS host**: same `release/linux/` output,
  same `Directory.Build.props`-sourced version (read via `grep`/`sed` rather than an XML
  parser, since this script has no PowerShell `[xml]` cast available), same `README.txt`/
  `VERSION`/package-content-audit/checksum treatment — continues Phase 11A's own convention of
  only ever updating/adding its own (Linux-specific) lines in an existing `SHA256SUMS.txt`
  written by a prior Windows-host run, never clobbering the Windows lines it cannot itself
  produce. Cannot and does not attempt either Windows artifact — WPF, and the CLI's
  `net8.0-windows` TFM (needed to compile `ServerSleuth.Windows` in), both require the Windows
  build toolchain regardless of which OS `dotnet` itself runs on.
- **Real artifact acceptance — genuinely re-executed this phase, not assumed from Phase 11A's
  own (different-artifact) evidence**:
  - **Windows CLI** (the brand-new artifact): `release/windows/serversleuth-cli.exe` alone —
    `--help`/`--version` (`1.0.0.0`, confirming the centralized version reached a real
    published executable)/`scan --help` all correct. A real local scan (`--verbose --overwrite`,
    output directed to an isolated temp directory well outside `release/`) completed in 22.8s:
    Discovery 34,915 entities/12 scanners/4 partial (18.39s), Analysis 1.52s (consistent with
    Phase 10A-I/10A-J's own prior performance fix — no regression), Risk (318 Critical/17,346
    High/253 Medium), Migration (72 Blocked/30 NeedsRemediation), `report.json` (275MB)/
    `report.html` (209MB) both written, exit code 4 (`PartialDiscovery`) — matching every prior
    real-machine run on this same dev host almost exactly (same scanner partial-count pattern),
    which is itself evidence the packaging change introduced no behavioral difference.
  - **Windows GUI**: `release/windows/ServerSleuth.exe` launched via `Start-Process`, alive
    after 5s, terminated cleanly on request, no orphan process — re-confirms Phase 11A's own
    finding still holds for the new `release/` layout. **Not** a visual/interactive
    confirmation — same disclosed constraint as every prior GUI phase.
  - **Linux CLI**: re-validated (not merely carried over) inside WSL2 (Ubuntu), isolated `/tmp`
    directory. This phase went one step further than Phase 11A's own Linux verification: `type
    dotnet` was run first and confirmed `.NET`/`dotnet` genuinely absent from that WSL instance
    ("not found"), and `file serversleuth` confirmed the binary is a self-contained ELF
    (`dynamically linked, interpreter /lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2` — linked only against the
    OS's own libc, no .NET shared-runtime dependency) — stronger, more direct evidence for the
    "does not require a pre-installed .NET runtime" claim than Phase 11A's own liveness-only
    check provided. `--help`/`--version` (`1.0.0.0`)/`scan --help` all correct; a real local
    scan completed in ~4.8s (1,635 entities, 11 scanners, 6 partial — `linux-kubernetes-scanner`
    correctly `NotInstalled`, no cluster present on this WSL instance), `report.json`/
    `report.html` both written, exit code 0.
  - **Package content audit — independently re-verified outside the build script too**: both
    archives were actually unzipped/untarred and their file listings inspected by hand —
    `ServerSleuth-v1.0.0-windows-x64.zip` contains exactly `README.txt`, `serversleuth-cli.exe`,
    `ServerSleuth.exe`, `VERSION` (4 entries, nothing else); `ServerSleuth-v1.0.0-linux-x64.tar.gz`
    contains exactly `README.txt`, `serversleuth`, `VERSION`.
- **`SECURITY.md`/`MIGRATION.md`** (repository root, both new): closes the two documentation
  gaps Phase 11A flagged as non-blocking. `SECURITY.md` documents the read-only design,
  credential-isolation guarantees (`SecureString`-only passwords, path/env-var-name-only key/
  passphrase references, fail-closed host-key/certificate validation — all facts this
  repository's own existing tests already enforce, not new claims), secret-redaction-in-output,
  and vulnerability-reporting guidance that deliberately does not invent a contact address this
  repository doesn't actually have. `MIGRATION.md` clarifies ServerSleuth's own scope boundary
  — assessment/planning output only (`MigrationStatus`/`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationDependency`/
  `MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck`), never execution against a target — addressing
  the phase instruction's own explicit "do not add migration execution capability."
- **Repository hygiene**: `release/` added to `.gitignore` alongside the pre-existing `dist/` —
  both are multi-hundred-MB generated build output; neither belongs in the git history.
- **Deliberately not touched, per this phase's own explicit scope**: Phase 11A's `dist/`
  directory and Phase 11A's own already-verified GUI/Linux-CLI artifacts (superseded, not
  deleted, by the `release/` output above — no destructive cleanup was performed); the two
  intermittent WPF async-timing test classes (`MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests`/
  `ScanExecutionViewModelTests` — environmental, pre-existing, re-confirmed unchanged, not a
  packaging concern); `GraphValidator`'s suspected-but-unmeasured performance characteristic;
  `RuntimeIdentifiers` (evaluated — the existing `-r win-x64`/`-r linux-x64` command-line flags
  already work correctly, proven by this phase's own three successful publishes, so no
  `<RuntimeIdentifiers>` declaration was added to any `.csproj` per the phase's own "only if it
  actually helps" instruction).

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual GUI verification (same disclosed constraint as
every prior GUI phase). No live SSH/WinRM remote acceptance was performed or newly claimed.
No MSI/MSIX/installer/code-signing/auto-update was added — the portable ZIP/tar.gz distribution
was a deliberate choice (see `PROGRESS.md`'s own Phase 11B entry for the full installer-decision
rationale), not an oversight.

## Phase GUI-7 Addendum — Localization / Language Toggle (as built)

A genuine new user-requested feature, made after GUI-6's own "FINAL STOP" — that stop applied to
the GUI-1..GUI-6 spec-driven phase sequence, not to future user requests, so it was implemented
directly rather than treated as a violation of the prior strict-stop. Scope: a runtime English ⇄
Traditional Chinese toggle for the WPF GUI only (not the CLI, not `report.json`/`report.html`
content, which `ServerSleuth.Reporting` still generates unchanged).

- **Why not `.resx` + `CultureInfo`**: `ServerSleuth.Gui.csproj` sets `<InvariantGlobalization>true</InvariantGlobalization>` (see GUI-1's own decision record above), which makes the standard satellite-assembly/`CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture` resource-fallback pipeline unsafe to rely on. Instead, `LocalizedStrings` (`src/ServerSleuth.Gui/Resources/LocalizedStrings.cs`) is a single `Dictionary<string, (string En, string ZhHant)>` — one key, both languages, in one place — and `LanguageService` (`src/ServerSleuth.Gui/Services/LanguageService.cs`) writes every entry straight into `Application.Current.Resources` for whichever language is now current. Every XAML `{DynamicResource SomeKey}` binding across `MainWindow`/`ScanConfigurationView`/`ScanExecutionView`/`ResultsDashboardView`/`ApplicationDetailView` picks up the new value immediately — no window reload, no restart. A single shared table structurally rules out the classic two-file-resource-dictionary failure mode (a key present in one language's file but not the other): there is only ever one file to add a key to, and it takes both translations at once.
- **Toggle location/behavior**: two buttons ("EN" / "中文") in `MainWindow`'s header, reachable from every screen, bound to `MainViewModel.SetLanguageCommand` (parameter: `GuiLanguage.English` or `GuiLanguage.TraditionalChinese`). Default is English — unchanged behavior for anyone using the app before this existed. Switching language never rebuilds `ScanConfigurationViewModel`/`ScanExecutionViewModel`/the results dashboard, so an in-progress scan or already-computed result is never lost; only the navigation item labels, the currently-shown placeholder page (if any), and the status footer — the three pieces of ViewModel-owned (not plain-XAML) text — are explicitly re-resolved via `MainViewModel.ApplyLanguageChange()`.
- **Scope of what's covered**: every static label, button, header, column header, and empty-state message across all four real screens plus the navigation shell and status footer — roughly 90 keys. Deliberately **out of scope** for this pass (left English-only, a disclosed known limitation, not silently skipped): `ScanConfigurationValidator`'s 13 validation-error messages, `GuiExceptionHandler.GenericUserFacingMessage` (a `public const string` several tests reference directly), report export/viewer result messages, and any value rendered by a plain `{Binding SomeEnum}` with no display converter (e.g. `ScanStage`, `RiskSeverity`, migration status render their raw enum `ToString()`). Localizing these would mean either changing a `const` referenced by name in existing tests or injecting `ILanguageService` into classes with substantial existing unit-test surfaces asserting exact English message text — judged disproportionate risk for a feature request scoped to "the interface," versus the clear, safe, high-value win of localizing everything a user actually reads while operating the four real screens.
- **Composition**: `ILanguageService`/`LanguageService` registered as a `Singleton` in `CompositionRoot` — pure presentation, so it lives in `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself, never `Gui.Contracts`/`Gui.ExecutionHost` (no reason to cross that boundary for a string lookup). `MainViewModel` takes `ILanguageService? languageService = null` as its last constructor parameter (defaults to a real `LanguageService()` when omitted) — the same optional-parameter pattern GUI-5 already established for `IGuiReportExportService`/`IGuiReportViewerService`, so both pre-existing test call sites kept compiling unchanged.
- **No persistence**: language choice lives only in the running process's memory; it resets to English on the next launch. No settings/preferences-file mechanism exists anywhere in the app to hook into, and building one solely for this was judged out of scope — disclosed as a known limitation rather than silently added as a new feature surface.
- **Tests added**: `LocalizedStringsTests` (4 tests) — a completeness sweep proving every key has a non-empty value for BOTH languages (structurally guaranteed by the single-table design, but verified explicitly so a future empty-string entry is still caught), plus an unknown-key-never-throws guard. `LanguageServiceTests` (5 tests) — default language, `SetLanguage` changes `CurrentLanguage`, `T()` resolves genuinely different text per language for the same key and restores on switch-back, `LanguageChanged` fires exactly once per call, unknown key falls back to the key itself. `MainViewModelLanguageTests` (7 tests) — the toggle exercised exactly as the real XAML binding invokes it: default language, nav-item labels change and restore, status footer text changes, a currently-shown placeholder page's title updates in place, and — the one most worth stating plainly — switching language while the Scan Configuration page is showing does **not** replace `CurrentPageViewModel` (`Assert.Same`), proving no scan-configuration state is lost by a language switch.
- **Regression**: `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test` — **2017/2017 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (the pre-existing 2001-test GUI-6/Phase-11A baseline plus these 16 additive tests). Zero existing tests were modified, weakened, or deleted — the new `ILanguageService` parameter's default-null value meant no pre-existing `MainViewModel` test call site needed to change.
- **Interactive/visual validation**: **not performed** — the same environment constraint disclosed in every prior GUI phase (no interactive Windows desktop session available; only Chrome browser automation exists, inapplicable to WPF). A non-visual liveness check was performed instead (`dotnet build` succeeded; the actual `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` launched via `Start-Process`, stayed alive 3 seconds with no crash — which does exercise `CompositionRoot` constructing `LanguageService` and seeding `Application.Current.Resources` on real startup — and terminated cleanly with no orphan process). This is explicitly **not** visual confirmation that the toggle buttons render correctly or that switching them visibly changes on-screen text; a real click-through of the language toggle has never been performed in any environment this project has been developed in.
- **Documentation**: README.md gained a "語言切換" subsection (toggle location/behavior, the no-persistence limitation, and what remains untranslated) plus a full "各畫面逐步說明" walkthrough covering every field/button on all four real screens — addressing the separate but related user request that the README only explained how to *run* the project, not how to *operate* it once it's open.

**Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual verification of the toggle (see above); no persistence across restarts; validation-error text, exception-handler text, report export/viewer result messages, and raw-enum-rendered values (scan stage, risk severity, migration status) remain English-only regardless of the selected language.

## Phase GUI-6A Addendum — Discovery Inventory & Asset Explorer (as built)

Answers "what did this server actually have on it" — the raw, evidence-backed `DiscoveryEntity`
list — as its own first-class section on the Results Dashboard, distinct from and never
recomputing the existing Risk/Migration summaries GUI-4 already built.

- **Data flow (the gap this phase closed)**: `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` produces an
  `AggregateDiscoveryResult`; `GuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync` consumed it only for counts
  (`EntityCount`/`ErrorCount`/`ScannerStatuses`) and then let the reference go out of scope —
  neither `ScanCompletionState` nor `ScanExecutionState` ever carried it. Separately,
  `ScanPipelineRunner.Analyze` computes Phase 5B's `ApplicationBoundary` list and Phase 5C's
  derived `ExternalDependency` entities as local variables, folds them into the correlation graph/
  risk context, and discards both once `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` is built —
  `ScanPipelineResult` previously exposed only `{ Aggregation, Report }`. This phase's entire
  production change is closing that gap: `ScanPipelineResult` gained three additive fields
  (`Discovery`, `Boundaries`, `ExternalDependencies`), populated in `ScanPipelineRunner.Analyze`
  from the exact instances already computed there — zero new analysis, zero reordering, and
  `GuiScanExecutor` needed no change at all (it already threads the whole `ScanPipelineResult`
  through unchanged).
- **Inventory model — reused `Core.Models.DiscoveryEntity` directly, no second domain model**: the
  base already has everything a presentation layer needs (`Id`/`Name`/`Type`/`Version`/`Status`/
  `Architecture`/`Path`/`Publisher`/`Source`/`Confidence`/`Evidence`/`Tags`/`Metadata`), is already
  platform-neutral, and `ServerSleuth.Gui` already references `ServerSleuth.Core` directly (GUI-1's
  original dependency rule). Building a second, parallel "presentation DTO" copy of every concrete
  entity type was judged unnecessary complexity; instead the GUI-layer types
  (`InventoryItemViewModel`/`InventoryDetailViewModel`) are thin, one-way *projections* — never
  copies — that additionally resolve one derived fact `DiscoveryEntity` itself has no concept of:
  which `ApplicationBoundary`(ies), if any, claim this entity as a member.
- **Category vocabulary — confirmed by reading every Windows/Linux scanner's actual `Type =`
  assignment before writing any UI code**, not assumed: `Software`, `Service`, `Configuration`,
  `Server`, `OperatingSystem`, `Certificate`, `Port`, `ComComponent`, `WebSite`, `Application`,
  `ApplicationPool`, `ScheduledTask`, `Process`, `Package`, `Image`, `Volume`, `Network`,
  `NativeBinary`, `Cluster`, the ten `Kubernetes*` types, the Windows binary scanner's
  `BinaryType`-derived values, and Analysis-layer `ExternalDependency`. `InventoryExplorerViewModel.Categories`
  is built by grouping the actual entity list by `Type` — it never hard-codes this list, so a type
  no scanner in a given run produced simply never appears (no synthetic zero-count row).
- **Application attribution — resolved from existing evidence only, never guessed**: a
  `Dictionary<string, List<string>>` (entity id → sorted boundary names) is built once from
  `ApplicationBoundary.MemberEntityIds` (Phase 5B's own real output, now carried through). An
  entity legitimately belonging to zero boundaries renders "Unassigned"; an entity belonging to
  two or more (shared infrastructure) lists every one of them, deliberately not repeating Phase
  7B's own previously-fixed single-boundary-attribution bug — covered by a dedicated 3-boundary
  test case.
- **Security boundary**: no redaction logic exists anywhere in the new GUI code, by design — every
  scanner already redacts secret-shaped `Metadata`/`Evidence.Detail` values via `ISecretRedactor`
  (`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Security`) before a `DiscoveryEntity` is ever constructed (verified
  by reading `WindowsComScanner`'s `SetRedactedMetadata` call site), the same guarantee the
  existing JSON/CSV/HTML reports already rely on — `ServerSleuth.Gui` cannot reference
  `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` at all, so re-implementing redaction there was never an option.
  `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`, `ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests`, and
  `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests` were all extended (additively) with the four new Inventory types.
- **Performance**: entity-by-type grouping, item construction, and the boundary-membership index
  are each a single O(N) or O(N+M) pass built once in `InventoryExplorerViewModel`'s constructor —
  no nested entity×boundary scan. Search/category filtering never re-walks the boundary index; it
  filters the already-built `Items` projection.
- **Determinism**: `Items` is sorted `Type → Name → Id`, all `StringComparer.Ordinal` — no
  dependence on `Dictionary`/`HashSet`/DI-registration enumeration order — verified by a test that
  constructs two independent `ResultsDashboardViewModel`s from the same fixture and asserts
  identical ordering.
- **UI placement**: a new "Discovery Inventory" card directly beneath Scan Summary — category
  chips with live counts, a category filter + search box, a sortable grid, and a detail panel
  (`InventoryDetailView.xaml`, mirroring GUI-4's `ApplicationDetailView` pattern) bound to the
  selected row. No new NuGet package; reuses the existing `Dashboard.Card`/`Text.*`/`ListView`
  styling GUI-1 already established.
- **Tests added**: `ScanPipelineRunnerInventoryTests` (3, `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests`) —
  reference-equality on `Discovery`, and content assertions on `Boundaries`/`ExternalDependencies`
  against a real `ApplicationBoundaryEngine`/`DependencyExpansionEngine` run. `InventoryExplorerViewModelTests`
  (12, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests`) — empty/partial/cancelled states, type-driven category derivation,
  deterministic ordering, search/category filtering without master-list mutation, evidence/metadata
  pass-through, unassigned vs. multi-boundary attribution, and `ExternalDependency` appearing as its
  own category. All additive; nothing existing was weakened.
- **Regression**: `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors across the whole solution. Every non-GUI
  test project passes 100%. `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` shows 1-2 intermittent failures per run in the
  pre-existing `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests`/`ScanExecutionViewModelTests` classes (an
  async `WaitUntilAsync` polling timeout, a different test failing each run) — confirmed via
  `git stash -u` back to the exact pre-GUI-6A checkout to reproduce identically with none of this
  phase's changes present, i.e. pre-existing environmental flakiness in this sandbox, not a
  regression this phase introduced.
- **Interactive/visual validation**: not performed — no interactive Windows desktop session
  available in this environment (same disclosed constraint as every prior GUI phase). Verified
  instead via `dotnet build`, the WPF XAML compiler (both new/modified `.xaml` files compile with
  no binding-path errors), and the automated test suite above.

**Known Limitations**: "Application" attribution shows `ApplicationBoundary.Name`, matching the
rest of the dashboard's own convention (not a separate boundary-ID column); an entity with zero
evidence still appears (its `Status` is whatever the scanner assigned, typically `Unknown` — never
fabricated); Kubernetes/COM/binary categories are exercised only via hand-built fixtures in this
environment, never a real cluster/registry/filesystem scan.

## Phase GUI-7A Addendum — Lightweight Dashboard & Inventory (as built)

Turns two long-standing GUI-1 placeholders (Dashboard) and one GUI-6A section previously reachable
only *inside* Results (Discovery Inventory) into two real, lightweight, first-class navigation
pages — deliberately NOT a CMDB/monitoring/vulnerability-management console. Scan remains the
primary workflow; Dashboard/Inventory are read-only summaries of whatever `ScanPipelineResult` the
last completed scan already produced.

- **Dashboard (`DashboardOverviewViewModel`/`DashboardView.xaml`)**: built FRESH every time
  `NavigationPage.Dashboard` is navigated to (unlike `ResultsDashboardViewModel`, it holds no
  filter/selection state, so there is nothing to lose by rebuilding), directly from
  `ScanExecutionViewModel.State` — the exact same source `ResultsDashboardViewModel`/
  `InventoryExplorerViewModel` already read. Never touches `IDiscoveryEngine`/`ScanPipelineRunner`/
  any engine (`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests` extended). Every number is copied verbatim from an
  already-computed summary (`ServerMigrationSummary`/`ServerRiskSummary`) or `ScanExecutionState`
  itself — no new score, no percentage, no chart. Empty state (`ScanExecutionState.Idle`, or any
  scan that never reached a report) shows a plain "no results yet" card with a Start Scan button —
  never a zeroed-out fake summary.
- **Inventory promoted to a standalone page, not a second engine**: `InventoryExplorerViewModel`
  (GUI-6A) now also implements `IPageViewModel` (`Page => NavigationPage.Inventory`) and is
  constructed directly by `MainViewModel` from `ScanExecutionViewModel.State.PipelineResult` —
  the identical constructor call `ResultsDashboardViewModel` already makes for its own embedded
  `Inventory` property. Its XAML (category chips, search, grid, detail panel) was extracted
  verbatim out of `ResultsDashboardView.xaml` into a new reusable `InventoryExplorerView.xaml`,
  which `ResultsDashboardView` now references via `DataContext="{Binding Inventory}"` instead of
  duplicating the markup — one View, one ViewModel, two navigation paths to it (embedded in
  Results, and standalone). Rebuilt fresh on every navigation to `NavigationPage.Inventory`, same
  as Dashboard — its own filter state is presentation-only and cheap to rebuild (already the
  established GUI-6A performance contract: O(N+M) once per construction, never O(N²)).
- **Navigation**: `NavigationPage` gained one new member, `Inventory`, inserted between `Scan` and
  `Results` (Dashboard/Scan/Inventory/Results/Migration/Reports/Settings — the recommended order).
  `MainViewModel.ApplyCurrentPage` gained two new branches (Dashboard, Inventory); Migration/
  Reports/Settings are untouched and remain `PlaceholderPageViewModel`s. `ShowResults()` (the body
  of the former `ScanExecutionViewModel.ViewResultsRequested` handler) was extracted into its own
  private method so both `ScanExecutionViewModel.ViewResultsRequested` and the Dashboard's own
  "View Results" button route through the identical build/cache path — no second construction
  path, no second navigation system.
- **A real WPF binding-engine defect this phase's own real-window smoke test caught**: because
  Dashboard is rebuilt fresh on every navigation/language switch (unlike every other page, which
  is either cached or itself immutable-per-construction with no repeated rebuild), a `Run`-hosted
  binding to one of its read-only string properties (`TargetDisplayName`) threw the SAME
  `InvalidOperationException: TwoWay or OneWayToSource … read-only property` class of bug a prior,
  unrelated session's post-mortem had already diagnosed and fixed on the Results dashboard's
  Migration Summary panel (see that commit) — `Run`-in-`TextBlock` bindings on a read-only CLR
  property are unsafe specifically when the bound object is being freshly re-attached to layout
  (a collapsed `Expander`'s first expand, or — newly observed here — a `DataContext` swapped for a
  brand-new instance on every navigate). Fixed the same way: replaced every `Run`-hosted binding in
  `DashboardView.xaml`'s "Last Scan" card with plain `TextBlock.Text` bindings at default (unset)
  `Mode`, matching the already-proven-safe convention the Discovery/Risk/Migration cards elsewhere
  in this same file already used. Caught by `RealWindowNavigationSmokeTests`, which — per its own
  doc comment — exists precisely to catch a real layout-pass defect no ViewModel-only test can see.
- **Security boundary**: no new NuGet package, no new `ServerSleuth.*` assembly reference (still
  only `Core`/`Analysis`/`Gui.Contracts`/`Gui.ExecutionHost`), no filesystem/process/network access
  in either new View, no report regeneration on open, no scan execution from either page
  (mechanically verified — see Tests below). `DashboardOverviewViewModel` added to
  `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`, `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`, and
  `ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests`'s existing sweeps (additive only).
- **Tests added**: `DashboardOverviewViewModelTests` (14, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests`) — empty state,
  populated state, statistics copied verbatim from the same fixture `ResultsDashboardViewModel`
  reads, deterministic rebuild, partial/cancelled/failed/zero-application scans, command wiring.
  `MainViewModelDashboardAndInventoryNavigationTests` (9) — Dashboard/Inventory reachable through
  the real `NavigationService`, real data after a completed scan (via the existing
  `FakeGuiScanExecutor`, never a real pipeline run), Dashboard's own buttons routing to
  Results/Inventory/Scan Configuration, Migration/Reports/Settings still placeholders, navigation
  item order. `InventoryExplorerViewModelTests` (GUI-6A, unchanged) continues to cover Inventory's
  category derivation/filtering/attribution/determinism unmodified — its subject type's core logic
  was not touched, only additively given a `Page` property. Existing architecture/security tests
  extended additively, never weakened.
- **Regression**: `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors. `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` full suite passes
  (the same pre-existing intermittent `WaitUntilAsync`-timeout flakiness `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests`/
  `ScanExecutionViewModelTests` already showed in the GUI-6A regression run recurs here
  independently of this phase's changes). Production code outside `ServerSleuth.Gui` was not
  touched.
- **Interactive/visual validation**: not performed — no interactive Windows desktop session
  available in this environment (same disclosed constraint as every prior GUI phase). Verified
  instead via `dotnet build`, the WPF XAML compiler, the automated test suite above, and
  `ServerSleuth.Gui.RealWindowHarness`/`RealWindowNavigationSmokeTests` — a real `Application`/
  `MainWindow`/layout pass through every page, with and without a language switch, which is what
  actually caught the binding defect described above.

**Known Limitations**: Dashboard shows only Critical/High/Medium risk counts (not the full
Critical/High/Medium/Low/Info breakdown Results already has) and only the four migration-status
counts, per the phase's own "concise summary" brief — the full breakdown remains one click away via
"View Results." Dashboard and the standalone Inventory page rebuild on every navigation (no
selection/filter state is preserved across a nav-away-and-back) — an intentional simplicity
trade-off, not a defect, since neither page owns state worth preserving the way Results' filters
do.

## Phase GUI-7B Addendum — Lightweight Migration, Reports & Settings (as built)

Completes the visible GUI-1 navigation with three more real, lightweight pages — Migration,
Reports, Settings — following GUI-7A's exact established conventions (rebuild fresh on every
navigation, reuse existing data/services, no new engine, no new score). Presentation/UX only:
every byte of Migration/Risk/Report data comes from the existing Discovery/Analysis/Risk/
Migration/Reporting engines, untouched by this phase.

- **Migration (`MigrationOverviewViewModel`/`MigrationView.xaml`, both new)**: a summary of the
  latest completed scan's migration assessment — Blocked/Needs Remediation/Ready With Conditions/
  Ready counts (copied verbatim from `ServerMigrationSummary`, never recalculated) plus an
  application list. Selecting a row reuses GUI-4's own `ApplicationDetailViewModel`/
  `ApplicationDetailView.xaml` VERBATIM — issues/actions/verification checks/dependencies,
  declarative only, no Execute/Apply/Install/Restart affordance anywhere (none was added). The
  application-row join itself (`ApplicationMigrationSummary` + `ApplicationRiskSummary`, keyed by
  boundary id) was extracted out of `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s constructor into a new
  `ApplicationRowViewModel.BuildFrom(ScanPipelineResult?)` static factory — both
  `ResultsDashboardViewModel` and `MigrationOverviewViewModel` now call the SAME method, so the
  join logic exists exactly once (re-verified byte-for-byte behavior-preserving via the full,
  unmodified `ResultsDashboardViewModelTests`/`ResultsDashboardDeterminismAndMutationTests` suite
  before and after the refactor).
- **Reports (`ReportsOverviewViewModel`/`ReportsView.xaml`, both new)**: the latest completed
  scan's already-written report files, reusing the EXACT SAME `IGuiReportExportService`/
  `IGuiReportViewerService` boundary GUI-5 established for Results — no second export
