  implementation, no `File.WriteAllText`/`File.WriteAllBytes`/`StreamWriter`/`FileStream`
  anywhere in `ServerSleuth.Gui` (new `NoDirectReportFileWritingTests`, a source-text sweep since
  these are static BCL calls with no type to reflect on). JSON/HTML are shown as plain text via
  a read-only `TextBox`, exactly like Results — no WebView2, no embedded browser, no HTML
  rendering, no JavaScript execution.
- **Settings (`SettingsViewModel`/`SettingsView.xaml`, both new)**: deliberately NOT a new
  settings framework/subsystem — every property is a THIN, DIRECT proxy over state that already
  exists as the session's single long-lived instance: `ScanConfigurationViewModel` (a DI
  singleton) for Default Output Directory/Report Format/Overwrite Policy/Verbose, and the
  existing `ILanguageService` for language. There is no separate storage anywhere in this type —
  reading/writing e.g. `DefaultReportFormat` reads/writes `ScanConfigurationViewModel.OutputFormat`
  directly, so a change is immediately what Scan Configuration's own selector shows next time it's
  opened, with zero risk of the two drifting apart. Since a `ScanRequest` is an immutable record
  built once at "Start Scan" time, nothing here can retroactively alter one already built (proven
  by a dedicated test). No password/SecureString/credential-shaped property exists anywhere on
  this type — `ScanConfigurationViewModel` itself structurally cannot expose its private
  credential field as a property (see that type's own `SetPassword` doc comment), so there is
  nothing for this proxy to accidentally surface either. Appearance/theme selection was
  deliberately left out (see Known Limitations) — no existing theme-switching infrastructure to
  hook into, and skill.md's own instruction is to leave out anything that would need a new
  subsystem rather than force one in.
- **Navigation**: no `NavigationPage` change needed (Migration/Reports/Settings already existed
  in the enum since GUI-1) — only `MainViewModel.ApplyCurrentPage` gained three branches, all
  following GUI-7A's exact "rebuild fresh from `ScanExecutionViewModel.State`" pattern. No second
  navigation system, no `Dictionary`/`HashSet` for navigation state (still the same ordered array
  + `NavigationService`).
- **Security**: no new NuGet package, no new `ServerSleuth.*` assembly reference (still only
  `Core`/`Analysis`/`Gui.Contracts`/`Gui.ExecutionHost`), no scan/pipeline execution from any of
  the three new types (`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests` extended), no credential-shaped property
  (`NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests` extended), no direct
  raw report-file writing (`NoDirectReportFileWritingTests`, new). All additive — nothing existing
  was weakened.
- **A real WPF binding-engine pitfall avoided proactively, not just fixed reactively**: since
  Migration/Reports (like Dashboard before them) rebuild fresh on every navigation, every binding
  in `MigrationView.xaml`/`ReportsView.xaml` was written as a plain `TextBlock.Text` binding at
  default `Mode` from the start — never a `Run`-hosted one — matching the already-proven-safe
  convention GUI-7A's own post-mortem established (see `DashboardView.xaml`'s comment). Verified
  empirically, not just by inspection: `ServerSleuth.Gui.RealWindowHarness`'s own
  `RunResultsDashboardScenario` was extended to also visit Migration (selecting the first
  application, so `ApplicationDetailView`'s binding actually attaches), Reports (opening the
  first report file), and Settings, with a real completed scan and both with and without a
  language switch — exit code 0, no exception, before this phase's documentation was written.
- **Tests added**: `MigrationOverviewViewModelTests` (16), `ReportsOverviewViewModelTests` (14),
  `SettingsViewModelTests` (9), `MainViewModelMigrationReportsSettingsNavigationTests` (13) — all
  additive, covering empty/populated/partial/cancelled/failed/zero-application states, summary
  counts and application ordering copied verbatim from the source fixture, selection exposing
  real issues/actions/checks/dependencies without mutating the master list, Export/Viewer
  commands calling the injected fakes with the exact chosen options (never a second
  implementation), export-failure representation, HTML shown as raw text, Settings proxying
  `ScanConfigurationViewModel` live (including the "already-built `ScanRequest` is never altered"
  guarantee), and full navigation reachability for all seven pages through the one existing
  `NavigationService`. Four existing architecture-test files extended additively (three GUI-7A
  already touched, plus the new `NoDirectReportFileWritingTests`).
- **Regression**: `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors across the whole solution. Every non-GUI
  test project passes 100% (confirms zero production changes/regression outside
  `ServerSleuth.Gui`, and that no backend code was touched this phase at all). `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests`
  reproduces the SAME pre-existing, environment-specific async cold-start flakiness already
  documented in the GUI-6A/GUI-7A entries above (a fresh test process's first `Task.Run`-based
  test can exceed its polling deadline on this sandboxed machine, then pass instantly on any
  later run) — re-confirmed the same way GUI-7A's own entry did, by isolating a single affected
  test and observing the identical fail-then-immediately-pass pattern.
- **Real-machine validation**: not performed — no interactive Windows desktop session available
  in this environment (same disclosed constraint as every prior GUI phase). Verified instead via
  `dotnet build`, the WPF XAML compiler, the automated test suite above, and
  `ServerSleuth.Gui.RealWindowHarness`/`RealWindowNavigationSmokeTests` — a real `Application`/
  `MainWindow`/layout pass through every page (including selecting a Migration application and
  opening a Report file) with a real completed scan, with and without a language switch.

**Known Limitations**: no Appearance/Theme setting (no existing theme-switching infrastructure to
build on — documented rather than forcing a new subsystem in, per this phase's own instruction);
Migration/Reports rebuild on every navigation, so a selected application/opened report resets on
nav-away-and-back (the same intentional trade-off GUI-7A already established for Dashboard/
Inventory); Settings' Language section duplicates the header toggle for discoverability rather
than replacing it — both call the identical `ILanguageService`, never two mechanisms.

## Phase GUI-7C Addendum — Final Integration, Acceptance & Release Hardening (as built)

The final GUI hardening/acceptance phase — no new functionality, only integration verification,
genuine-defect fixing, and final v1.0.0 release evidence. All seven pages (Dashboard/Scan/
Inventory/Results/Migration/Reports/Settings) were confirmed real and functional; none is a
placeholder any more.

- **Scope discipline**: no scanner/Risk rule/Migration rule/execution capability/HTTP API/PDF/
  WebView2/cloud/database/monitoring/scheduling/credential vault was added. No backend project
  (`Core`/`Analysis`/`Infrastructure`/`Windows`/`Linux`/`Cli`/`Reporting`) was touched — every
  production change this phase is under `src/ServerSleuth.Gui`.
- **Two genuine WPF binding-safety defects found and fixed** (skill.md §15's own binding sweep,
  taken seriously rather than treated as a formality):
  1. `InventoryExplorerView.xaml` still had three `Run`-hosted bindings carried over verbatim
     from GUI-6A (`FilteredItems.Count`, and a `Type`/`Count` pair inside the category-chip
     `DataTemplate`). GUI-7A had already promoted this View to also serve as a standalone,
     freshly-rebuilt-per-navigation page (`NavigationPage.Inventory`) — exactly the scenario a
     `Run`-hosted binding on a read-only property already threw `InvalidOperationException` for
     on Dashboard (see the GUI-7A addendum above) — but no test had ever exercised the standalone
     page with real, non-empty inventory data to actually trigger it. Fixed by converting all
     three to plain `TextBlock.Text` bindings at default `Mode`, matching the convention every
     other GUI-7A/7B page already follows. Caught by extending
     `ServerSleuth.Gui.RealWindowHarness`'s own completed-scan scenario to give the fixture a real
     `DiscoveryEntity` and select an inventory item on the standalone page (it previously only
     ever saw an empty inventory there) — confirmed the fix by running the harness both before
     converting the InventoryDetailView pass-through (already safe) and after.
  2. `InventoryExplorerView.xaml` and `SettingsView.xaml` had no `ScrollViewer` of their own.
     `MainWindow.xaml`'s content `ContentControl` provides none either (every OTHER top-level
     page — Dashboard/Migration/Reports/Results/Scan Configuration/Scan Execution — wraps
     itself in one), so as standalone nav pages, a tall detail panel (long Evidence/Metadata
     lists) on a shorter/resized window would have been clipped with no way to reach it — a real
     window-resizing defect (skill.md §14). Fixed by wrapping both in `ScrollViewer
     VerticalScrollBarVisibility="Auto"`, matching every other page. Nesting one inside
     `ResultsDashboardView`'s own outer `ScrollViewer` (the embedded-Inventory case) is a
     standard, well-tolerated WPF pattern, not a defect.
- **Verification method**: `ServerSleuth.Gui.RealWindowHarness`'s completed-scan scenario now
  exercises all seven pages with a real completed scan and REAL, non-empty data throughout —
  selects an Inventory item (new), selects a Migration application, opens a Reports file, visits
  Settings — with and without a language switch. Exit code 0 after both fixes above.
- **No other genuine defects found** in the review sweep (localization coverage, empty/partial/
  cancelled/failed states across Dashboard/Migration/Reports, navigation reachability, no
  execution affordance anywhere in Migration, credential isolation, no duplicate pipeline/
  exporter/report-writing path) — all were already correct from GUI-1 through GUI-7B and are
  re-confirmed, not re-implemented, by this phase's regression run below. The three hardcoded
  `ComboBoxItem` labels in `ScanConfigurationView.xaml` (`Windows`/`Linux`, `JSON`/`HTML`/`Both`)
  are pre-existing, intentional exclusions from `LocalizedStrings`' own documented scope (enum-
  value labels, not static UI text) — not a new gap, left as-is.
- **Regression** (full solution):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:               61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:          445 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:    171 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:             345 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:           376 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0):       98 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0-win):  104 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:         146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:        14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests:  22 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests:                370 total; 369-370 passed per run (0-1 intermittent
                                          WaitUntilAsync-timeout failure, always in the
                                          pre-existing MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests)
  ```
  Every non-GUI project passes 100% across an independent full run this phase — confirms zero
  production changes/regression outside `ServerSleuth.Gui`. `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests`'s
  intermittent failures remain the same pre-existing, environment-specific async cold-start
  flakiness documented in every GUI phase since GUI-6A (re-isolated and re-confirmed this phase
  too); no test was deleted, weakened, or rewritten to reach green.
- **Architecture/security tests**: `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`, `NoDuplicatePipelineEngineTests`,
  `NoDuplicateDomainModelTests`, `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`,
  `ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests`, `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`,
  `NoDirectReportFileWritingTests` all re-run independently and pass — reference boundary still
  exactly `Core`/`Analysis`/`Gui.Contracts`/`Gui.ExecutionHost`, no `SSH.NET`/
  `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure`/`System.Net.Http` reference, no credential-shaped type
  anywhere reachable from any of the seven pages, no duplicate engine, no direct raw report-file
  write.
- **Release build**: `build-release.ps1` run end-to-end from a clean state — Windows GUI
  (160.13 MB), Windows CLI (71.78 MB), Linux CLI (70.19 MB), both archives, `VERSION` (`1.0.0`),
  `SHA256SUMS.txt`. The script's own package-content audit found zero forbidden development
  artifacts in either archive; independently re-verified by hand (`ServerSleuth-v1.0.0-windows-x64.zip`
  → exactly `README.txt`/`serversleuth-cli.exe`/`ServerSleuth.exe`/`VERSION`;
  `ServerSleuth-v1.0.0-linux-x64.tar.gz` → exactly `README.txt`/`serversleuth`/`VERSION`; no
  `.pdb`/`.dll`/`.deps.json`/`.runtimeconfig.json`/source file in either). All 5 SHA-256 checksums
  independently recomputed with `sha256sum` (a different tool than the script's own
  `Get-FileHash`) and matched exactly. Both executables and both archives scanned for
  secret-shaped literal patterns (`password=`, PEM private-key headers, `Server=…Password=`
  connection strings): zero matches. `windows/ServerSleuth.exe`, copied to an isolated temp
  directory (never the repo's own `bin`/`obj`) and launched: alive after 5 seconds, terminated
  cleanly on request with no orphan process — non-visual liveness only, not interactive
  validation (see below). `windows/serversleuth-cli.exe`, likewise from an isolated directory:
  `--version` (`1.0.0.0`, the existing documented assembly-version contract — unchanged),
  `--help`, and a real local scan all behaved correctly.
- **Interactive GUI validation**: **not performed** — no interactive Windows desktop
  session/automation available in this environment, the same disclosed constraint present in
  every GUI phase back through GUI-4A. `FINAL_USER_ACCEPTANCE_CHECKLIST.md` was updated with the
  full current seven-page click-through (Dashboard/Inventory/Migration/Reports/Settings added —
  it previously only covered the GUI-4/GUI-5 feature set) for a human to complete.
- **Documentation**: this addendum; `PROGRESS.md` gained a matching GUI-7C entry;
  `docs/CHANGELOG.md` gained an Unreleased (Phase GUI-7C) entry; `docs/releases/FINAL_RELEASE_SIGNOFF.md`
  and `docs/releases/FINAL_USER_ACCEPTANCE_CHECKLIST.md` (both last updated at Phase 11C, before
  GUI-6A/7A/7B/7C existed) updated with this phase's own final evidence. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`
  not touched (does not track GUI phases, per GUI-7/GUI-7A/GUI-7B precedent).

**Known Limitations** (carried forward, none newly introduced, none silently resolved): no
Appearance/Theme setting; Migration/Reports/Inventory rebuild on every navigation (no
cross-navigation selection/filter persistence); Settings' Language section duplicates the header
toggle; no interactive/visual GUI validation performed in this environment (see
`FINAL_USER_ACCEPTANCE_CHECKLIST.md` for the outstanding human click-through); no live SSH/WinRM
remote acceptance performed (no authorized live host available, unchanged since Phase 10D-2/
11C); intermittent `WaitUntilAsync`/`Task.Run` cold-start test flakiness persists in this specific
sandbox, pre-existing and environmental, not a release blocker.

## Phase GUI-8A Addendum — Inventory-first Dashboard (as built)

**Goal:** Make the Dashboard's primary content the per-type inventory breakdown drawn from real
discovered data — entities counted by C# class from `ScanPipelineResult.Discovery.Entities`
(so variance in `Type` strings across scanners, e.g. `NativeBinary` vs `Dll`, or runtime family
names vs the literal string `"Runtime"`, lands in the correct bucket automatically).

**What changed:**

- `DashboardOverviewViewModel` gained 10 new read-only properties (all computed once in the
  constructor, never recomputed): `ApplicationEntityCount`, `DllEntityCount`,
  `ServiceEntityCount`, `ComComponentEntityCount`, `SoftwareEntityCount`,
  `RuntimeEntityCount`, `ScheduledTaskEntityCount`, `CertificateEntityCount`,
  `ConfigurationEntityCount` (all from `Discovery.Entities.OfType<T>().Count()`), and
  `ExternalConnectionCount` (from `pipeline.ExternalDependencies.Count`). The existing
  `ApplicationCount`/`DependencyCount` from `Report.ServerSummary` (application-boundary
  and dependency counts from the Analysis layer) are unchanged and still shown as totals.

- `DashboardView.xaml`'s "Discovery" card was renamed to "Inventory" (new resource key
  `Overview.Inventory`) and extended with a per-type `WrapPanel` row below the existing
  totals row, showing all 10 categories with plain `TextBlock.Text` bindings — same
  safety rule as the rest of the card (no `Run`-hosted bindings on a freshly-rebuilt VM).
  The Risk and Migration cards remain below the Inventory card in unchanged form.

- `LocalizedStrings.cs` gained 11 new `Overview.Inventory`/`Overview.Inv.*` keys (both
  English and Traditional Chinese).

- `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/ViewModels/DashboardInventoryCountsTests.cs` (15 new tests):
  zero counts with no scan, individual type coverage per class, mixed-entity scenario,
  ExternalConnections via `ExternalDependencies`, determinism, empty-discovery completed scan,
  and independence from Risk/Migration summaries.

- `ServerSleuth.Gui.RealWindowHarness/Program.cs`: the completed-scan fixture now includes
  one entity of each of the 9 Discovery entity classes (Application, Dll, Service,
  ComComponent, Software, Runtime, ScheduledTask, Certificate, Configuration), exercising
  all new binding paths with non-zero values against a real layout pass.

**What was not changed:** No scanner, `IDiscoveryScanner`, `DiscoveryEngine`,
`ApplicationBoundaryEngine`, `DependencyExpansionEngine`, Risk rule, Migration engine, or
Reporting component was touched. `ServerSleuth.Gui`'s assembly-reference boundary is
unchanged. No new NuGet package. No chart library. No filter/navigation change.
`DashboardInventoryCountsTests` count by C# class only, never by `Type` string — immune to
scanner vocabulary variance by construction.

## Decisions Log

| Decision | Rationale | Date |
|---|---|---|
| Adopt `skill.md` §4 layout verbatim (renamed `ServerDiscovery.*` → `ServerSleuth.*`) | Repository was empty; no existing architecture to reconcile against | 2026-08-23 |
| Target `net8.0` across all projects | Matches spec's ".NET 8 or newer LTS" requirement; SDK 10.0.400 present on dev machine can target it | 2026-08-23 |
| Single root `ServerSleuth.slnx` (SDK-default solution format, functionally equivalent to `.sln`) | `dotnet new sln` on SDK 10.0.400 generates `.slnx` by default; enables `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` from repo root per skill.md §9 build requirement | 2026-08-23 |
| Core/Analysis/Reporting/Infrastructure compiled without OS-conditional code; platform isolation done via runtime `PlatformSupport` checks in Cli, not `#if` | Keeps those four projects testable on the Windows dev machine and avoids divergent binaries per OS | 2026-08-23 |
| `IPortInspector` ships in Phase 2 with only its interface + `NetworkEndpoint` DTO, no concrete implementation | skill.md explicitly defers real Windows/Linux socket enumeration to Phase 3/6; registering an interface with no binding would be a DI footgun, so it's left out of `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` until a real implementation exists | 2026-08-23 |
| `SecretRedactor` rules require a key immediately (optionally with whitespace) followed by `:`/`=` before matching | Bare substring matching on words like "token" or "secret" produces heavy false positives (`TokenType=`, `SecretQuestion=`, prose mentioning "token"); anchoring to the key/separator/value shape was verified against explicit false-positive test cases | 2026-08-23 |
| `ServerSleuth.Windows` targets `net8.0-windows`, not plain `net8.0` | Needed for direct, unsuppressed use of `Microsoft.Win32.Registry`, `ServiceController`, and `System.Management`; Core/Infrastructure/Analysis/Reporting remain plain `net8.0` so the cross-platform rule in §13 still holds for them | 2026-08-23 |
| `DiscoveryEntity.Version` changed from `init` to a settable property | OS version (and future version-bearing entities) is only known after a lookup that happens after construction — the same reason `Status`/`Confidence` were already settable in Phase 1; verified via Core's full test suite still passing (31/31) | 2026-08-23 |
| `WindowsInstalledSoftwareScanner` does not merge/dedup entries found across the three registry roots — every registry key becomes its own entity, with the registry path baked into its Id | The operating instructions for Phase 3 raised software dedup as a scanner-level concern, but IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md's Phase 5 explicitly owns "Deduplication," and skill.md's own architecture principle (§33, §recap in this file's §8/§10) keeps correlation out of scanners; merging here would duplicate that logic in two places. Each entity instead carries full identity fields (Name/Publisher/Version/Architecture/InstallLocation) for Phase 5's correlation engine to actually merge later | 2026-08-23 |
| `Microsoft.Web.Administration` is loaded via `Assembly.LoadFrom` + `dynamic` at runtime, not a compile-time `<Reference>`/NuGet package | A compile-time reference needs either a hard-coded `HintPath` to `%windir%\System32\inetsrv\Microsoft.Web.Administration.dll` (breaks `dotnet build` on any machine without IIS Management installed — a real scenario, not hypothetical) or an unofficial third-party NuGet republishing a Windows system DLL (a licensing/provenance concern the Phase 4A instructions explicitly asked to weigh). Late binding means `ServerSleuth.Windows` always builds regardless of IIS presence, and a missing DLL becomes the already-required `IisAvailability.NotInstalled` runtime outcome instead of a build break | 2026-08-23 |
| IIS's virtual "Application" (site + virtual path + pool) reuses `Core.Models.Application` rather than a new `IisApplication` type | `skill.md`'s own entity list (§5) and graph node list (§21) both name a single `Application` node — Phase 7's cross-scanner grouping (§20) will later populate the same entity type from correlation instead of a single scanner; introducing a second, IIS-specific type would fragment one modeled concept into two for no benefit | 2026-08-23 |
| `DiscoveryEntity.Publisher` changed from `init` to a settable property | Same root cause as the earlier `Version` change: COM component publisher is only known after a post-construction `FileVersionInfo` read on the resolved server path; re-verified against Core's full test suite (31/31 still passing) | 2026-08-23 |
| COM Machine-type/architecture detection (custom PE header parsing) is out of scope for Phase 4B; `FileVersionInfo`-based Version/Publisher is in scope | skill.md §2 explicitly excludes "DLL deep analysis" from this phase, and custom PE byte parsing is squarely that; `FileVersionInfo.GetVersionInfo` is a standard, non-executing .NET API already conceptually covered by §17's "File Verification" list — a real, non-arbitrary line between the two | 2026-08-23 |
| Same CLSID found in multiple registry views/scopes produces multiple `ComComponent` entities (never merged in this scanner) | Matches the same "scanner discovers, Correlation (Phase 5) merges" principle already applied to `WindowsInstalledSoftwareScanner` in Phase 3 — each entity's `Id` bakes in scope+view specifically so this is safe, not accidental | 2026-08-23 |
| Task Scheduler accessed via late-bound COM (`Schedule.Service` ProgID + `dynamic`), same pattern as the Phase 4A IIS provider | Avoids a compile-time COM dependency so the project builds on any machine; Task Scheduler is always present on Windows (unlike IIS) so there's no `NotInstalled` branch for this scanner, only `AccessDenied`/`Failed`/`Supported`/`PartiallySupported` | 2026-08-23 |
| `CertificateExpiryClassifier` is a standalone pure helper, not logic inlined in `WindowsCertificateScanner` | Explicit instruction (skill.md §16) not to hard-code expiry/risk bands into the scanner; keeping it separate and taking an explicit `asOf` parameter also makes the classification fully deterministic-testable per §22, without needing to fake `DateTime.Now` | 2026-08-23 |
| No active IIS-binding-to-Certificate correlation code written in Phase 4C | Both sides already produce identically-normalized (uppercase, no separator) thumbprints, which alone satisfies the "lightweight reference is allowed" instruction — writing an actual join/graph edge would start doing Phase 5's job (Application/Site → USES → Certificate) ahead of schedule | 2026-08-23 |
| One `IRuntimeDetector` per runtime family + one shared `RuntimeEntityBuilder`, rather than one scanner per family or one monolithic scanner | Explicit Phase 4D architecture requirement (no hard-coded if/else per runtime); a shared entity builder avoids duplicating status/confidence/evidence rules eight times over | 2026-08-23 |
| `.NET Framework` release-mapping uses Microsoft's own `>=`-threshold table instead of an exact-match dictionary | This is Microsoft's own officially documented detection method; an exact-match table would incorrectly report "unknown" for legitimate hotfix Release values that don't exactly match a table entry | 2026-08-23 |
| `JavaDetector` merges registry and command evidence only when both resolve to the same `JavaHome` path, keeping a `ConflictNote` on version disagreement rather than picking one source | Matches skill.md §26's explicit instruction to preserve conflicting observations rather than silently resolving them | 2026-08-23 |
| `SecretRedactor` (Phase 2) rules extended: optional quote after the key (`["']?`) and three new compound-key rules (ClientSecret/AccessToken/RefreshToken) | Building Configuration Discovery surfaced that the existing rules missed quoted JSON-style keys (`"password": "x"`) and common camelCase/snake_case compound keys entirely — a real, pre-existing gap directly blocking this phase's stated goal, not scope creep; fixed once in shared Infrastructure rather than worked around per-scanner | 2026-08-23 |
| Connection-string detection matches by value *shape* (a recognized ADO.NET key like `Server=`/`Host=` followed by `=`) rather than requiring adjacency to a `"connectionString"` key name | The original adjacency-based design missed ASP.NET Core's real `"ConnectionStrings": { "Default": "..." }` nesting — caught by a real-shaped test before shipping, not assumed to work | 2026-08-23 |
| `IisScanner`, `WindowsServiceScanner`, `WindowsScheduledTaskScanner` are now also registered as their own concrete types in DI (shared singleton), not only via `IDiscoveryScanner` | `WindowsConfigurationScanner` needs to re-run these specific scanners to derive scan roots — registering the concrete type and pointing the `IDiscoveryScanner` registration at the same instance (`sp => sp.GetRequiredService<T>()`) avoids constructing two separate instances of each | 2026-08-23 |
| PE parsing uses the official `System.Reflection.Metadata` package (`PEReader`) rather than a hand-rolled byte-offset parser | This is precisely the "prefer BCL/minimal parser over a large dependency" case working as intended — it's a small, first-party, read-only, purpose-built library (used internally by Roslyn/ildasm-class tooling), and correctly implementing PE32/PE32+/import-table/CLR-header parsing by hand carries real correctness risk for comparatively little benefit | 2026-08-23 |
| `WindowsServiceScanner` is also registered as a shared concrete-type singleton (already true for `IisScanner`/`WindowsScheduledTaskScanner`); `WindowsComScanner` added to that same pattern | `WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner` needs to re-run all four scanners (IIS/Service/Task/COM) to derive both scan roots and direct file references — same reasoning as the Phase 4E-1 DI change | 2026-08-23 |
| `ServerSleuth.Analysis` targets plain `net8.0` and references only `ServerSleuth.Core`, not `ServerSleuth.Windows` or `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` | Correlation must work identically once Linux discovery exists (skill.md's own layered-architecture principle); a Windows dependency would make the correlation layer Windows-only for no reason, since it only needs entity/evidence/graph models | 2026-08-23 |
| `CommandLineReference` (Analysis) reimplements the same quoted/ambiguous-path parsing algorithm as `ServerSleuth.Windows.COM.ServerReference` (Phase 4B) rather than referencing it | Taking a dependency from the platform-agnostic Analysis layer onto the Windows-only layer would invert the architecture; the two parse different raw inputs at different pipeline stages (COM registry values at scan time vs. generic ImagePath-style command lines at correlation time), so this is the same proven algorithm reused, not duplicated scope | 2026-08-23 |
| `DependencyEdgeType` extended with `Contains`/`Imports`/`Configures` rather than reusing `DependsOn`/`Loads`/`ConfiguredBy` for these cases | skill.md §8/§14 specifies a small but exact controlled vocabulary (HOSTS/USES/CONTAINS/RUNS/REFERENCES/BINDS_TO/IMPORTS/CONFIGURES/DEPENDS_ON); collapsing CONTAINS into DEPENDS_ON specifically would violate §2/§9's explicit warning that mere containment must never be conflated with a real dependency | 2026-08-23 |
| Rule 9 (Binary IMPORTS Binary) resolves an import name only against binaries discovered in the *importing* binary's own directory | The only safe, non-guessing resolution rule given the negative fixture requirement (skill.md §23): two unrelated applications' same-named DLLs must never cross-link. No filesystem rescanning is performed to find "the" DLL elsewhere | 2026-08-23 |
| Rule 10 (Configuration REFERENCES ...) only ever creates Runtime edges, never Database/Endpoint/UNC edges | No scanner yet produces Database/ExternalDependency/UNC-share entities to link to; creating an edge to a nonexistent node would violate skill.md §12/§17. This is a disclosed limitation, not an oversight — see Known Limitations in the Phase 5A Completion Report | 2026-08-23 |
| `ApplicationBoundary` is a new `Core.Boundaries` model, not a reuse of `Core.Models.Application` | `Application` already has an established, tested meaning (one IIS site + virtual path) since Phase 4A; the two concepts genuinely differ (skill.md §1 explicitly warns against equating them) and repurposing `Application` would be a breaking change to existing Phase 4A behavior, which the Phase 5B instructions explicitly forbid without a genuine blocker | 2026-08-23 |
| Cross-workload merging triggers on sharer *count* (exactly 2), not sharer *type* | skill.md §7's example is Service+Task, but restricting the rule to that literal pairing would still wrongly merge e.g. two Services that happen to share `svchost.exe`; counting sharers and refusing to merge above 2 is the actual invariant skill.md §8 is protecting, and is verified by a dedicated 3-sharer test | 2026-08-23 |
| `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` reuses Phase 5A's `CorrelationContext` and `DependencyGraph` directly instead of building its own identity indexes | Phase 5B's own instructions (§4/§18) require consuming existing Phase 5A evidence rather than rediscovering it; `CorrelationContext` already has every index (Applications/Services/ScheduledTasks/Configurations/Dlls) Phase 5B needs | 2026-08-23 |
| `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` gets a new, additive `RuntimeVersionReferences` field (explicit `TargetFramework`-anchored version detection) rather than Phase 5C guessing a version in the Analysis layer | Configuration entities never carry raw file text (by design), so Analysis structurally cannot recover a version string Phase 4E-1 never captured — this is the "genuine data-contract blocker" the Phase 5C instructions explicitly allow a narrow scanner change for; existing family-only `RuntimeReferences` behavior is untouched | 2026-08-23 |
| `Endpoint{i}.Path`/`Database{i}.Port`/`NetworkPath{i}.Path` added to `Configuration.Metadata` | All three values were already computed by `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` since Phase 4E-1 but never surfaced — omitting them would force Phase 5C's `ExternalDependency` normalization to guess a port/path it could otherwise state factually; purely additive, no existing key/value changed | 2026-08-23 |
| `DerivedWorkloadDependency` is a new Core model, not a `DependencyEdge` | `ApplicationBoundary` (Phase 5B) is deliberately not a `DiscoveryEntity` and not a `DependencyGraph` node; reusing `DependencyEdge` for boundary-level derived relationships would blur "entity X depends on entity Y" (direct) with "workload X appears to depend on Y" (derived via membership) — skill.md (Phase 5C) §21-22 explicitly wants that distinction preserved with a provenance chain | 2026-08-23 |
| COM→workload association requires either direct boundary membership of the referenced binary, or a boundary-member binary importing it — no other path | skill.md (Phase 5C) §5-6 explicitly forbids a blanket "every COM belongs to every workload" association; this two-tier rule is the narrowest one that still satisfies §23's positive example, verified against a 200-unrelated-registration stress test | 2026-08-23 |
| `GraphValidator` implements duplicate-node/duplicate-edge checks even though `DependencyGraph`'s own API makes both states unreachable today | skill.md (Phase 5D) §5 explicitly frames these as regression safety nets, not reachable-today bugs; duplicate-node detection instead operates on the raw pre-graph `entities[]` list, where a genuine silent-drop is real and catchable | 2026-08-24 |
| Cycle detection is an iterative (not recursive) Tarjan's SCC implementation | A recursive DFS risks a real call-stack overflow on a long chain (e.g. the 1,000-node large-graph test's import chain); the iterative version with an explicit work stack has no such risk at the same O(V+E) cost | 2026-08-24 |
| `GraphValidator` re-derives runtime-version-match and confidence-escalation invariants independently rather than trusting Phase 5C computed them correctly | The validator's entire purpose is to catch a future regression in Phase 5A/5B/5C's own logic; trusting those engines' outputs at face value for the exact invariant they're supposed to enforce would defeat that purpose | 2026-08-24 |
| `ServerSleuth.Linux` targets plain `net8.0` (not a `net8.0-linux`-style TFM) and references only Core + Infrastructure | Matches `ServerSleuth.Windows`'s own precedent of a platform-named project on a plain TFM; there is no Linux-specific TFM in .NET the way `net8.0-windows` exists, and the project needs no Linux-only BCL surface beyond what's already cross-platform (`File.ResolveLinkTarget`/`FileInfo.LinkTarget`, process execution) | 2026-08-24 |
| `/proc/<pid>/exe` and `/proc/<pid>/fd/*` symlink resolution uses `FileInfo.LinkTarget` directly rather than a new `IFileSystemReader` method | This is a genuinely OS-specific mechanism (a "magic" symlink unique to Linux's /proc), the same category of exception `ServerSleuth.Windows` already uses WMI/registry APIs directly for, rather than routing every OS-specific primitive through one shared interface | 2026-08-24 |
| Process UID is preserved as raw metadata, never resolved to a username via `/etc/passwd` | Phase 6A's own instructions list only `/etc/os-release`, `/proc/*`, and systemd metadata as allowed read targets — `/etc/passwd` is outside that list; resolving it would be scope creep this phase's instructions didn't authorize | 2026-08-24 |
| Process `StartTime` is left null on Linux | Converting `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s tick-based starttime into a real timestamp requires assuming the kernel's `CLK_TCK` value, which is not portably discoverable without a syscall — an assumption, not a fact, so it was not implemented rather than guessed | 2026-08-24 |
| `LinuxPortInspector`/`LinuxPortScanner` mirror `WindowsPortInspector`/`WindowsPortScanner`'s structure closely, as separate per-OS classes, rather than one shared implementation | `IPortInspector` (Phase 2) is exactly the seam designed for this — one interface, one implementation per OS reading from that OS's native source (CIM/WMI vs `/proc/net`) — collapsing them would require a fictitious shared data source that doesn't exist on either OS | 2026-08-24 |
| Scan roots under `%windir%` (System32, SysWOW64, WinSxS, ...) are excluded from full directory walks; the specific file a COM registration references there is still checked directly | Measured, not assumed: the first real-machine run took 6m39s and surfaced 29,461 binaries, most of it OS-shipped noise from a handful of COM registrations resolving into these huge system directories. After the fix: 8.3 seconds, 18,125 binaries — all genuine application/vendor content. Directly serves skill.md §4's "never scan the whole disk" and §35's "must demonstrate discovery is bounded" | 2026-08-23 |
| `IRuntimeDetector`/`RuntimeDetectionRow`/`RuntimeDetectionResult`/`RuntimeEntityKind`/`RuntimeEntityBuilder`/`RuntimeDetectionDefaults`/`DotNetCliOutputParser` moved from `ServerSleuth.Windows.Runtimes` to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Runtimes` | `ServerSleuth.Linux` cannot reference `ServerSleuth.Windows`, and duplicating Phase 4D's entity-mapping/confidence logic for Linux would violate "do not duplicate RuntimeEntityBuilder logic if it can be reused safely" — this is the platform-neutral half of that architecture, verified safe by the full 299-test Windows suite passing unmodified after the move | 2026-08-24 |
| `RuntimeEntityBuilder.Build` gained an optional `resolveSource` parameter instead of hard-coding a Source label | The original implementation hard-coded Windows-specific `EvidenceSources.WindowsRegistry`/`WindowsProcessApi` strings, which cannot live in a platform-neutral Infrastructure class; making the parameter optional (default: generic "Registry"/"Command") kept every pre-existing single-argument call site — including all 13 `RuntimeEntityBuilderTests` — compiling and passing unchanged | 2026-08-24 |
| `IExecutableLocator`/`ExecutableLocator` and `ArchitectureHint` stayed in `ServerSleuth.Windows`, not extracted | Both are genuinely OS-shaped (`;`-separated PATH and `.exe` matching; `"Program Files (x86)"` path heuristics) — Linux has its own distinct `IExecutableLocator`/`LinuxExecutableLocator` (`:`-separated PATH, no extension) rather than a shared interface with two incompatible implementations pretending to be one abstraction | 2026-08-24 |
| Linux runtime detectors use a small internal `LinuxPath` helper instead of `System.IO.Path` | A real, measured bug: `Path.GetDirectoryName`/`Path.Combine` rewrite `/` to `\` when the assembly runs on Windows (confirmed via a diagnostic test), which would corrupt every Linux-shaped path this project ever manipulates on a non-Linux host; `LinuxPath` is forward-slash-only regardless of the host OS | 2026-08-24 |
| `Core.Models.Package` (existing since Phase 1) is reused for all three package managers, with `PackageManager` as the only manager-specific field | The model already exists exactly for this purpose (skill.md §18) and already carries a `PackageManager` discriminator; no new Core model was needed | 2026-08-24 |
| Package identity never merges across package managers, only within one manager's own result set | skill.md (Phase 6B) §8 explicitly forbids merging `dpkg`'s and `rpm`'s `openssl` into one entity just because the name matches — they are genuinely different packages with different versions/provenance | 2026-08-24 |
| `apk`'s package/version split uses Alpine's own `-r<release>` suffix convention rather than a naive last-hyphen split | `apk info -v` has no per-field query-format option (unlike `dpkg-query -W -f=...`/`rpm --queryformat`); a naive split would misparse hyphenated package names like `ca-certificates` | 2026-08-24 |
| `Core.Models.ScheduledTask` (existing since Phase 1) is reused for cron jobs, with `Folder` holding the source file path and `Trigger` holding the schedule expression (or run-parts interval name) | The model's fields (Folder/Trigger/Action/RunAsAccount/Enabled) already map onto cron semantics without any Windows-only assumption — no new model was needed, matching skill.md (Phase 6B) §16 | 2026-08-24 |
| Cron executable-path extraction only recognizes an explicit leading absolute path; a bare command name or shell-chained command stays unresolved | Resolving either would require a PATH lookup (which depends on the cron job's own environment, not analyzable statically) or evaluating shell syntax — both explicitly forbidden; skill.md §20 explicitly allows "unresolved commands must remain unresolved" | 2026-08-24 |
| `Core.Models.Container` (existing since Phase 1) is reused via a `Type` discriminator for Container/Image/Volume/Network, instead of introducing new Core models | The model's own doc comment already anticipated "Docker container/image" use, and the Phase 6C instructions explicitly forbade `DockerContainer`/`PodmanContainer` types; extending the existing generic-entity pattern to Volume/Network kept one model instead of four | 2026-08-24 |
| Docker and Podman share one internal `ContainerCliRuntimeProvider`, parametrized only by executable name, rather than two independent provider classes | Their read-only CLI/JSON output (`ps`, `inspect`, `images`, `volume ls`, `network ls`/`inspect`) is compatible enough that a second full implementation would be pure duplication, not a genuine platform difference | 2026-08-24 |
| Container environment variable values are discarded before ever reaching an entity — only variable names are stored | Container env vars are disproportionately likely to carry live credentials directly (`DB_PASSWORD=...`); masking a value after storing it is a weaker guarantee than never storing it, per the phase's explicit "prefer preserving KEY=[REDACTED]" instruction | 2026-08-24 |
| Container/Image/Volume/Network identity always includes the runtime name (`container:{runtime}:{id}`) | Docker and Podman both use 64-hex-character container/image IDs from the same underlying lineage; two coincidentally-identical IDs from different runtimes must never collide into one entity | 2026-08-24 |
| No `Container → host Process` relationship is inferred from PID/command-line/port similarity alone in Phase 6C | Container networking and host socket ownership are different evidence domains; skill.md's own architecture principle keeps correlation out of scanners — namespace-based correlation, if ever added, belongs to Phase 5 analysis | 2026-08-24 |
| Ten new Core entity models introduced for Kubernetes (Cluster, KubernetesNamespace, KubernetesNode, KubernetesPod, KubernetesWorkload, KubernetesService, KubernetesIngress, KubernetesConfigMap, KubernetesSecret, KubernetesPersistentVolumeClaim, KubernetesPersistentVolume) rather than reusing Phase 1 models | No existing Core entity honestly represents any of these Kubernetes resource shapes, unlike every prior Linux phase (which reused Package/Runtime/ScheduledTask/Container); the phase's own instructions named these exact types as preferred over provider-specific ones | 2026-08-24 |
| Pod containers reuse `Core.Models.Container` (Type="KubernetesContainer") rather than a new model | A container-within-a-pod is semantically close enough to the Phase 6C Docker/Podman container shape (name/image/state/restart count) to justify reuse; a new type would duplicate fields for no benefit | 2026-08-24 |
| `IKubernetesProvider` has exactly one implementation (`KubectlKubernetesProvider`), unlike Phase 6C's two-provider Docker/Podman split | Kubernetes has exactly one real acquisition path in scope (kubectl using its own configured context) — the interface exists only to keep a future non-CLI path pluggable, not to switch between two current runtimes | 2026-08-24 |
| Kubernetes Secret values are prevented from ever being materialized as a string, not merely redacted after being read | `SecretItem.Data` is typed `Dictionary<string, JsonElement>` so the base64 payload is never converted to a usable string anywhere in the provider; skill.md marked Secret handling CRITICAL (§15), warranting a stronger guarantee than the "store then redact" approach used for container labels/ConfigMap values | 2026-08-24 |
| `Cluster.ClusterIdentifier` is populated only from the `kube-system` namespace's own UID, never a generated GUID | skill.md §6/§22 explicitly forbid inventing cluster IDs; `kube-system`'s UID is a fact the API already reports, not a derived or guessed value | 2026-08-24 |
| `GetList<T>` records a partial failure when JSON deserialization returns `null`, not just when the process call itself fails | A real bug: a malformed top-level JSON response was initially indistinguishable from a genuinely empty `items: []`, silently reporting a false-empty result as a fully successful scan — caught by a dedicated test, not assumed | 2026-08-24 |
| The platform-neutral configuration-analysis architecture moved from `ServerSleuth.Windows.Configuration` to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Configuration` | None of it (formats, structural validation, content analysis, the Service/ScheduledTask half of scan-root collection) touches a Windows-only API; Linux Phase 6E needed the identical logic and duplicating it would have violated the "reuse, don't duplicate" instruction — mirrors the Phase 6B `RuntimeEntityBuilder` extraction precedent | 2026-08-24 |
| `ScanRootCollector.Deduplicate` takes a `StringComparer` and an optional path-normalization function as parameters, rather than hard-coding one comparison rule | Windows paths are case-insensitive (and were previously trimmed/lower-cased before comparison); Linux paths are case-sensitive and must never be lower-cased — the shared method needed to support both without either platform's behavior changing | 2026-08-24 |
| Linux application-root derivation climbs exactly one directory level when the executable sits directly under a `bin`/`sbin` directory, but never for any other directory name and never past `/` | `/opt/app/bin/executable` is a ubiquitous Linux convention with no Windows analog; the climb is bounded to one level and guarded against reaching the filesystem root, per skill.md (Phase 6E) §5-6's "do not climb arbitrarily toward /" | 2026-08-24 |
| `/etc/cron.d` is deliberately excluded from Linux configuration scan roots despite being named in skill.md (Phase 6E) §4's own root list | §4's header text explicitly forbids duplicating Phase 6B's cron job discovery responsibilities; `LinuxScheduledTaskScanner` already produces `ScheduledTask` entities for those exact files, and re-scanning them here would create a duplicate `Configuration` entity with no new information | 2026-08-24 |
| A systemd unit's `EnvironmentFile=` reference triggers a second, one-level-deep scan pass to discover that file as its own `Configuration` entity, owned by the referencing Service | skill.md (Phase 6E) §17 requires the referenced file be discovered "only if the path is explicit"; bounding the pass to exactly one level (never expanding an `.env` file's own content for further references) keeps the discovery deterministic and prevents unbounded recursive expansion | 2026-08-24 |
| `SecretRedactor`'s key-value rule leading anchor changed from `\b` to `(?<![A-Za-z])` | `\b` never matched the Linux `.env`/systemd convention of an underscore-prefixed key (`DB_PASSWORD=...`) since `_` is a word character; the new anchor accepts an underscore immediately before the key while still rejecting an immediately-preceding letter (so camelCase run-ons like `MyPassword=...` remain unmatched, unchanged from before) — found via a Phase 6E test failure on the exact `DB_PASSWORD` example skill.md §9 specifies, verified against the full pre-fix 758-test baseline with zero regressions | 2026-08-24 |
| ELF parsing is hand-written rather than pulled from a third-party ELF library | The subset needed (identification header, program headers, dynamic section array) is a small, stable, well-specified binary layout with low version-skew risk — not worth a new dependency for; skill.md (Phase 6F) §4 itself asks for "the minimum ELF parser required," not full tooling | 2026-08-24 |
| `EntityArchitecture` gained a `RiscV` member, mapped from ELF `EM_RISCV` | skill.md (Phase 6F) §5 explicitly requires RISC-V architecture mapping; every existing `switch` over `EntityArchitecture` already has a `_ =>` default arm, so the addition is zero-risk (confirmed by the full regression) | 2026-08-24 |
| Big-endian ELF input causes the parser to stop immediately after reading `EI_CLASS`/`EI_DATA`, never decoding `e_machine` or any further field | Every field past the identification header is multi-byte and endian-dependent; this parser does not implement byte-swapping, so decoding further would require either guessing or silently misinterpreting the value — both explicitly forbidden by skill.md §6 | 2026-08-24 |
| Dependency resolution is a fixed five-tier order (RPATH, RUNPATH, known-binary-paths, well-known-locations, ldconfig), with `Ambiguous` reachable from exactly one tier (known-binary-paths) | RPATH/RUNPATH/well-known-locations are each internally ordered lists where the real dynamic loader (or this scanner's own documented convention) already defines a winner; only the known-binary-paths tier compares two independently-discovered, unordered paths with no principled way to prefer one, which is the genuine ambiguity case skill.md §19 describes | 2026-08-24 |
| Only `$ORIGIN` is expanded in RPATH/RUNPATH entries; `$LIB`/`$PLATFORM` are left as literal, unresolved text | `$ORIGIN` always resolves to exactly one bounded, already-known value (the importing binary's directory); `$LIB`/`$PLATFORM` would require inferring the runtime's ABI/multiarch identity, which is exactly the kind of non-ELF-header-derived architecture inference skill.md §5 forbids | 2026-08-24 |
| Path normalization for RPATH/RUNPATH candidates is pure string manipulation (a segment stack that drops a `..` at the top rather than underflowing), never a filesystem-resolving API call | skill.md (Phase 6F) §20 requires that `../..` can never resolve outside a bounded root; doing this lexically, with no filesystem access during normalization itself, means a maliciously crafted RPATH entry cannot cause any traversal — only the final normalized path is ever existence-checked, and only as one exact candidate, never a directory walk | 2026-08-24 |
| `ldd` was not implemented in this phase | skill.md (Phase 6F) §13 explicitly prefers not implementing it; DT_NEEDED parsing alone is the authoritative, execution-free dependency source and fully covers the phase's requirements | 2026-08-24 |
| `LinuxProcessScanner` and `LinuxRuntimeDiscoveryScanner` are now (Phase 6F) also registered as their own concrete types in DI, extending the pattern Phase 6E introduced for the systemd/cron scanners | `LinuxNativeDependencyScanner` needs to reuse all four scanners' already-discovered executable paths; registering each as both its concrete type and its `IDiscoveryScanner` interface (via a factory pointing at the same singleton) avoids instantiating any of them twice | 2026-08-24 |
| `IDiscoveryScannerRegistry`/`IDiscoveryEngine` live in `ServerSleuth.Core`, not a new dedicated project | Both types depend only on `IDiscoveryScanner`/`DiscoveryContext`/`DiscoveryResult`, already in Core; a new assembly for two small classes would be pure ceremony, and Core is already the one place every consumer (Windows, Linux, Integration.Tests) can reference without a new cross-project edge | 2026-08-24 |
| `AddServerSleuthDiscoveryEngine()` is a separate DI extension method, not folded into `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` | The registry only has value once `IEnumerable<IDiscoveryScanner>` is populated by `AddServerSleuthWindows()`/`AddServerSleuthLinux()`; keeping registration separate makes that ordering dependency visible in the composition root's own code rather than hidden inside a method name that doesn't suggest it | 2026-08-24 |
| `DiscoveryEngine` performs zero entity merging/deduplication of any kind | skill.md (Phase 6G) §8 explicitly forbids unsafe global merging at the orchestration layer; deciding whether two observations describe the same logical entity is Analysis's (Phase 5's) job, verified by a dedicated test that two different scanners' entities for "the same real thing" both survive untouched | 2026-08-24 |
| A new `ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests` project (targeting `net8.0-windows`) was created rather than adding cross-platform tests to an existing project | No existing test project references both `ServerSleuth.Windows` and `ServerSleuth.Linux` — Windows.Tests/Linux.Tests are deliberately platform-scoped and Analysis.Tests is deliberately platform-neutral (constructs Core entities directly); proving the cross-platform composition and shared vocabulary genuinely requires a project referencing both | 2026-08-24 |
| The Phase 6G cross-platform Analysis fixture constructs Windows/Linux entities directly rather than running real scanners | The goal (skill.md §17) is proving the shared Core vocabulary and the Analysis engines' behavior on mixed-platform data, not re-testing scanner discovery logic that every prior phase's own suite already covers | 2026-08-24 |
| Real Linux validation was executed on a genuine WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 LTS instance found already present on the machine, with the .NET 8 runtime + .NET 10 SDK installed user-locally (no sudo) after explicit user approval | skill.md (Phase 6G) §11 names this the preferred distro; installing only the build/test tooling (never a scan target like Docker/Podman/Kubernetes) keeps the validation environment honest per §12, and running the *existing, unmodified* test suites there (rather than writing new Linux-specific tests first) is what actually surfaced the two real bugs below | 2026-08-24 |
| `WindowsPathNormalizer` gained hand-rolled `GetDirectoryName`/`Combine`/`GetFileName` methods, replacing raw `System.IO.Path` calls in `BinaryImportsBinaryRule`/`ApplicationBoundaryEngine` (and the `EntityFactory`/`ExternalDependencyExtractorTests` test fixtures) | `System.IO.Path` interprets separators according to the *current* runtime OS; on a POSIX runtime it does not recognize `\` at all, silently breaking directory/filename derivation for Windows-style paths already canonicalized to backslash form by `WindowsPathNormalizer.Normalize` — found via real Linux execution (5 real Analysis.Tests failures), fixed with zero Windows regressions, and re-verified fixed on the same real machine | 2026-08-24 |
| `KubectlKubernetesProvider.Classify`/`ContainerCliRuntimeProvider.Classify` now check both `OperationStatus.StartFailed` and `OperationStatus.NotFound` for a missing executable | The real `ProcessRunner` classifies a genuinely-missing executable as `NotFound`, not `StartFailed`; `FakeProcessRunner`'s hard-coded StartFailed default for an unregistered command meant no fixture had ever exercised this path — found on a real WSL machine with `kubectl` genuinely absent, which surfaced as `ScannerStatus.Failed` instead of the correct `NotInstalled` before the fix | 2026-08-24 |
| `RiskAnalysisContext.BoundaryIdByEntityId` now built by grouping every (entityId, boundaryId) membership pair first and sorting, instead of `Dictionary.TryAdd` over `Boundaries` in list order; a new `BoundaryIdsByEntityId` (`IReadOnlyDictionary<string, IReadOnlyList<string>>`, ordinal-sorted) exposes ALL boundaries an entity is a member of | **Shared Infrastructure Attribution Hardening.** Root cause: a binary shared by three-or-more workloads is legitimately a member of all of their (deliberately un-merged, Phase 5B §8) boundaries at once — each boundary's own `ApplicationBoundaryEngine.BuildCandidate` independently claims the shared RUNS target as one of its members — but the old single-valued `TryAdd`-based map silently kept only whichever boundary happened to be enumerated first, and `RiskAggregator` only ever consulted a finding's `SourceEntityId` through that map. Flagged as a known limitation at the end of Phase 7B (the ERP fixture's shared `host.exe` across BatchA/BatchB/BatchC) and fixed as its own corrective task rather than folded into Phase 8 | 2026-08-24 |
| `RiskAggregator.ResolveAffectedBoundaryIds` (replacing the old single-valued `ResolveBoundaryId`) unions boundary membership across a finding's `SourceEntityId` AND every `RelatedEntityIds` entry, and attributes the finding to EVERY resolved boundary — never just one | A shared-infrastructure finding's `SourceEntityId` is the shared binary itself (rarely anyone's sole boundary member) while its `RelatedEntityIds` are the sharing workload anchors (each a member of exactly one boundary); unioning both sides is what makes attribution correct without inventing ownership from naming similarity. The same finding instance (never copied) is added to every affected boundary's `ApplicationRiskSummary.Findings`, while `ServerRiskSummary` still counts the logical finding exactly once, since it is built directly from the original deduplicated `Findings` list, unaffected by how many boundaries the finding fans out to | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 8A** — `ServerSleuth.Analysis/Migration/` sits directly after Risk Aggregation in the pipeline (Discovery→Correlation→Boundary→Expansion→Validation→Risk Analysis→Risk Aggregation→**Migration Assessment**), consuming only `RiskAnalysisResult`/`RiskAggregationResult`/`RiskAnalysisContext` | Migration Assessment must remain analysis-only per skill.md §1 — it needs the already-attributed per-boundary findings Phase 7B produced (including the corrected shared-infrastructure fan-out), not raw discovery data, so it sits as a pure downstream consumer with no new access surface | 2026-08-24 |
| `MigrationPolicy.Classify(RiskFinding)` is keyed PRIMARILY by `RuleId`, with `Severity` as a secondary refinement only for the two rules (RR2-MissingBinary, RR5-CertificateExpiry) whose severity genuinely varies per-finding — never a blind `RiskSeverity → MigrationStatus` table | skill.md (Phase 8A) §1/§6 explicitly forbid "blindly classify every Critical finding as Blocked"; an expired (Critical) certificate is remediable pre-migration, never a structural blocker — RR5 never escalates to Blocking regardless of severity, which only a RuleId-first policy can express | 2026-08-24 |
| `MigrationAssessmentEngine.Assess` takes `RiskAnalysisContext`/`RiskAnalysisResult`/`RiskAggregationResult` but deliberately NOT a raw `GraphValidationResult` | Every Error-severity `GraphValidator` finding already flows through as an RR12-GraphIntegrity `RiskFinding` (Phase 7A's `GraphIntegrityRule`), which the policy always classifies `Blocking` — consuming `GraphValidationResult` a second time here would be "inventing a separate scanner" for information Risk Analysis already surfaced, violating skill.md §19's own instruction to reuse the existing result | 2026-08-24 |
| `MigrationDependency` is a model distinct from `MigrationIssue`, with `RelatedRiskFindingId` nullable | skill.md §10: "a dependency may be valid and non-risky but still require migration verification" — a `MigrationIssue` always traces to a `RiskFinding`, but a `MigrationDependency` (e.g. a discovered `ExternalDependency` or shared binary) exists independently of whether any rule flagged it as risky | 2026-08-24 |
| No `MigrationReadinessScore`/0-100 value, and no automatic remediation capability, exists anywhere in `Migration/` | skill.md §2/§12/§15 explicitly forbid both in this phase — a numeric score belongs to a later phase (if ever), and this tool is strictly read-only/inspection-only per the repository's own non-negotiable constraints | 2026-08-24 |
| `MigrationPolicy`'s RR11-ConfigurationRisk mapping is severity-aware (High → Conditional, Low/Info → Informational), not the flat `Informational` it started as | Real bug found while writing `MigrationPolicyTests`: the real `ConfigurationRiskRule` assigns High severity to FileShare/NetworkStorage references ("an explicit path dependency," per its own doc comment) — a blanket-Informational mapping would have silently discarded a genuine migration-sensitive UNC/NFS/CIFS reference, contradicting skill.md §6/§22 | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 8B** — one `MigrationActionType` enum is shared by both `MigrationAction.ActionType` and `MigrationVerificationCheck.CheckType`, rather than two separately-defined enums | skill.md (Phase 8B) §5's own example action-type list already names `VerifyFile`/`VerifyConfiguration`/`VerifyService`/`VerifyScheduledTask`/`VerifyIISApplication`/`VerifyNativeDependency`, and §9-13 ask for the identical `Verify*` vocabulary on checks — a second, near-duplicate `MigrationCheckType` enum would only be able to drift out of sync with the first | 2026-08-24 |
| `MigrationActionPlanner` indexes `MigrationDependency` by `RelatedRiskFindingId` once (`Dictionary<string, List<MigrationDependency>>`) instead of filtering `ServerMigrationAssessment.Dependencies` per issue | Filtering per issue is O(Issues × Dependencies); at the §26 performance target (10,000 issues × 5,000 dependencies) that is 50,000,000 comparisons per run, while indexing once is O(Dependencies) with O(1) lookup per issue — verified to complete in ~88ms at that exact scale | 2026-08-24 |
| A `MigrationDependency` with no `RelatedRiskFindingId` (or whose Issue produced no action) still gets its own standalone PostMigration `MigrationVerificationCheck` from `MigrationVerificationPlanner`, tracked separately via `MigrationVerificationDiagnostics.OrphanDependencyChecksCreated` | skill.md §8: "A dependency may exist without a risk... [but] the migration plan must preserve it" — an Action is inherently remediation-oriented (something is wrong), while a Dependency's mere existence always warrants target-environment verification regardless of whether anything is wrong with it | 2026-08-24 |
| `ReviewGraphIntegrity` (RR12) is the one `MigrationActionType` that produces NO PreMigration/PostMigration `MigrationVerificationCheck` at all | skill.md §19's own framing: a dependency-graph structural-integrity error either is or isn't trustworthy — there is no meaningful "verify this again after migration" step, unlike every other action type, which describes a concrete pre-existing thing (a binary, a runtime, a certificate) that legitimately has both a before-state and an after-state to confirm | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 8C** — `AssessmentCoverage` is derived exclusively from an optional `AggregateDiscoveryResult` parameter and never reads `MigrationAssessmentSummary`/`MigrationPlan`, and `ServerMigrationSummary.OverallMigrationStatus` is copied verbatim from `MigrationAssessmentSummary.Server.OverallStatus` and never reads `AggregateDiscoveryResult` | skill.md (Phase 8C) §12 explicitly forbids coverage silently downgrading migration status ("Coverage must NOT automatically determine MigrationStatus"); keeping the two computations structurally unable to see each other's inputs is what makes that guarantee verifiable by inspection, not just by convention | 2026-08-24 |
| `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine` indexes `MigrationPlan.Actions`/`PreMigrationChecks`/`PostMigrationChecks` by affected boundary Id once, instead of filtering each flat list per `ApplicationMigrationSummary` | Filtering per application is O(Applications × Actions); at the §22 performance target (1,000 boundaries × 10,000 actions) that is 10,000,000 comparisons per list per run, while indexing once is O(Actions) with O(1) lookup per application — verified to complete in ~97ms at the full 10k-entity/1k-boundary/10k-issue/5k-dependency/10k-action/20k-check scale | 2026-08-24 |
| `CoverageWarning.AffectedPlatform` is derived from the scanner Id's own `windows-*`/`linux-*` naming convention rather than a new first-class Platform field threaded through `AggregateDiscoveryResult` | Every scanner in the codebase already follows this naming convention (confirmed by grepping every `public string Id =>` across `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`); adding a new field to a Core/Orchestration type used well outside Phase 8C's own scope was avoidable, and this consolidation phase's own mandate is to compose existing information, not extend upstream models | 2026-08-24 |
| `SharedInfrastructure`/`ServerLevelIssues` in `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` are `.Where(...)` filters over `MigrationAssessmentSummary.Server.Dependencies`/`.Issues`, not new records constructed by this phase | skill.md §3: "Use existing models where possible. Do not duplicate existing data unnecessarily" — a shared dependency or server-scoped issue is already a first-class, fully-evidenced record from Phase 8A; re-wrapping it in a Phase-8C-specific type would only create a second place the same data could drift out of sync | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 9A** — `JsonReportRenderer` serializes a dedicated `Json/Dto` type tree, never `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`/`MigrationIssue`/etc. directly | skill.md (Phase 9A) §6: "solve it at the reporting DTO/contract boundary rather than weakening discovery security" — a fixed, explicitly-declared DTO property list cannot expose a field the mapper didn't deliberately copy, giving a second, structural safety layer independent of every upstream phase staying disciplined | 2026-08-24 |
| `JsonReportRenderer` uses `JavaScriptEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)` instead of `System.Text.Json`'s default encoder | The default encoder escapes all non-ASCII as `\uXXXX` — valid, uncorrupted JSON, but unreadable for a report a human opens directly; `UnicodeRanges.All` keeps literal Unicode characters (e.g. Traditional Chinese) while the JSON writer still correctly escapes every structurally-required character regardless of encoder choice | 2026-08-24 |
| `JsonReportRenderer.Render` has no try/catch anywhere | skill.md §11: "do not swallow exceptions... do not silently return malformed JSON" — letting `System.Text.Json` exceptions propagate unmodified is simpler than, and strictly safer than, a catch-and-rethrow that could later be "improved" into an accidental swallow | 2026-08-24 |
| `ReportFormat` has exactly one member, `Json` — no `Html`/`Pdf` placeholder added ahead of Phase 9B | skill.md §3/§21: HTML/PDF are explicitly deferred; an unused enum member today would be exactly the kind of speculative, unnecessary abstraction the project's own CLAUDE.md guidance (and this phase's spec) warns against — add the member when the renderer that needs it actually exists | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 9B** — `HtmlReportRenderer` calls the same internal `ReportDtoMapper.ToDto()` that `JsonReportRenderer` already used, rather than a second HTML-specific mapping | skill.md (Phase 9B) §2: "Do not create a second reporting architecture... reuse the existing ReportDtoMapper/reporting DTO model" — one mapping means one place the secret-safety/raw-configuration-safety property list is maintained, for both renderers | 2026-08-24 |
| Collapsible sections use native `<details>`/`<summary>` instead of any JavaScript | skill.md §20: "prefer zero JavaScript... the report must remain fully readable without JavaScript" — native HTML elements satisfy this with a strictly stronger guarantee than a minimal inline script would: there is no JavaScript-dependent behavior distinction to maintain at all | 2026-08-24 |
| `HtmlReportRenderer` uses `System.Text.Encodings.Web.HtmlEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)` rather than `System.Net.WebUtility.HtmlEncode` | `WebUtility.HtmlEncode` numeric-entity-escapes Latin-1 Supplement characters (Ü, ï, ö, é) while leaving CJK text alone — inconsistent, and less readable than necessary for accented Western text; `HtmlEncoder` with `UnicodeRanges.All` keeps all non-ASCII literal while still always escaping the five HTML-structural characters regardless of range configuration, mirroring the JSON renderer's `JavaScriptEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)` choice for the identical underlying reason | 2026-08-24 |
| `CssClassName.From` is a pure PascalCase→kebab-case regex transform with no switch/mapping table over specific status values | skill.md §6-7: "CSS classes must be derived from the existing status value only... do not introduce new status semantics" — a mapping table would be a second, parallel place a status/severity/coverage value could be (mis)classified; a pure syntactic transform structurally cannot diverge from the value it's given | 2026-08-24 |
| `HtmlReportRenderer(DateTimeOffset? generatedAt = null)` — timestamp display is an opt-in constructor parameter, not a default-on feature with an escape hatch | skill.md §22: "if a generated document title contains a timestamp, make it explicitly opt-in and exclude it from the deterministic renderer tests" — making `null`/absent the default keeps the zero-argument, most-commonly-used construction path deterministic without requiring callers to remember to suppress a timestamp | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 9C** — `ReportFileNameValidator` uses an explicit, fixed character/pattern blacklist instead of `Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars()`/`Path.IsPathRooted` as the primary safety check | Both BCL helpers are platform-dependent (`GetInvalidFileNameChars()` returns only NUL on Linux) — relying on them alone would accept `<script>` unmodified on a Linux host; skill.md (Phase 9C) §18 explicitly requires the same behavior regardless of host OS | 2026-08-24 |
| The atomic-write temp file is created in the SAME directory as the final target, never a shared system temp directory | `File.Move`'s atomicity guarantee (OS replace-file on Windows, `rename(2)` on Linux) only holds for a same-filesystem rename; a temp file in a different directory/volume would require a copy-then-delete, which is never atomic and could leave a partially-written final file | 2026-08-24 |
| `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle` is the ONLY constructor of a `ReportBundle`, taking a single `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` argument and rendering both formats from it in one call | skill.md §9-10: "Both must originate from the SAME `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` instance... do not re-run analysis between formats" — making inconsistency structurally impossible (no API path could construct a bundle from two different reports) is a stronger guarantee than merely documenting the expectation | 2026-08-24 |
| `ReportManifestEntry.Sha256` is computed over the identical `byte[]` passed to `FileStream.Write`, not a separate read-back-and-hash pass | skill.md §12: "Verify hash(read(file)) == manifest hash" — computing the hash from the same buffer that gets written makes the equality hold by construction, and a unit test still independently re-reads each file and recomputes the hash to prove the on-disk bytes actually match | 2026-08-24 |
| `ReportOverwritePolicy.FailIfExists` is the default parameter value on every public `Export`/`ExportBundle` overload, not merely documented behavior | skill.md §8: "Do not silently overwrite an existing report" — a default-parameter value is enforced by the compiler for every call site that doesn't explicitly opt into `Overwrite`, rather than relying on every caller to remember to pass it | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 10A** — `ServerSleuth.Cli` is multi-targeted `net8.0-windows;net8.0` with a `SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS`-gated `#if` around the `AddServerSleuthWindows()` call, instead of `EnableWindowsTargeting`+a single `net8.0` TFM | A plain `net8.0` project's `ProjectReference` to the `net8.0-windows`-only `ServerSleuth.Windows` project fails NuGet's NU1201 compatibility check outright — discovered by attempting the `EnableWindowsTargeting` approach first and hitting the restore error; multi-targeting with a conditional `ItemGroup`/compilation symbol is the documented, standard fix for "a cross-platform app with one Windows-only optional component" | 2026-08-24 |
| No third-party CLI parsing library (e.g. System.CommandLine) was added — `ScanOptionsParser` is a hand-rolled `switch` over four options | skill.md (Phase 10A) §2: "do not introduce a large CLI framework unless skill.md explicitly requires one... if a third-party CLI package is genuinely necessary, stop and explain why before adding it" — the `scan` option surface (`--output`/`--format`/`--overwrite`/`--quiet`) is small and fixed enough that a parsing library would add a dependency for a job four `switch` arms already handle deterministically | 2026-08-24 |
| `CliExitCode` defines its own 6-value exit-code set (0/1/2/3/4/5), documented as this phase's own invention | A grep for "exit code" across skill.md/ARCHITECTURE.md/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md/PROGRESS.md returned zero matches — no existing spec exists to conform to, so the doc comment says so explicitly rather than implying an external spec was followed | 2026-08-24 |
| `CliExitCode.PartialDiscovery`'s trigger set (PartiallySupported/AccessDenied/Failed, never NotApplicable/NotInstalled) reuses Phase 8C's `AssessmentCoverage.Limited`/`Partial` classification rather than a new one | Two independent "how complete was this scan" classifications (one in Phase 8C, one in the CLI) could silently drift apart over time; reusing the same trigger set keeps them provably in sync by construction | 2026-08-24 |
| `ReportExportRunner` calls `IReportExporter.Export` per-artifact (not always `ExportBundle`) so `--format json`/`--format html` write only the requested file | `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle` always renders both formats internally (cheap, in-memory, preserves the bundle's own same-report guarantee) — but skill.md (Phase 10A) §11 requires `--format json` to produce only `report.json`, never also a `report.html` nobody asked for | 2026-08-24 |
| `ScanPipelineRunner`'s post-discovery `Analyze` method is a synchronous, straight-line sequence of calls (no `Task.Run`, no internal parallelism) | Every stage (Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk/Aggregation/Migration Assessment/Migration Plan/Consolidation) is pure, in-memory, CPU-bound computation with no I/O of its own — introducing async/parallelism here would add complexity without addressing the actual cost driver (large real-world entity counts), and would make "no reimplementation, no reordering" harder to verify by inspection | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 10A-H** — the investigation fixture is a COM-heavy, densely-cross-referenced synthetic entity set (`RealisticScaleFixtureBuilder`), not the flat/disconnected shape every prior phase's own 10k/35k synthetic performance fixtures used | Flat, disconnected fixtures (used deliberately in Phase 8C/9A/9B/9C to cheaply exercise Reporting/Consolidation) produce near-zero graph edges, which never exercises `DependencyGraph`'s O(edge-count) `AddEdge`/`EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo` cost at any meaningful scale — a realistic, edge-dense fixture was required to reproduce the real-machine hang at all | 2026-08-24 |
| A 60-second diagnostic-only observation timeout (via `Task.Run`+`Task.Wait`) gates each stage in the investigation, with the orphaned background task simply abandoned on timeout rather than aborted | skill.md (Phase 10A-H) §10: "this is NOT a production CLI timeout... terminate only the test/diagnostic process itself, never terminate discovered server processes" — none of the existing pipeline methods accept a `CancellationToken`, so a true mid-call abort isn't possible without modifying production signatures (out of scope); abandoning the task on a one-off diagnostic test process is safe and sufficient | 2026-08-24 |
| No optimization (indexed `DependencyGraph`, pre-indexed `BuildComAssociations`) was implemented in this phase, despite the root cause being fully identified | skill.md §18: "STOP after diagnosis... do not optimize algorithms" — this phase's explicit, repeated instruction; the fix is documented as a recommended target for a future phase instead | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 10A-I** — `DependencyGraph`'s new lookup indexes store `int` positions into `_edges`, not a second `DependencyEdge` reference | Storing indices lets a duplicate-edge merge update the one authoritative `_edges` slot with a single write, since both index dictionaries already point at that slot; storing edge references directly would require also locating and replacing the same logical edge inside the target-side index on every merge, with no equivalently cheap way to find it | 2026-08-24 |
| `BuildComAssociations`' optimization pre-filters candidate COMs per boundary rather than replacing the per-com, per-edge tier-checking logic itself | The exact Tier 1/Tier 2 rules and first-match-wins ordering are the specified, tested behavior to preserve byte-for-byte; only which (boundary, COM) pairs get evaluated at all needed to change, so the safest fix reduces the search space rather than rewriting the matching logic | 2026-08-24 |
| The `RiskRuleEngine.Merge` `KeyNotFoundException` surfaced by the real-machine re-run was documented, not fixed, in this phase | This phase's own strict-stop scope is DependencyGraph indexing + COM lookup optimization + performance validation only; the bug is in an unrelated Phase 7A component and was never reachable before this phase's fix let Analysis progress past Expansion for the first time on real-machine data | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 10A-J** — the fix falls back to `primary.SourceEntityId` inside `Merge`, rather than making `MissingDependencyRule` aggregate its per-import findings or discriminate their Id by import name | `MissingDependencyRule`'s own output shape is RiskRule semantics, which this phase's own scope explicitly forbids changing; every colliding member of a same-Id `"solo:"` group already shares one `SourceEntityId` by construction, so the fallback needs no new data and reuses the exact evidence-merge machinery already trusted for the anchor case | 2026-08-24 |
| A throwaway diagnostic console harness (scratchpad-only, never checked into the repository) was used to capture the full exception/stack trace, rather than adding a verbose/diagnostic flag to the CLI | The CLI's own error path deliberately prints only `ex.Message` (Phase 10A §17: "do not add excessive logging infrastructure") — changing that would be a CLI UX change, explicitly out of scope for this phase; a standalone harness referencing the real projects directly gets the full stack trace without touching any shipped code | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 10B** — `--verbose` was added; `--no-color`/color output was not | `--verbose` directly satisfies skill.md §5-6's requirement using only already-computed data; color is explicitly optional (§14: "If color is implemented...") and the existing plain-text output is already unambiguous, so adding it would only add untested-in-headless-CI complexity for no required behavior change | 2026-08-24 |
| `ScanProgressReporter.WriteDiscoveryComplete`/`WriteAnalysisComplete` gained a `TimeSpan elapsed` parameter rather than a separate always-on timing method | Keeping duration output gated behind the same `verbose` flag as the scanner breakdown (rather than a third orthogonal flag) means one existing test suite's byte-for-byte console assertions never needed to account for a new, always-present line | 2026-08-24 |
