| The real Linux acceptance run built/ran directly from the Windows-mounted path (`/mnt/f/...`) inside WSL, instead of re-copying the repository into WSL's native filesystem | Phase 6G's own WSL-native repo copy pre-dated the `ServerSleuth.Cli` project entirely (no `src/ServerSleuth.Cli` there) and is not a git repository, so it could not be `git pull`ed up to date; building directly from the existing Windows mount avoided a second full copy while still exercising a genuine Linux `dotnet build`/`dotnet test`/CLI run | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase 10C** — `ScanTarget` lives in `ServerSleuth.Core`, but `ITargetTransport` lives in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`, not alongside it | `ITargetTransport` composes `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` (Infrastructure types); Core must never depend on Infrastructure in this project's established one-directional layering, and duplicating those interfaces' shape into Core purely to keep one type co-located with another would be a second, redundant abstraction | 2026-08-24 |
| `ScanTarget.Local()`'s Id is the fixed literal `"local"`, not a hostname resolved via a system call | There is exactly one logical "the machine ServerSleuth is running on" per scan; a hostname could be ambiguous or change, and resolving one would be an unnecessary system call this phase has no actual need for — skill.md §2/§15 only require determinism, which a fixed literal satisfies trivially and unambiguously | 2026-08-24 |
| `DiscoveryContext.Target` was added as a new, non-`required` property on the EXISTING type rather than a new parallel "target execution context" type | skill.md §4 explicitly frames the new context as needed "if the current architecture needs it" — `DiscoveryContext` already is that context (Profile/CancellationToken/ScanRoots); reusing it avoids a redundant type and, being non-required, keeps every pre-existing call site across the whole test suite compiling unchanged | 2026-08-24 |
| `IPortInspector` was not added to `ITargetTransport` | Unlike `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`, `IPortInspector` has no cross-platform default registration in `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` — each platform's own composition (`AddServerSleuthWindows()`/`AddServerSleuthLinux()`) registers its own implementation directly; folding it into a cross-platform transport boundary would require restructuring that DI ordering, beyond this phase's own architecture-foundation-only scope | 2026-08-24 |
| `ITargetTransport` declares zero methods of its own — only three property getters | Its entire operation surface is intentionally already the pre-existing, structured `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` contracts; adding any method of its own (even a well-intentioned convenience wrapper) would be a second place a future implementer could accidentally introduce a raw-string `Execute`-shaped API, which skill.md §6 explicitly forbids | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase GUI-4** — `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` gained a direct `ServerSleuth.Analysis` reference so `ScanCompletionState` could carry the real `ScanPipelineResult`, rather than inventing a Results-specific DTO project | `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself has referenced `ServerSleuth.Analysis` since GUI-1; widening the ALREADY-shared-DTO project to the same, already-allowed dependency avoided a fourth GUI-family project purely to move one nullable property, while `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`' new facts keep the forbidden-assembly guarantee mechanically re-verified on `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts.dll` itself | 2026-08-27 |
| `ApplicationRowViewModel`/`ApplicationDetailViewModel` were written as new presentation types, but every list they expose (Issues/Actions/Checks/Dependencies/Findings) is the SAME `IReadOnlyList` instance from the underlying report — never copied | Phase 8C's `ApplicationMigrationSummary` already carries per-application Actions/Checks; Phase 7B's `ApplicationRiskSummary` already carries per-application Findings/TopRisks — re-filtering the server-wide lists down to one boundary a second time in the GUI would be exactly the "recompute what a phase already computed" pattern skill.md forbids throughout the project | 2026-08-27 |
| `ResultsDashboardViewModel` is constructed directly by `MainViewModel` (`new ResultsDashboardViewModel(state)`), not resolved through the DI container like the other page ViewModels | It is per-result state, not a long-lived singleton service — DI-registering it would either force a parameterless constructor (losing the "built once per completed scan" guarantee) or a factory delegate registration that adds indirection with no benefit over a direct, explicit `new` at the one call site that actually has the `ScanExecutionState` to construct it from | 2026-08-27 | 2026-08-24 |
| **Phase GUI-5** — `IGuiReportViewerService`/`GuiReportViewerService` live directly in `ServerSleuth.Gui`, not routed through `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` like `IGuiScanExecutor`/`IGuiReportExportService` | Reading an already-written UTF-8 text file the same completed scan produced, from a directory the user themselves chose, is a plain local file read (`System.IO.File`/`Path`, BCL) — not a Reporting/Infrastructure/Windows/Linux concern; routing it through `ExecutionHost` would add a hop without closing any real dependency gap, and `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`' existing "Gui references only Core+Analysis+Gui.Contracts+Gui.ExecutionHost" fact still holds unmodified | 2026-08-27 |
| `GuiScanExecutor`'s private `ExportReport` was refactored to call the new `GuiReportExportService.ExportReport` shared static method, rather than leaving its own inline implementation alongside a second one on the new service | skill.md GUI-5 §1's explicit "never a second export implementation" — the two call sites (end-of-scan auto-export, on-demand dashboard export) now share the literal same code path, verified unchanged by `GuiScanExecutorTests` still passing after the refactor | 2026-08-27 |
| `IGuiReportExportService.Export`/`IGuiReportViewerService.ReadReportFile` are OPTIONAL (default-`null`) constructor parameters on `ResultsDashboardViewModel`/`MainViewModel`, not required ones | GUI-4 left ~30 existing test call sites constructing `ResultsDashboardViewModel` from just a `ScanExecutionState`; making the two new services required would have forced updating every one of them for a capability most of those tests aren't exercising, against no real benefit — `CanExecute` already degrades Export/Open to disabled rather than throwing when a service is absent | 2026-08-27 |
| **Phase 11A** — `build-release.ps1` renames the published executable via a post-publish copy step, not `-p:AssemblyName=<name>` on the `dotnet publish` command line | A global `-p:` MSBuild property applies to the entire build graph, not just the entry-point project — overriding `AssemblyName` this way gave every `ProjectReference` in the graph (`Core`, `Analysis`, `Infrastructure`, `Windows`, `Gui.Contracts`, `Gui.ExecutionHost`) the identical assembly name simultaneously and broke NuGet restore ("Ambiguous project name 'ServerSleuth'") — found by actually running the script, not assumed | 2026-08-27 |
